[联] composure：镇定，更日常, serenity：宁静，更绑平和, poise：从容，更绑仪态, agitation 焦躁, restlessness 坐立不安, hysteria 歇斯底里, equanimity, composure：composure 多指外表不露乱；equanimity 更绑内心不被扰动的平衡 equanimity, indifference：equanimity 是主动稳态；indifference 是漠不关心
[记] equanimity 为正式书面词；口语常说 stay calm / keep your composure。with equanimity 修饰面对方式。本篇 equanimity 开启「静定—从容」链，与 restlessness 等「动」词成对照

**poise**
英/美 /pɔɪz/
n. 从容，镇定，仪态
[义] Graceful and confident control of your body, behavior, and feelings; a calm, self-assured manner. 举止优雅、自信，情绪与身体都控得住的从容感——grace and poise 优雅从容, maintain poise 保持风度, natural poise 天生的从容。比 composure 更绑仪态、风度与自信感；比 posture 更绑内在气质。不可数为主。
[例] ① The young diplomat handled the crisis with poise beyond her years. 年轻外交官以超乎年龄的从容处理危机。 ② Her poise on stage made even nervous viewers feel at ease. 她在台上的镇定风度让紧张的观众也安心。
[搭] grace and poise 优雅从容, maintain poise 保持风度, poise under pressure 压力下仍从容, natural poise 天生的从容, poised 从容的（形容词）
[联] composure：镇定，更绑不露乱, grace：优雅，更绑动作美感, self-assurance：自信，更绑内心把握, awkwardness 笨拙, agitation 焦躁, fluster 慌乱
[记] poised 形容词（a poised speaker 从容的演讲者）。poise 可褒义形容人；动词 poise（使平衡）为另一用法。与 confident 相关但 poise 更绑外在风度。本篇 poise 与 tranquility 并读为「静而有度」

**placidity**
英/美 /pləˈsɪdəti/
n. 平静，温和，波澜不惊
[义] The quality of being calm and peaceful, with little show of emotion or disturbance; a gentle, untroubled stillness. 温和、少波澜、几乎不受扰动的平静——placidity of expression 神情平和, surface placidity 表面的平静。比 tranquility 更文学、更绑「温和平淡」；比 composure 更偏天性或氛围而非应激自控。不可数为主。
[例] ① Behind his placidity, he was carefully observing every detail. 在平静外表下，他仔细观察每个细节。 ② The placidity of the village life appealed to the tired city worker. 乡村生活的平和吸引了疲惫的城里人。
[搭] placidity of expression 神情平和, surface placidity 表面的平静, placidity of the sea 海面平静, placid 平静的/温和的（形容词）
[联] tranquility：宁静，更常用, serenity：宁静，更绑深层, calmness：平静，更中性, restlessness 坐立不安, agitation 焦躁, turbulence 动荡
[记] 日常更常说 placid（a placid baby 好带的婴儿, placid lake 平静的湖）。placidity 名词偏书面。placid 可形容人「少情绪起伏」；未必褒义（placid indifference 冷淡的漠然）。本篇 placidity 与 nonchalance 对照：前者偏温和静，后者偏「不在乎」

**imperturbability**
英 /ˌɪmpətɜːbəˈbɪləti/ 美 /ˌɪmpɜːrtɜːrbəˈbɪləti/
n. 沉着，泰然自若，不易被扰动
[义] The quality of being unable to be upset or excited; calmness and steadiness that is not easily disturbed. 核心义：外界变故、挑衅或压力都难以打乱其平稳，始终泰然——legendary imperturbability 传奇般的沉着, with imperturbability 泰然地, imperturbable calm 岿然不动。比 sangfroid 更绑长期性格或天性；比 equanimity更绑「不被 perturb 」、难被动摇。不可数为主。
[例] ① His imperturbability during the market crash made him a trusted leader. 市场崩盘时他的泰然自若使他成为受信赖的领袖。 ② Nothing seemed to shake her imperturbability, not even the rudest comments. 似乎没有什么能动摇她的沉着，连最无礼的评论也不行。
[源] 拉丁语 im-（不）+ perturbare（扰乱）+ -ability（性质）→ 难以被外界扰动、始终保持平稳 → 今指面对变故或挑衅时仍泰然自若。
[族] imperturbable 沉着的, perturb 扰乱, perturbation 扰动
[搭] legendary imperturbability 传奇般的沉着, with imperturbability 泰然地, imperturbable calm 岿然不动, show imperturbability 显露沉着, imperturbable 沉着的（形容词）
[联] equanimity：泰然，更绑内心平衡, composure：镇定，更日常, serenity：宁静，更绑平和, agitation 焦躁, restlessness 坐立不安, volatility 情绪多变, imperturbability, indifference：imperturbability 是稳而不乱；indifference 是不在乎 imperturbability, sangfroid：sangfroid 偏危机一刻的冷；imperturbability 偏长期难扰
[记] 日常更常说 imperturbable（an imperturbable face 不动声色的脸）。imperturbability 名词偏书面。本篇 imperturbability 收束「凝神—不动」链，转向 touchy / sensitive 等「心敏」词

**sangfroid**
英 /sæŋˈfrwɑː/ 美 /sɑːŋˈfrwɑː/
n. 沉着，冷静，处变不惊
[义] Composure or coolness shown in danger or under pressure; calm self-control in a difficult situation. 核心义：危险、压力或突发状况下仍冷静自控、不露乱——remarkable sangfroid 惊人的沉着, with sangfroid 冷静地, maintain one's sangfroid 保持镇定。比 composure更书面、带法语借词色彩；比 imperturbability 更偏一时应激中的冷静而非天性难扰。不可数为主。
[例] ① The pilot answered the emergency call with extraordinary sangfroid. 飞行员以非凡的沉着回应紧急呼叫。 ② She handled the hostile interview with sangfroid that impressed viewers. 她以令观众印象深刻的冷静应对充满敌意的采访。
[源] 法语 sang（血）+ froid（冷）→ 「冷血的」、血脉不热 → 引申为危机中仍保持冷静、不露慌乱。
[族] 法语 froid（冷）；英语近义 composure 镇定, equanimity 泰然
[搭] with sangfroid 冷静地, remarkable sangfroid 惊人的沉着, maintain one's sangfroid 保持镇定, sangfroid under fire 压力下仍沉着, show sangfroid 显露冷静
[联] composure：镇定，更日常, equanimity：泰然，更绑内心平衡, poise：从容，更绑风度, panic 恐慌, agitation 焦躁, hysteria 歇斯底里, sangfroid, nonchalance：sangfroid 偏危机中的冷静；nonchalance 偏「不在乎、不着急」 sangfroid, indifference：sangfroid 是控住情绪；indifference 是不关心
[记] sangfroid 为不可数名词；with sangfroid 修饰面对方式。无常见形容词形式，常说 cool / composed。本篇 sangfroid 与 imperturbability 并读为「凝神」链，与 touchy / sensitive 成对照

**insouciance**
英/美 /ɪnˈsuːsiəns/
n. 无忧无虑，满不在乎，轻松自在
[义] A cheerful lack of concern or anxiety; a lighthearted, untroubled attitude toward problems or responsibilities. 对麻烦或责任显得轻松、不操心、不太当回事——with insouciance 无忧无虑地, an air of insouciance 一副满不在乎的样子, youthful insouciance 少年式的无忧。比 nonchalance更书面、更绑「无忧无虑、不沉重」；比 indifference 更可褒（轻盈）、少绑冷漠。不可数为主。
[例] ① She waved away the warning with insouciance that annoyed her parents. 她满不在乎地挥手打发警告，父母很恼火。 ② There was a charming insouciance in the way he treated formal rules. 他对待正式规则时有一种迷人的不拘。
[搭] with insouciance 无忧无虑地, an air of insouciance 满不在乎的样子, youthful insouciance 少年无忧, insouciance about 对……满不在乎, insouciant 无忧无虑的（形容词）
[联] nonchalance：若无其事，更日常, carefreeness：无忧无虑，更口语, lightheartedness：轻松，更绑心情, anxiety 焦虑, worry 担忧, solemnity 严肃
[记] insouciant 形容词（an insouciant smile 满不在乎的笑）。insouciance 可褒（youthful insouciance 少年无忧）或略贬（insouciance about deadlines 对截止日期不当回事）。与 nonchalance 并读：nonchalance 偏「不在乎的风度」，insouciance 偏「不沉重的轻松」。本篇 insouciance → sangfroid → imperturbability 为「凝神—不动」链

**nonchalance**
英/美 /ˌnɒnʃəˈlɑːns/
n. 满不在乎，若无其事，冷淡
[义] The appearance or quality of being calm and not worried, concerned, or eager; casual lack of interest or effort. 表面冷静、不着急、不太在乎；或显得对重要之事也满不在乎——with nonchalance 若无其事地, an air of nonchalance 一副满不在乎的样子, studied nonchalance 装出的冷淡。比 composure 更绑「不在乎、不投入」；比 indifference 更绑态度与风度。不可数为主。
[例] ① He accepted the award with nonchalance, as if it were nothing special. 他若无其事地领奖，仿佛这没什么大不了。 ② Her nonchalance about the deadline worried her teammates. 她对截止日期满不在乎，队友很担心。
[搭] with nonchalance 若无其事地, an air of nonchalance 满不在乎的样子, studied nonchalance 装出的冷淡, nonchalance about 对……满不在乎, nonchalant 若无其事的（形容词）
[联] indifference：漠不关心，更绑无兴趣, casualness：随意，更绑不正式, insouciance：无忧无虑，更书面, anxiety 焦虑, concern 关切, agitation 焦躁
[记] nonchalant 形容词（nonchalant shrug 满不在乎地耸肩）。nonchalance 可褒（cool under pressure 压力下仍从容）或贬（nonchalance about safety 对安全漠然）。with nonchalance 修饰动作方式。本篇以 nonchalance 收束：与 equanimity / composure 同属「稳」，但 nonchalance 含「不在乎」色彩，与 placidity 的温和静形成对照

**dispassionate**
英/美 /dɪsˈpæʃənət/
adj. 冷静的，客观的，不带个人感情的
[义] Not influenced by strong emotion; calm, fair, and based on reason rather than personal feeling. 不受强烈情绪左右，依理性与事实判断，公平冷静——dispassionate analysis 客观分析, dispassionate observer 冷静旁观者, remain dispassionate 保持冷静客观。比 objective 更绑「无 passion、不激动」；比 cold-hearted 更中性、可褒（专业）。只作形容词。
[例] ① The journalist tried to give a dispassionate account of the riot. 记者试图对骚乱作客观报道。 ② Judges are expected to remain dispassionate when hearing emotional testimony. 法官听取情绪激动的证词时也应保持冷静公正。
[搭] dispassionate analysis/view 客观分析/看法, dispassionate observer 冷静旁观者, remain dispassionate 保持客观, dispassionate tone 冷静的语气, dispassionately 冷静地（副词）
[联] objective：客观的，更泛, impartial：公正的，更绑无偏袒, detached：超然的，更绑抽离, passionate 热情的, biased 有偏见的, emotional 情绪化的
[记] dispassionate 常褒（专业、公正）；勿与 cold-hearted（缺人性）混淆。dispassionate about 对……保持冷静。本篇 dispassionate 收束「心之温度—客观」链，转向 intuition / foreboding 等「心知—预感」词

**stressed**
英/美 /strest/
adj. 有压力的，焦虑不安的
[义] 因任务多、时间紧、责任重而紧绷、易怒或失眠——强调外因压力下的状态。口语 I'm so stressed 极普遍；亦可指物理「受压的」（stressed metal，专业语境）。
[例] ① He's been stressed about the deadline all week. 整个星期他都在为截止日期焦虑。 ② Too much homework is making the students stressed and sleep-deprived. 作业太多让学生压力巨大、睡眠不足。
[搭] feel/get stressed 感到/变得有压力, stressed about/over 为……焦虑, stressed out 压力山大（口语）, stressed worker 压力过大的员工, highly stressed 压力很大的
[联] anxious：焦虑，未必因任务压力, overwhelmed：不堪重负, tense：紧张，可因压力或情境, relaxed 放松的, calm 平静的
[记] stressed about/over + 名词/动名词。stress（n./v.）为同源词；stressed out 语气更强。区分 stressed（形容词）与 stressful（令人有压力的）：a stressful job vs. I feel stressed

**tense**
英/美 /tens/
adj. 紧张的，绷紧的
n. 紧张，时态
[义] Unable to relax because of nervousness, stress, or danger; physically tight. 因压力、焦虑或危险而无法放松；身体绷紧——tense silence 紧张的沉默, feel tense 感到紧张。名词 tense 亦指语法「时态」。动词 tense 绷紧（肌肉）。
[例] ① The room grew tense when salaries were discussed. 谈到薪水时房间气氛紧张。 ② My shoulders are always tense after desk work. 伏案工作后肩膀总是绷紧。
[搭] tense situation/silence 紧张局势/沉默, feel tense 感到紧张, tension 紧张（名词）
[联] strained：紧张的, anxious：焦虑的, stressed：有压力的, relaxed：放松的, calm：平静的, mellow：惬意的
[记] tense 可形容气氛、肌肉、人。tense 语法时态与情绪义同形。本篇 tense → ambivalent 为「单一紧张—矛盾复合」过渡

**uptight**
英/美 /ʌpˈtaɪt/
adj. 紧张的，拘谨的，放不开的
[义] Anxious, annoyed, or unable to relax; overly tense about rules or image. 焦虑、烦躁或无法放松；过分拘谨——feel uptight 感到紧绷, uptight boss 拘谨上司。非正式；比 tense 更口语、更绑「放不开」。
[例] ① Don't get so uptight about one small mistake. 别为一个小错这么紧张。 ② She felt uptight before the presentation. 演讲前她很紧绷。
[搭] feel uptight 感到紧绷, uptight about sth. 对……紧张, loosen up 放松（反义口语）
[联] tense：紧张的, stressed：有压力的, rigid：僵硬的, relaxed：放松的, mellow：惬意的, easygoing：随和的
[记] uptight 略非正式；可说 wound up（同义口语）。uptight → tense 为「拘谨—张力」链

**restlessness**
英/美 /ˈrestləsnəs/
n. 坐立不安，焦躁，静不下来
[义] The inability to stay still, relax, or feel satisfied; a nervous, uneasy feeling that makes you want to move or change something. 身体或心里静不下来、难以放松或满足，总想动或想变——feel restlessness 感到焦躁, restlessness at night 夜间辗转, growing restlessness 日益不安。比 anxiety更绑身体层面的「坐不住」；比 agitation 更轻、更日常。不可数为主。
[例] ① A sense of restlessness kept him pacing the room before the interview. 面试前的坐立不安让他在屋里来回踱步。 ② There was growing restlessness among employees waiting for news about layoffs. 等待裁员消息的员工中焦躁日增。
[搭] feel restlessness 感到焦躁, restlessness at night 夜间辗转, growing restlessness 日益不安, restlessness in children 儿童的好动/不安, restless 焦躁的（形容词）
[联] unease：不安，更泛, agitation：焦躁，更重、更绑激动, fidgetiness：坐不住，更口语、更轻, calm 平静, tranquility 宁静, composure 镇定
[记] restless 形容词（restless night 睡不安稳的一夜, restless legs 不宁腿）。restlessness 可与 boredom 并现（bored restlessness 无聊坐不住）。本篇 restlessness 开启「动—激」链，与 equanimity 等静词成对照

**overwhelm**
英/美 /ˌəʊvəˈwelm/
v. 使应接不暇，使难以承受；淹没
n. 压倒性力量（罕）
[义] To affect someone very strongly with too much of something; to cover or bury completely. 核心义：① （情感、信息、任务）过多而让人扛不住——feel overwhelmed 感到不堪重负, overwhelmed with work 工作堆积；② 淹没、压倒（overwhelmed by the flood 被洪水淹没）。动词；形容词 overwhelmed 极常用。名词 overwhelm 罕用。
[例] ① She felt overwhelmed by the amount of homework due the same week. 同一周到期的大量作业让她应接不暇。 ② Good applications overwhelmed the small hiring team. 大量优秀申请让小型招聘团队难以招架。
[源] over-（越过、过度）+ whelm（覆盖、淹没，古英语 *hwelfan*）→ 原义「翻覆、淹没」→ 今指情感或任务量过大而难以承受。
[族] overwhelming 压倒性的, whelm 淹没（罕/诗）
[搭] feel/get overwhelmed 感到不堪重负, overwhelmed by/with 被……淹没/压垮, overwhelm sb. 使某人难以承受, overwhelming 压倒性的, overwhelmingly adv.
[联] swamp：使应接不暇，更口语, overcome：战胜，可褒（overcome difficulties）或中性, inundate：使应接不暇，更正式, cope 应付, manage 应对, overwhelm, overcome：overcome 常指「战胜困难」；overwhelm 指「被过多事物压垮」 overwhelmed, stressed：stressed 强调压力；overwhelmed 强调量太大、处理不过来
[记] overwhelmed by/with + 名词/动名词。overwhelming majority 绝大多数（另一义）。stressed 条同义词列 overwhelmed；本篇专收动词与「不堪重负」链

**overwhelmed**
英/美 /ˌəʊvəˈwelmd/
adj. 不堪重负的，应接不暇的
[义] 因任务、情感或信息过多而难以承受（feel overwhelmed 感到不堪重负, overwhelmed with work 工作压得人喘不过气）。口语极常见；动词（上条）。
[例] ① New parents often feel completely overwhelmed in the first weeks. 新手父母头几周常感到完全招架不住。 ② She was overwhelmed with gratitude when friends showed up to help. 朋友赶来帮忙时，她感激得不知所措。
[搭] feel overwhelmed 感到不堪重负, overwhelmed by/with 被……压垮, emotionally overwhelmed 情绪上难以承受, overwhelmed parent 疲于应付的家长
[联] stressed 有压力的, swamped 忙得不可开交（口语）, on top of things 游刃有余, relaxed 放松的
[记] 只作表语或补语（feel overwhelmed）。与 overwhelm、burnout 并读。

**mindset**
英/美 /ˈmaɪndset/
n. 心态，思维模式
[义] 看待问题、挑战与成长的习惯性态度或信念（growth mindset 成长型思维, fixed mindset 固定型思维, change your mindset 转变心态）。可数/不可数均可。
[例] ① A growth mindset helps you treat mistakes as learning. 成长型思维让你把错误当作学习。 ② Coaching focused on shifting her mindset before skills training. 辅导在技能培训前先调整她的心态。
[搭] growth mindset 成长型思维, fixed mindset 固定型思维, change one's mindset 转变心态, positive mindset 积极心态, entrepreneurial mindset 创业思维
[联] attitude 态度, outlook 看法
[记] 可数：a mindset / mindsets。心理与教育文本高频，宜与 self-esteem、self-doubt 并读。

**frenzy**
英/美 /ˈfrenzi/
n. 狂乱，疯狂，狂热状态
[义] A state of great excitement, activity, or anxiety that is often uncontrolled or wild. 极度兴奋、忙乱或焦虑，常接近失控——in a frenzy 狂乱中, feeding frenzy 疯狂抢食/抢购, work in a frenzy 疯狂赶工。比 agitation 更剧烈、更绑「狂」；比 hysteria 更可中性（shopping frenzy 购物狂潮）。可数/不可数均可；a frenzy 可数指「一阵狂乱」。
[例] ① Shoppers were in a frenzy on the first day of the sale. 大减价首日，购物者陷入疯狂。 ② She cleaned the house in a frenzy before the guests arrived. 客人到来前她狂乱地打扫屋子。
[搭] in a frenzy 狂乱中, feeding frenzy 疯狂抢购/抢食, frenzy of activity 一片忙乱, work in a frenzy 疯狂赶工, frenzied 狂乱的（形容词）
[联] fever：狂热，更绑高涨情绪（buying fever）, hysteria：歇斯底里，更绑集体或情绪崩溃, mania：狂热，更绑持痴迷, calm 平静, composure 镇定, placidity 平和
[记] frenzied 形容词（frenzied pace 狂乱的节奏）。in a frenzy of + 名词（a frenzy of excitement 一阵狂喜）。frenzy 可中性描热闹（media frenzy 媒体狂热）。本篇 frenzy 与 hysteria / mania 并读为「激」的升级

**hysteria**
英/美 /hɪˈsteriə/
n. 歇斯底里，情绪失控
[义] Extreme and uncontrolled emotion, especially fear, anger, or laughter; wild, irrational behavior in a group or individual. 极端且难以自控的情绪，尤指恐惧、愤怒或狂笑；个人或群体的非理性狂热——mass hysteria 群体性歇斯底里, burst into hysteria 情绪崩溃, hysteria over 对……的过度恐慌。比 frenzy 更绑情绪崩溃、非理性；比 mania 更突发、更绑失控感。不可数为主。
[例] ① Rumors of contamination caused hysteria in the town. 污染谣言在镇上引发群体性恐慌。 ② She was almost hysterical with relief when she found her lost child. 找到走失的孩子时，她几乎情绪失控地如释重负。
[搭] mass hysteria 群体性歇斯底里, burst into hysteria 情绪崩溃, hysteria over 对……的过度恐慌, hysterical 歇斯底里的/极其可笑的（形容词，依语境）
[联] panic：恐慌，更绑恐惧, frenzy：狂乱，更中性, hysterics：歇斯底里发作，更口语, composure 镇定, equanimity 泰然, calm 平静
[记] hysterical 可贬（hysterical reaction 过激反应）或口语褒（hysterical laughter 笑到停不下来）。mass/collective hysteria 为固定说法。临床 hysteria 历史用法已更新，日常多指「过激、失控」。与 mania 对照：hysteria 偏突发崩溃，mania 偏持狂热

**manic**
英/美 /ˈmænɪk/
adj. 狂躁的, 亢奋的；manic 发作
[义] ① Clinical: relating to mania, an abnormally elevated, expansive, or irritable mood (manic episode 躁狂发作). ② Informal: wildly energetic or frantic (manic pace 疯狂节奏, manic energy 亢奋精力). ① 临床：与躁狂相关的（manic episode 躁狂发作, manic symptoms 躁狂症状）。② 日常比喻：精力异常旺盛、忙乱（manic Monday 忙乱的周一）。与 bipolar 并提：bipolar disorder 含抑郁与 manic（或 hypomanic）相。只作形容词。表述临床义宜用中性医学语气。
[例] ① During a manic phase, he slept only three hours yet felt unstoppable. 躁狂期他只睡三小时却觉得精力无穷。 ② The office had a manic energy before the product launch. 产品发布前办公室有一种亢奋忙乱的气氛。
[搭] manic episode 躁狂发作, manic symptoms 躁狂症状, manic energy 亢奋精力, manic pace 疯狂节奏, manic depression 躁郁症（旧称, 现多称, bipolar）, hypomanic 轻躁狂的
[记] mania 名词；manic 与 bipolar 并读心境障碍。勿将日常 manic 与临床躁狂混为一谈

**mania**
英/美 /ˈmeɪniə/
n. 狂热，癖好；躁狂（医学）
[义] ① An extremely strong enthusiasm or desire for something. 对某事物的极度热衷或癖好——football mania 足球热, mania for collecting 收藏癖。② In psychiatry, a state of abnormally elevated mood and energy (often paired with depression as bipolar disorder). 医学上的躁狂：异常高涨的情绪与精力。比 frenzy 更可持；比 hysteria 更绑「高涨、痴迷」而非崩溃。可数/不可数均可；a mania 可数指「一种狂热」。
[例] ① The city was caught up in World Cup mania. 整座城市陷入世界杯狂热。 ② He has a mania for organizing every drawer in the house. 他有把家里每个抽屉都整理好的癖好。
[搭] mania for 对……的狂热, football/collecting mania 足球/收藏热, mania and depression 躁狂与抑郁（医学）, maniac 狂人/……迷（名词，依语境）, manic 狂热的/躁狂的（形容词）
[联] obsession：痴迷，更绑执念, craze：风潮，更绑一时流行, frenzy：狂乱，更突发, apathy 冷漠, indifference 漠然, depression 抑郁, bipolar, manic：bipolar 为障碍名；manic 为躁狂相或亢奋态
[记] -mania 作后缀（bibliomania 藏书狂）。manic 形容词（manic energy 精力过剩）；manic 临床义与日常「busy」需依语境区分。本篇 mania 收束「动—激」链，转向 dysphoria / anhedonia 临床情绪词

**bipolar**
英/美 /ˌbaɪˈpəʊlə(r)/
adj. 双相的；双相障碍的
[义] Relating to or affected by bipolar disorder, a mental health condition with episodes of depression and mania or hypomania. 与双相障碍相关的：情绪在抑郁 与躁狂/轻躁狂两极间波动（bipolar disorder 双相障碍, bipolar depression 双相抑郁, live with bipolar 患有双相）。亦泛指物理「双极的」（bipolar magnet 双极磁铁）——心理语境以外需据上下文。只作形容词。名词常说 bipolar disorder，不宜用 bipolar 单独指代人（宜用 person with bipolar disorder）。表述宜用去污名化、中性医学语气。
[例] ① With treatment and support, many people with bipolar disorder lead stable lives. 在治疗与支持下，许多双相障碍患者生活稳定。 ② The psychiatrist explained the difference between bipolar and unipolar depression. 精神科医生说明了双相抑郁与单相抑郁的区别。
[源] bi-（二）+ polar（极）→ 两极 → bipolar 本义「双极的」→ 精神病学 bipolar disorder（双相障碍）指抑郁与躁狂/轻躁狂两极波动。
[族] bipolarity 双极性, polar 极的, unipolar 单极的（抑郁障碍对比术语）
[搭] bipolar disorder 双相障碍, bipolar depression 双相抑郁, manic and depressive episodes 躁狂与抑郁发作, live with bipolar 患有双相, bipolar treatment 双相治疗, mood stabilizer 心境稳定剂
[记] 说 person with bipolar disorder，避免 the bipolar 作名词指人。bipolar 与 dissociated, volatile 并提心境—解离—不稳链

**bipolarity**
英 /ˌbaɪpəʊˈlærəti/ 美 /ˌbaɪpoʊˈlærəti/
n. 双极性, 两极对立
[义] The condition of having two poles or opposite extremes; in politics, a world order dominated by two superpowers; in psychology, related to bipolar disorder as a concept. 具有两极或对立极端之状态；政治学指两极格局；心理学语境可指双相相关概念（global bipolarity 全球两极格局, emotional bipolarity 情绪两极性〔描述语〕）。不可数。bi-（双）+ polarity。
[例] ① Cold-war texts analyse bipolarity between rival blocs. 冷战文本分析阵营间 bipolarity。 ② Therapists distinguish clinical disorder from metaphorical bipolarity. 治疗师区分临床障碍与比喻 bipolarity。
[搭] global bipolarity 全球两极格局, emotional bipolarity 情绪两极性（描述语）, bipolar disorder 双相障碍, bipolarize 两极化
[联] bipolarity, bipolar disorder（日常）：格局/概念, 诊断 bipolarity, bipolarize：名词, 动词, bipolarize, polarize（日常）：强调两极, 一般 polarize bipolarize, bipolarity：动词, 名词
[记] 政治与心理义依语境区分。与 bipolarize 并读

**bipolarize**
英 /baɪˈpəʊləraɪz/ 美 /baɪˈpoʊləraɪz/
v. 使两极化, 使分成对立阵营
[义] To divide into two sharply opposed factions or poles; to polarize. 分成尖锐对立两派或两极，即使两极化/使分成对立阵营（debates bipolarize voters 辩论两极化选民, bipolarize public opinion 使舆论对立）。动词。
[例] ① Social media can bipolarize debates overnight. 社交媒体可一夜 bipolarize 争论。 ② Leaders warned that scapegoating would bipolarize the nation. 领袖警告嫁祸会 bipolarize 国家。
[搭] debates bipolarize voters 辩论两极化选民, bipolarize public opinion 使舆论对立, polarize 使两极化, bipolarity 双极性
[记] 产出亦常用 polarize。与 becluster 并读

**volatile**
英/美 /ˈvɒlətaɪl/
adj. 易变的, 反复无常的；易挥发的
[义] ① Of emotions or a person: liable to change suddenly, especially becoming angry or upset. ② Of a substance: easily vaporized. ③ Of a market: subject to rapid change. ① 情绪或人：易怒、骤变、反复无常（volatile temper 暴躁脾气, volatile mood 情绪不稳）。② 化学：易挥发的。③ 市场：剧烈波动。与 steadfast 相对：volatile 为不稳、易爆，steadfast 为坚定不变。只作形容词。
[例] ① After little sleep, he became volatile and snapped at colleagues. 睡眠不足后他变得易怒，对同事发火。 ② The volatile stock market worried investors. 剧烈波动的股市令投资者担忧。
[源] 拉丁语 *volatilis*（会飞的, 易散的）← *volare*（飞）→ 易变、易爆发 → volatile。化学义「易挥发的」与情绪义「易爆、易变」同根。
[族] volatility 波动性, volatilize 使挥发, volley 齐射（不同源, 勿混）
[搭] volatile temper 暴躁脾气, volatile mood 情绪不稳, volatile situation 易失控局面, volatile market 波动市场, volatile substance 挥发性物质, emotionally volatile 情绪易变
[联] steadfast 坚定的 stable 稳定的
[记] volatility 名词（市场/情绪）。volatile 与 steadfast, vacillating 并提稳态—摇摆对照

**vacillating**
英/美 /ˈvæsəleɪtɪŋ/
adj. 摇摆的, 优柔寡断的
[义] Unable to decide firmly; changing opinions or actions repeatedly. 立场、意见或行动上反复摇摆、缺乏定力（vacillating leader 摇摆不定的领导, vacillating between options 在选项间犹豫, vacillating policy 反复无常的政策）。动词 vacillate。与 steadfast 成对对照（性格深解-立场决断 vacillating 条）。贬义；强调反复改变已表态立场。只作形容词。
[例] ① Voters lost trust in the vacillating government that changed course every month. 选民对每月改弦易辙的摇摆政府失去信任。 ② He was vacillating between staying and leaving until the deadline forced a choice. 在截止日期逼迫选择前，他在去留之间犹豫不决。
[搭] vacillating leader 摇摆不定的领导, vacillating between options 在选项间摇摆, vacillating policy 反复无常的政策, vacillating attitude 摇摆态度, vacillate on 在…上犹豫, politically vacillating 政治上摇摆
[联] steadfast 坚定的 resolute 坚决的, vacillating, steadfast：vacillating 摇摆；steadfast 坚定——本篇核心对照对
[记] vacillate 动词；vacillation 名词。vacillating 与 steadfast, volatile 并提情绪与品格的稳定度

**tired**
英 /ˈtaɪəd/ 美 /ˈtaɪərd/
adj. 疲倦的，累的
[义] 身体或精神需要休息——跑累了、熬晚了、工作太多都会 tired。可指一时（I'm tired, let's go home）或长期（tired of waiting 等烦了）。
[例] ① I'm too tired to cook tonight—let's order takeout. 今晚太累不想做饭——叫外卖吧。 ② She was tired of hearing the same excuses again and again. 她听腻了那些反复出现的借口。
[搭] feel/look tired 感到/看起来累, tired of 对……厌倦, tired from/out from 因……而累, dead tired 累坏了, tired eyes 疲惫的双眼
[联] exhausted：精疲力竭，程度更重, sleepy：想睡，偏生理, energetic 精力充沛的, refreshed 恢复精神的
[记] tired from/out from + 原因（工作、运动）；tired of + 名词/动名词表「厌倦」。作定语可修饰 eyes、voice 等（tired voice 疲惫的声音）

**weariness**
英/美 /ˈwɪərɪnəs/
n. 疲倦，厌倦
[义] The state of being very tired, especially after a long effort; also, a feeling of being bored or impatient with something. 身心疲惫，或因某事久而「倦了、烦了」——physical weariness 身体疲劳, weariness of war 对战争的厌倦。比 tired更名词化、略正式；比 exhaustion 更轻、可含「心累」。不可数为主。
[例] ① She could not hide her weariness after working three night shifts in a row. 连三个夜班后，她藏不住疲惫。 ② There was a growing weariness with the endless political debates. 人们对无休止的政治辩论越来越厌倦。
[搭] physical weariness 身体疲劳, weariness of/from 对……的厌倦/因……而疲, overcome weariness 克服疲惫, weary 疲倦的（形容词）, weary of 对……厌倦
[联] tiredness：疲倦，更口语, fatigue：疲劳，更正式、可医学, exhaustion：精疲力竭，程度更重, energy 精力, freshness 清新, awearied, aweary：-ed 形, 谓语/表语形 awearied, weary（日常）：文学/旧, 常用
[记] weary 形容词：weary of + 名词/动名词表「对……厌倦」；weary from + 原因表「因……而累」。与 tired 近：tired 更口语；weariness 偏书面名词。tired of 与 weary of 可互换

**awearied**
英/美 /əˈwɪərid/
adj. 疲倦的, 厌倦的（文学/旧）
[义] Tired or weary (archaic or literary form of wearied). 疲倦或厌倦的（awearied traveler 疲惫旅人〔文学〕, grow awearied of war 对战争生厌〔旧〕, awearied limbs 倦肢）。只作形容词。a- + wearied。与 aweary近义。表述宜用中性、文学语气。
[例] ① The ballad follows an awearied soldier homeward. 民谣跟随 awearied 士兵归乡。 ② Modern prose prefers weary or exhausted over awearied. 现代散文用 weary 或 exhausted 取代 awearied。
[搭] awearied traveler 疲惫旅人（文学）, grow awearied of war 对战争生厌（旧）, awearied limbs 倦肢, weary 疲倦的
[记] 诗歌与文学识读即可。产出用 weary, tired。与 aweary 并读

**aweary**
英/美 /əˈwɪəri/
adj. 疲倦的, 厌倦的（文学/旧）
v. 使疲倦（罕）
[义] Weary, tired (predicative or literary adjective); to make weary (rare verb). 疲倦的、厌倦的（表语或文学形容词）；使疲倦（罕动词）（I am aweary 我倦了〔文学〕, aweary of the road 对旅途生厌, aweary the spirit 使精神疲惫〔罕〕）。形容词/动词。a- + weary。与 assure 链后之 aw- 敬畏—疲倦并读。表述宜用中性、文学语气。
[例] Pilgrim cried, "I am aweary of the long road." 朝圣者叹：「长路使我 aweary。」 Contemporary editors usually replace aweary with weary. 当代编者通常以 weary 替换 aweary。
[搭] I am aweary 我倦了（文学）, aweary of the road 对旅途生厌, aweary the spirit 使精神疲惫（罕）, weary 疲倦的
[联] aweary, awearied：表语形, -ed 形 aweary, awed（日常）：疲倦, 敬畏
[记] 莎士比亚体与诗歌常。与 awedness 并读 aw- 链

**exhaustion**
英/美 /ɪɡˈzɔːstʃən/
n. 精疲力竭，耗尽
[义] The state of being extremely tired; the act of using something up completely. 体力或心力被用到极限——physical/mental exhaustion 身心俱疲, collapse from exhaustion 累垮。比 tired / weariness 程度 更重；比 burnout 更泛、未必绑「职业耗竭」。不可数为主。
[例] ① The rescue workers treated the survivors despite their own exhaustion. 救援人员不顾自身精疲力竭仍救治幸存者。 ② After the exam week, she slept for twelve hours straight out of pure exhaustion. 考试周结束后，她因极度疲惫一口气睡了十二小时。
[搭] physical exhaustion 体力耗尽, mental exhaustion 心力交瘁, collapse from exhaustion 累垮, exhausted 精疲力竭的（形容词）, exhaust 耗尽（动词）
[联] fatigue：疲劳，更正式, weariness：疲倦，更轻, tiredness：疲倦，更口语, energy 精力, vigor 活力
[记] exhausted 形容词比名词更口语。exhaust 动词另有「耗尽资源、排气」义。与 tired / exhausted 对照：exhausted 为形容词；本篇收名词 exhaustion

**burnout**
英 /ˈbɜːnaʊt/ 美 /ˈbɜːrnaʊt/
n. 倦怠（尤职业），耗竭
[义] Physical or mental collapse caused by overwork or stress; the state of having no energy or enthusiasm left for your job. 因长期高压工作而身心耗竭、对工作失去热情——teacher burnout 教师职业倦怠, suffer burnout 出现倦怠。比 exhaustion 更绑「慢性压力 + 职业/角色」；现代心理与 HR 高频词。不可数为主；a burnout 较少用。
[例] ① Many nurses experience burnout after years of long shifts and emotional stress. 许多护士在多年长班与情绪压力后出现职业倦怠。 ② He took a month off to recover from burnout. 他休了一个月以从倦怠中恢复。
[搭] job/career burnout 职业倦怠, suffer burnout 出现倦怠, burnout rate 倦怠率, burned out / burnt out 倦怠的（形容词）, prevent burnout 预防倦怠
[联] exhaustion：精疲力竭，更泛, fatigue：疲劳，更正式 overwork 过劳（名词/动词）, invigoration 振奋, renewal 更新
[记] burned out / burnt out 作表语（I'm burned out）。burnout 亦可指「烧尽、熄火」（引擎 burnout），依语境区分。与 stressed 近：stressed 可短期；burnout 为长期结果

**bored**
英/美 /bɔːd/
adj. 无聊的，厌烦的
[义] 因无事可做、重复或缺乏兴趣而提不起劲——想离开或找点事做。只作表语（feel bored），不说 × a bored book（无聊的书用 boring）。
[例] ① I was bored during the long meeting because nothing new was discussed. 冗长会议里没讨论新内容，我觉得很无聊。 ② She got bored with doing the same exercises every day. 她每天做同样的练习，腻了。
[搭] feel/get bored 感到/变得无聊, bored with 对……感到厌烦, bored of 对……腻了（英式口语）, bored stiff/silly 无聊透顶, bored to tears 无聊至极
[联] uninterested：没兴趣，语气更中性, restless：坐不住，未必因无聊, interested 感兴趣的, excited 兴奋的
[记] bored with/of + 名词/动名词。主语是人；boring 修饰事物（a boring class）。bored 与 boring 是感受/致因配对

**boredom**
英/美 /ˈbɔːdəm/
n. 无聊，厌倦
[义] The state of feeling bored; the condition of being dull and uninteresting. 因无事可做、重复或缺乏刺激而提不起劲——die of boredom 无聊透顶, relieve boredom 解闷。名词形式；日常感受用形容词 bored。不可数为主。
[例] ① The long delay at the airport was pure boredom. 机场长时间延误纯粹是无聊。 ② She read magazines to escape the boredom of the train ride. 她读杂志以打发火车旅途的无聊。
[搭] die of boredom 无聊透顶, relieve boredom 解闷, boredom at work 工作中的无聊, bored 无聊的, boring 令人无聊的
[联] tedium：单调乏味，更书面, ennui：倦怠，更文学、更绑「空虚」, apathy：漠然，更绑「无兴趣」, excitement 兴奋, interest 兴趣
[记] bored表「感到无聊的人」；boring 修饰事物。本篇 boredom → tedium → ennui 为「无聊—单调—倦怠」强度链；与 bored 对照阅读

**tedium**
英/美 /ˈtiːdiəm/
n. 单调，乏味，冗长
[义] The state of being boring and lasting too long; monotonous dullness. 因重复、冗长而令人厌烦——the tedium of daily chores 日常杂务的单调, relieve the tedium 打破乏味。比 boredom 更书面、更绑「单调冗长」；比 ennui 更具体、少「存在主义式空虚」。不可数为主。
[例] ① He tried to distract himself from the tedium of the long flight. 他试图分散注意力，摆脱长途飞行的单调。 ② The tedium of filling out forms made everyone impatient. 填表的冗长乏味让大家都 impatient。
[搭] the tedium of ……的单调, relieve tedium 打破乏味, sheer tedium 纯粹乏味, tedious 单调乏味的（形容词）
[联] boredom：无聊，更日常, monotony：单调，更绑「一成不变」, drudgery：苦差，更绑「劳累的重复劳动」, variety 多样, excitement 兴奋
[记] tedious 形容词更常用（a tedious meeting 冗长会议）。tedium 与 tedious 同族；与 bored 近义，bored 偏感受，tedium 偏「局面本身的乏味」

**ennui**
英 /ɒnˈwiː/ 美 /ɑːnˈwiː/
n. 倦怠，无聊（尤因生活缺乏意义或刺激）
[义] A feeling of listlessness and dissatisfaction arising from a lack of occupation or excitement; boredom with life itself. 核心义：不是一时没事做，而是对生活整体感到空虚、提不起劲——existential ennui 存在性倦怠, wealthy ennui 富贵闲愁。比 boredom 更文学、更绑「意义感缺失」；比 tedium 更重、更抽象。不可数为主。
[例] ① After years of the same routine, a quiet ennui settled over him. 多年一成不变的 routine 后，一种沉静的倦怠笼罩了他。 ② The novel captures the ennui of suburban life in the 1950s. 小说捕捉了五十年代郊区生活的空虚无聊。
[源] 借自法语 *ennui*（烦恼、无聊），与 annoy（烦扰）同族 → 今指因生活空虚、重复或缺乏意义而生的倦怠，带一点存在主义或上流社会闲愁色彩。
[族] annoy 烦扰, annoyance 烦恼, ennuye 使厌倦（罕）
[搭] a sense of ennui 倦怠感, ennui of modern life 现代生活的空虚, suffer from ennui 感到倦怠, enervating ennui 令人萎靡的倦怠
[联] boredom：无聊，更日常, listlessness：无精打采，更绑「没劲」, apathy：漠然，更绑「无兴趣」, enthusiasm 热情, zest 热忱, ennui, boredom：boredom 可因一时无事；ennui 多指对生活整体的倦怠与空虚ennui, annoy：annoy 表「烦」；ennui 表「倦、空」，同族义已分化
[记] 较正式、文学；口语日常说 bored / fed up 即可。与 bored 对照：bored 可短期；ennui 更长期、更 existential。blasé近义，偏「见多了、腻味了」

**listless**
英/美 /ˈlɪstləs/
adj. 无精打采的，倦怠的，提不起劲的
[义] Lacking energy and enthusiasm; showing little interest or spirit. 缺乏精力与兴致，提不起劲、漠然——feel listless 感到无精打采, listless afternoon 慵懒午后。比 lethargic 更偏「没精神、没兴趣」；比 languid 更绑倦怠而非优雅慵懒。
[例] ① After the flu, she felt listless for a week. 流感后她无精打采了一周。 ② The hot weather left everyone listless. 酷暑让大家都提不起劲。
[搭] feel listless 感到无精打采, listless gaze 无神目光, listlessness 倦怠（名词）
[联] lethargic：更绑身心沉重, languid：更绑慵懒优雅, apathetic：冷漠的，更绑「不在乎」, energetic：精力充沛的, ebullient：热情洋溢的
[记] listless 可形容人、眼神、一天。与 deflated 不同：deflated 偏「被戳破泄气」，listless 偏「没劲、空乏」

**languid**
英/美 /ˈlæŋɡwɪd/
adj. 慵懒的，无力的，悠闲的
[义] Displaying a pleasant lack of energy; slow, relaxed, and graceful rather than weak. 慵懒、徐缓、悠闲，常带优雅或迷人感——languid gesture 慵懒手势, languid summer day 慵懒夏日。比 listless 更可褒（悠闲）；比 lethargic 更轻、更 aesthetic。
[例] ① She gave a languid wave from the hammock. 她在吊床上慵懒地挥了挥手。 ② The languid heat slowed the whole town. 慵懒暑气让整个小镇慢了下来。
[搭] languid gesture/movement 慵懒手势/动作, languid summer 慵懒夏日, languor 慵懒（名词）
[联] leisurely：悠闲的, languorous：慵懒的（更书面）, sluggish：迟钝的，更贬, energetic：精力充沛的, brisk：轻快的
[记] languid 可褒可中性；勿与 language 混淆。languor 名词

**lethargic**
英/美 /ləˈθɑːdʒɪk/
adj. 倦怠的，昏睡的，无精打采的
[义] Affected by lethargy; sluggish, drowsy, and lacking energy. 身心沉重、昏沉、缺乏活力——feel lethargic 感到倦怠, lethargic patient 昏沉病人。比 sluggish 更医学/沉重；比 listless 更绑「昏沉、想睡」。形容词；名词 lethargy。
[例] ① The medication made him lethargic all morning. 药物让他整个上午昏沉无力。 ② Hot afternoons make me lethargic. 炎热午后我会倦怠。
[搭] feel lethargic 感到倦怠, lethargic patient 昏沉病人, lethargy 倦怠（名词）
[联] sluggish：迟钝倦怠的, drowsy：困倦的, listless：无精打采的, alert：警觉的, energetic：精力充沛的
[记] lethargic 偏身心状态；03-性格 lethargic 偏气质。lethargy 不可数名词

**sluggish**
英/美 /ˈslʌɡɪʃ/
adj. 迟钝的，倦怠的，缓慢的
[义] Moving or reacting slowly; lacking energy or alertness. 行动或反应迟缓，缺乏活力——sluggish morning 慵懒早晨, sluggish economy 低迷经济（比喻）。比 lethargic 更偏「慢、拖」；性格义
[例] ① I always feel sluggish before my first coffee. 第一杯咖啡前我总昏沉迟钝。 ② Sales were sluggish in the first quarter. 第一季度销售低迷。
[搭] sluggish morning/economy 慵懒早晨/低迷经济, feel sluggish 感到迟钝, slug 蛞蝓（词源联想）
[联] slow：慢的, lethargic：更昏沉, torpid：更书面、更僵, brisk：轻快的, alert：警觉的
[记] sluggish 可形容人、市场、河流。本篇写情绪/身体 sluggish；性格 写作风 sluggish

**torpid**
英/美 /ˈtɔːpɪd/
adj. 麻木迟钝的，僵滞的，冬眠般的
[义] Mentally or physically inactive; sluggish as if in hibernation. 身心僵滞、反应迟钝，如冬眠般麻木——torpid state 僵滞状态, torpid mind 迟钝头脑。比 sluggish 更书面、更「僵、冷」。
[例] ① The village seemed torpid in the winter cold. 寒冬里村庄仿佛沉寂麻木。 ② After the long flight, his mind felt torpid. 长途飞行后他头脑迟钝。
[搭] torpid state/mind 僵滞状态/迟钝头脑, torpor 麻木迟钝（名词）
[联] sluggish：更日常, lethargic：更绑昏沉, inert：惰性的，更物理, alert：警觉的, animated：生动的
[记] torpor 名词；torpid 较书面，日常多用 sluggish/lethargic

**blasé**
英/美 /ˈblɑːzeɪ/
adj. 厌倦的，见怪不怪的，冷漠的
[义] Unimpressed or indifferent because of too much experience or exposure. 因见多识广而厌倦、冷漠、不以为然——blasé about fame 对名声见怪不怪。只作形容词；发音近 blah-zay。
[例] ① Teenagers can seem blasé about things that excite adults. 青少年对让大人兴奋的事可能不以为然。 ② After years in fashion, she was blasé about runway shows. 在时尚圈多年后，她对走秀见怪不怪。
[搭] blasé about sth. 对……见怪不怪, a blasé attitude 冷漠态度
[联] indifferent：冷漠的, jaded：厌倦的，更绑「腻烦」, unimpressed：不以为然的, enthusiastic：热情的, wide-eyed：天真的
[记] blasé about + 名词/-ing。勿写作 blase（缺音符时仍读 /blɑːzeɪ/）

**mellow**
英/美 /ˈmeləʊ/
adj. 醇和的，温和的，惬意的
v. 变温和，变醇熟
[义] ① Of mood: relaxed, gentle, and pleasantly calm, especially with age or after drinking. （情绪）醇和、惬意、温和——mellow mood 惬意心情, mellow out 放松下来（口语）。② Of sound/taste: soft and rich. ② 声音/味道圆润。动词 mellow 变温和。
[例] ① The music put us in a mellow mood. 音乐让我们心情惬意。 ② He has mellowed with age. 他年纪越大越温和。
[搭] mellow mood 惬意心情, mellow out 放松（口语）, mellow voice 圆润嗓音
[联] relaxed：放松的, soothing：舒缓的, genial：和蔼的, tense：紧张的, uptight：紧绷的
[记] mellow out 口语「放松」。mellow 可褒（惬意）形容老人/酒/秋日下午

**hopeful**
英/美 /ˈhoʊpfəl/
adj. 抱有希望的，有希望的
[义] 相信好事可能发生、未来会变好——心里还亮着一盏灯（hopeful about the future, hopeful that we can win）。可形容人（a hopeful student）或事物「有指望」（a hopeful sign 好兆头）。
[例] ① She remained hopeful that her father would recover from the illness. 她仍抱有希望，相信父亲会康复。 ② There are hopeful signs that the economy is improving. 有令人鼓舞的迹象表明经济正在好转。
[搭] feel/be hopeful 感到有希望, hopeful about 对……抱有希望, hopeful that 希望…… hopeful sign 好兆头, hopeful outlook 乐观前景
[联] optimistic：乐观的，更绑整体态度, confident：有把握的，未必涉「愿望」, hopeless 绝望的, pessimistic 悲观的
[记] hopeful about + 名词/动名词；hopeful that + 从句。名词 hope；hopeful 作定语修饰 sign、look 等。勿与 hopefully（adv. 但愿）混淆：Hopefully it won't rain 但愿别下雨

**hopeless**
英/美 /ˈhoʊpləs/
adj. 绝望的；无可救药的
[义] 两种常见义：① 绝望——觉得没希望了（feel hopeless about the situation）；② 差劲到没救——口语夸张（a hopeless cook 厨艺没救, you're hopeless at math 你数学不行）。语气比 sad 更重、更绑「无望」。
[例] ① After failing the exam twice, he felt hopeless about ever passing. 两次考试失败后，他觉得永远过不了，绝望了。 ② I'm hopeless at remembering names—I always forget them. 我完全不行，记不住名字——老是忘。
[搭] feel/be hopeless 感到绝望, hopeless about 对……绝望, hopeless at 在……方面不行, hopeless case 没救的情况/人, hopeless romantic 无可救药的浪漫主义者
[联] desperate：绝望的，可更急迫, pessimistic：悲观的，偏态度而非一时感受, hopeful 有希望的, optimistic 乐观的
[记] hopeless about + 名词/动名词；hopeless at + 名词/动名词 表「在某方面极差」。名词 hopelessness。hopeless romantic 为固定搭配，指对爱情极理想化的人，未必贬义

**optimistic**
英 /ˌɒptɪˈmɪstɪk/ 美 /ˌɑːptɪˈmɪstɪk/
adj. 乐观的，乐观主义的
[义] 倾向于往好处想、相信事情会顺利——对未来或结果持积极预期（optimistic about the outcome, optimistic that we will succeed）。可形容人、计划、报告（an optimistic forecast 乐观预测）。
[例] ① Investors are optimistic about the company's growth this year. 投资者对公司今年增长乐观。 ② She stayed optimistic that they would find a solution before the deadline. 她仍乐观地相信能在截止日期前找到解决办法。
[搭] feel/be optimistic 感到/很乐观, optimistic about 对……乐观, optimistic that 乐观地认为…… optimistic outlook 乐观看法, cautiously optimistic 谨慎乐观
[联] hopeful：抱有希望，未必像 optimistic 那样系统性地往好处想, positive：积极的，范围更广, pessimistic 悲观的, gloomy 悲观的
[记] optimistic about + 名词/动名词；optimistic that + 从句。名词 optimism；反义 pessimism。optimistic 与 pessimistic 常成对出现

**pessimistic**
英/美 /ˌpesɪˈmɪstɪk/
adj. 悲观的，悲观主义的
[义] 倾向于往坏处想、预期事情会失败或变糟——与 optimistic 相对（pessimistic about the economy, pessimistic that things will improve）。可形容人、预测、态度。
[例] ① Many people are pessimistic about finding affordable housing in the city. 许多人对在该市找到负担得起的住房悲观。 ② Don't be so pessimistic—we still have time to fix this. 别那么悲观——我们还有时间补救。
[搭] feel/be pessimistic 感到/很悲观, pessimistic about 对……悲观, pessimistic that 悲观地认为…… pessimistic view 悲观看法, overly pessimistic 过于悲观
[联] gloomy：阴郁的，可形容气氛, negative：消极的，未必专指对未来的预期, optimistic 乐观的, hopeful 有希望的
[记] pessimistic about + 名词/动名词；pessimistic that + 从句。名词 pessimism；反义 optimism。说 someone is pessimistic 描述性格或一时判断均可

**confident**
英 /ˈkɒnfɪdənt/ 美 /ˈkɑːnfɪdənt/
adj. 自信的，有把握的
[义] 相信自己的能力或判断——不怯场、敢表达。可指性格（a confident person）或对某事有把握（confident that we will win）。
[例] ① She feels confident about passing the exam because she studied hard. 她复习充分，对通过考试很有信心。 ② He walked into the room with a confident smile. 他带着自信的微笑走进房间。
[搭] feel/be confident 感到/很自信, confident about/in 对……有信心, confident that 确信…… confident smile 自信的微笑, confident manner 从容的态度
[联] self-assured：自信沉着，略正式, sure：有把握，语气较口语, insecure 缺乏安全感的, shy 害羞的
[记] confident about/in + 名词/动名词；confident that + 从句。名词 confidence；反义 insecurity。confident 与 confidential（机密的）拼写近，勿混

**shy**
英/美 /ʃaɪ/
adj. 害羞的，腼腆的
[义] 在人前不自在、不敢说话或目光接触——不是 rude，而是需要热身。常作表语（feel shy）或定语（a shy child）。动词 shy away from 表「回避、退缩」。
[例] ① He was too shy to ask her to dance. 他太害羞，不敢邀她跳舞。 ② Don't be shy—help yourself to more food. 别客气——再多吃点。
[搭] feel/be shy 感到/很害羞, shy about 对……不好意思, shy away from 回避, painfully shy 非常腼腆, shy smile 羞怯的微笑
[联] timid：胆怯，含「怕」意, bashful：腼腆，多指儿童或一时脸红, outgoing 外向的, bold 大胆的
[记] shy about + 动名词/名词（shy about speaking English）。shy 作动词表「受惊躲开」（The horse shied）与「害羞」义不同。名词 shyness

**assurance**
英 /əˈʃʊərəns/ 美 /əˈʃʊrəns/
n. 把握，自信；保证，安慰
[义] ① A feeling of confidence in yourself, your abilities, or a situation; freedom from doubt. 对自身、能力或处境的把握与自信，疑虑消除——self-assurance 自信, with assurance 有把握地, give sb. assurance 让某人安心。② 保证、担保（life assurance 人寿保险；assurances that... 承诺……），依语境区分。本篇侧重① 情感上的笃定与 reassurance。不可数为主；an assurance 可数指「一项保证」。
[例] ① Her calm assurance during the crisis reassured the whole team. 危机中她沉着的把握让全队安心。 ② He needed assurance that he was still valued after the reorganization. 重组之后，他需要确认自己仍被重视。
[搭] self-assurance 自信, with assurance 有把握地, assurance of support 支持的保证, give assurance 使安心/给予保证, assured 确信的（形容词）
[联] confidence：信心，更日常, certainty：确定，更绑无怀疑, conviction：确信，更绑坚定信念, doubt 怀疑, uncertainty 不确定, insecurity 不安全感, assure, ensure（日常）：对人保证, 确保结果 assure, insure（日常）：使确信, 投保
[记] assurance 与 insurance 不同（assurance 英式常指人寿保险）。self-assurance 与 self-esteem（下章）相关。动词 assure（assure sb. that... 向某人保证）。本篇以 assurance 收束「评价—冒犯—怀疑—笃定」链：从 approval 经 criticism / insult 至 doubt / assurance

**assure**
英 /əˈʃʊə(r)/ 美 /əˈʃʊr/
v. 使确信, 向……保证, 担保
[义] To tell someone confidently that something is true or will happen, so that they feel less worried; to guarantee or make certain. 自信地告知某事为真或将发生以消除其顾虑；保证或使确定，即使确信、向……保证（assure someone that… 向某人保证……, assure quality 保证质量, assure safety 确保安全）。及物动词。与 ensure 并读：assure 偏对人说话、消除疑虑；ensure 偏使事件/结果确定。与 approve 并读：approve 偏态度赞同；assure 偏消除不确定、给予保证。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① The manager assured us the deadline would be met. 经理向我们保证会赶上截止日期。 ② I can assure you the data is accurate. 我可以向你保证数据准确。
[源] 古法语 assurer ← 拉丁语 ad- + securus（安全）→ 使某人消除顾虑、相信某事为真或会实现。
[族] assured 确信的, assurance 保证, reassure 使安心, ensure 确保
[搭] assure someone that 向某人保证, assure quality 保证质量, assure safety 确保安全, reassure 使安心
[记] assure + 人 + that 从句；assure + 抽象名词（quality, safety）。与 assured 并读

**assured**
英 /əˈʃʊəd/ 美 /əˈʃʊrd/
adj. 确信的, 有把握的, 自信的
[义] Confident and certain; feeling or showing that something is true or will happen; guaranteed or certain (assured income 有保障的收入). 自信而确定；感到或表现出某事为真或将发生；有保障的（assured manner 自信态度, assured voice 有把握的声音, assured success 稳操胜券的成功）。只作形容词。由 assure + -ed 构成。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① She answered in an assured tone that calmed the room. 她以自信语气作答，使全场镇定。 ② Investors want assured returns before they commit. 投资者在投入前想要有保障的回报。
[搭] assured manner 自信态度, assured voice 有把握的声音, assured success 稳操胜券, rest assured 请放心
[联] assured, assure：形容词, 动词 assured, confident：更绑「已获保证/消除疑虑」, 一般自信, assuredly, assured：副词, 形容词 assuredly, surely（日常）：更正式, 更口语
[记] rest assured 为固定礼貌表达。与 assuredly 并读

**assuredly**
英 /əˈʃʊərɪdli/ 美 /əˈʃʊrɪdli/
adv. 确实地, 无疑地, 自信地
[义] Without doubt; certainly; in a confident or assured manner. 无疑地、确实地；以自信或有把握的方式（will assuredly succeed 必将成功, assuredly true 千真万确, speak assuredly 自信地说话）。副词。由 assured + -ly 构成。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① The plan will assuredly face legal review. 该计划无疑将面临法律审查。 ② He nodded assuredly when asked if he was ready. 被问是否准备好时，他自信地点头。
[搭] will assuredly succeed 必将成功, assuredly true 千真万确, speak assuredly 自信地说话, certainly 当然
[记] 正式强调用 assuredly；口语多用 surely, definitely。与 assuredness 并读

**assuredness**
英 /əˈʃʊədnəs/ 美 /əˈʃʊrdnəs/
n. 确信, 自信, 把握
[义] The state or quality of being assured; confidence and certainty in oneself or in a situation. 确信的状态或性质；对己或对情境的自信与把握（quiet assuredness 沉静的自信, assuredness in public speaking 公开演讲中的把握, moral assuredness 道德上的确信）。不可数。与 assurance 近义：assuredness 更偏内在确信感/气质；assurance 亦可指口头保证。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① Critics praised the assuredness of her debut performance. 评论家称赞她首演之 assuredness。 ② He spoke with assuredness that came from years of practice. 他以多年练习得来的 assuredness 发言。
[源] assured + -ness → 确信无疑或表现自信的状态与气质。
[族] assured 确信的, assurance 保证, confidence 自信, certainty 确定性
[搭] quiet assuredness 沉静自信, assuredness in public speaking 演讲把握, moral assuredness 道德确信, confidence 自信
[联] assuredness, assurance（日常）：内在确信, 保证/自信（兼） assuredness, confidence：更书面, 更常用, assurer, insurer（日常）：保证者/英式保险, 标准保险人 assurer, assure：名词, 动词
[记] 产出亦可用 confidence, self-assurance。与 assurer 并读 assur- 链

**assurer**
英 /əˈʃʊərə(r)/ 美 /əˈʃʊrər/
n. 保证者, 担保人, 保险人
[义] A person or entity that assures, guarantees, or insures something. 作出保证、担保或保险的人或机构，即保证者、担保人、保险人（life assurer 人寿保险人, designated assurer 指定担保人, assurer of quality 质量保证方）。可数。assure + -er。与 insurer 并读：英式 assurer 偶指保险人；美式多用 insurer。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① The contract named the bank as assurer of payment. 合同指定银行为付款 assurer。 ② In some policies, the assurer must respond within thirty days. 部分保单要求 assurer 三十日内答复。
[搭] life assurer 人寿保险人, designated assurer 指定担保人, assurer of quality 质量保证方, guarantor 担保人
[记] 英式金融文献偶。产出用 guarantor, insurer。与 assuring 并读

**assuring**
英 /əˈʃʊərɪŋ/ 美 /əˈʃʊrɪŋ/
adj. 令人安心的, 使人确信的
[义] Giving confidence or reassurance; making someone feel that things are under control or will be all right. 给予信心或 reassurance 的；使人感到情况可控或会好转，即令人安心的、使人确信的（an assuring smile 令人安心的微笑, assuring words 使人确信的话, assuring presence 令人踏实的在场）。只作形容词。assure 之现在分词形容词化。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① The doctor's assuring tone eased the family. 医生 assuring 的语气使家属安心。 ② Her assuring nod told us the test was fine. 她 assuring 的点头告诉我们检查没问题。
[搭] an assuring smile 令人安心的微笑, assuring words 使人确信的话, assuring presence 令人踏实的在场, reassuring 令人安心的（近义）
[联] assuring, reassuring（日常）：使人确信, 更常用「令人安心」 assuring, assure：形容词, 动词, assuringly, reassuringly（日常）：罕, 更常用 assuringly, assuring：副词, 形容词
[记] reassuring 更口语常见；assuring 仍可用于正式描写。与 assuringly 并读

**assuringly**
英 /əˈʃʊərɪŋli/ 美 /əˈʃʊrɪŋli/
adv. 令人安心地, 使人确信地
[义] In a manner that gives confidence or reassurance. 以给予信心或 reassurance 的方式；令人安心地、使人确信地（smile assuringly 令人安心地微笑, speak assuringly使人确信地说话, pat assuringly on the shoulder 安心地拍肩）。副词。由 assuring + -ly 构成。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① The pilot smiled assuringly before takeoff. 起飞前飞行员 assuringly 微笑。 ② She touched his arm assuringly during the wait. 等待时她 assuringly 碰了碰他的手臂。
[搭] smile assuringly 令人安心地微笑, speak assuringly 使人确信地说话, pat assuringly on the shoulder 安心地拍肩, reassuringly 令人安心地
[记] 产出亦可用 reassuringly。与 astonish 并读 assur- 收束后之 astonish- 链

**doubt**
英/美 /daʊt/
n. 怀疑，疑虑
v. 怀疑，不信
[义] A feeling of uncertainty or lack of conviction about someone, something, or oneself; the state of not being sure whether to trust or believe. 对某人、某事或自身的不确定、不确信，信心动摇——self-doubt 自我怀疑, cast doubt on 使……受质疑, no doubt 无疑（短语义）。比 skepticism 更日常、更绑内心波动；比 distrust更泛、可非人际。不可数为主；doubts 复数表「各种疑虑」。
[例] ① Doubts about her own talent kept her from applying for the role. 对才华的疑虑让她不敢申请那个角色。 ② His late replies planted doubt in her mind about his commitment. 他回复迟延，在她心里种下对承诺的怀疑。
[搭] self-doubt 自我怀疑, doubt about 对……的疑虑, cast doubt on 使……受质疑, without a doubt 毫无疑问, doubt oneself 怀疑自己, doubtful 怀疑的（形容词）
[联] uncertainty：不确定，更泛, skepticism：怀疑，更偏审慎立场, misgiving：顾虑，更绑不祥预感, certainty 确定, confidence 信心, assurance 把握
[记] doubt about + 名词/-ing；doubt that/whether 从句。self-doubt 与 insecurity 常并用。认知 已收 doubt 完整条；本篇补情感链上的 doubt → assurance 对照

**skepticism**
英/美 /ˈskeptɪsɪzəm/
n. 怀疑态度，怀疑精神
[义] An attitude of doubting whether something is true, useful, or worth believing until there is sufficient evidence; questioning rather than accepting claims at face value. 核心义： 在证据充分之前，对说法、方案或承诺保持审慎、不轻信——healthy skepticism 健康的怀疑精神, skepticism about 对……的怀疑, greet with skepticism 持怀疑态度看待。比 cynicism 更理性、可褒义；比 doubt更系统化、可指立场。不可数为主。
[例] ① The study was published amid widespread skepticism from other researchers. 研究发表时，其他研究者普遍持怀疑态度。 ② A little skepticism can protect you from online scams. 适度怀疑能帮你避开网络诈骗。
[源] 希腊语 skeptesthai（审视、思考）→ skeptic（审视者）+ -ism → 指对主张持证据不足则不轻信的态度，未必含 cynicism 的「人性本恶」。
[族] skeptic 怀疑论者, skeptical 怀疑的, sceptical 怀疑的（英式）
[搭] healthy skepticism 健康的怀疑精神, skepticism about/toward 对……的怀疑, scientific skepticism 科学怀疑精神, greet with skepticism 持怀疑态度看待, skeptical 怀疑的（形容词）
[联] doubt：怀疑，更泛、更日常, questioning：质疑，更绑提问, disbelief：不信，更强, credulity 轻信, trust 信任, certainty 确定, skepticism, cynicism：skepticism 可褒（审慎）；cynicism 多贬（觉得一切虚伪） skepticism, suspicion：suspicion 偏「觉得有问题」；skepticism 偏「先要看证据」
[记] skepticism about/toward + 主张/计划。skeptical of/about + 对象。美式 skeptic / skepticism；英式 sceptic / scepticism。认知 doubt 条与 suspicion 已辨析；本篇 skepticism 偏可自觉采纳的审慎立场

**cynicism**
英/美 /ˈsɪnɪsɪzəm/
n. 愤世嫉俗，怀疑一切
[义] A belief that people are motivated purely by self-interest and that sincerity or idealism is naive; a general distrust of others' motives and of institutions. 核心义：认定人皆自私、真诚与理想皆可笑，对动机与制度普遍不信——political cynicism 政治冷感, greet sth. with cynicism 以冷嘲热讽看待, deep cynicism 深层的犬儒心态。比 skepticism 更情绪化、更绑「人心险恶」；比 distrust更绑世界观与犬儒态度。不可数为主。
[例] ① Years of broken promises fed public cynicism about politicians. 多年背信加剧公众对政客的冷感。 ② She listened to his apology with barely hidden cynicism. 她听着他的道歉，冷嘲几乎藏不住。
[源] 源自希腊哲学流派 Cynic（犬儒学派）→ 原指「像狗一样不拘礼法、质疑世俗」→ 今多指对动机与制度普遍不信任、觉得一切皆为利益。
[族] cynic 愤世嫉俗者, cynical 愤世嫉俗的, cynically adv.
[搭] political cynicism 政治犬儒, cynicism about 对……的冷嘲, greet with cynicism 以, cynicism 看待, deep/public cynicism 深层/公众, cynicism, cynical 愤世嫉俗的（形容词）
[联] skepticism：怀疑，更偏理性审慎, pessimism：悲观，更绑对结果的负面预期, world-weariness：厌世，更文学, idealism 理想主义, trust 信任, optimism 乐观, cynicism, skepticism：skepticism 偏「证据不够、先别信」；cynicism 偏「动机必自私、真诚必假」 cynicism, skeptical：cynical 常含贬义；skeptical 可中性
[记] cynical about + 人/制度/承诺。cynicism 可描述对人性整体的冷感；skepticism 更常针对具体主张。本篇 cynicism ↔ skepticism 为关键对照，与 distrust / suspicion 合读

**cynic**
英/美 /ˈsɪnɪk/
n. 愤世嫉俗者
[义] A person who believes people are motivated purely by self-interest and distrusts sincerity or idealism. 愤世嫉俗者（a cynic about politics 对政治冷嘲的人）。
[例] ① The cynic doubted every campaign promise. 那位愤世嫉俗者怀疑每条竞选承诺。 ② Don't be such a cynic—some people really want to help. 别这么愤世——有些人确实想帮忙。
[搭] a cynic about 对……愤世嫉俗的人, cynical 愤世嫉俗的, cynicism 愤世嫉俗

**cynical**
英/美 /ˈsɪnɪkl/
adj. 愤世嫉俗的
[义] Believing people are motivated purely by self-interest; distrustful. 愤世嫉俗的（a cynical view 愤世看法）。
[例] ① Voters grew cynical about promises. 选民对承诺变得愤世嫉俗。 ② A cynical smile crossed his face. 他脸上掠过冷笑。
[搭] cynical view 愤世看法, cynic 愤世嫉俗者, skeptical 怀疑的

[联] skeptical 怀疑的, distrustful 不信任的
**cynically**
英/美 /ˈsɪnɪkli/
adv. 愤世嫉俗地
[义] In a cynical manner; with distrust of others' motives. 愤世嫉俗地，冷嘲地。
[例] ① He cynically dismissed the apology as a PR move. 他冷嘲地把道歉斥为公关手段。 ② Voters reacted cynically to the new pledge. 选民对新承诺反应冷淡。
[搭] speak cynically 愤世嫉俗地说, view cynically 以冷嘲眼光看待, cynical 愤世嫉俗的

**insecurity**
英 /ˌɪnsɪˈkjʊərəti/ 美 /ˌɪnsɪˈkjʊrəti/
n. 不安全感，缺乏自信
[义] The state of feeling uncertain about yourself, your abilities, or your relationships; anxiety about not being good enough or not being accepted. 对自身能力、外貌或关系缺乏信心，怕被否定或抛弃——feelings of insecurity 不安全感, job insecurity 工作不安全感（另一义：岗位不稳）。比 anxiety更绑自我评价与关系；比 fear 更绑「我不够好」。可数/不可数均可。
[例] ① Her insecurity about her accent made her avoid speaking in meetings. 她对口音的不安全感让她在会议上不愿发言。 ② Childhood bullying left him with lasting insecurity about making friends. 童年霸凌让他在交友方面留下持久的不安全感。
[搭] feelings of insecurity 不安全感, insecurity about 对……的不自信, deep insecurity 深层不安全感, financial/job insecurity 经济/工作不安全感, insecure 缺乏安全感的
[联] self-doubt：自我怀疑，更具体, uncertainty：不确定，更泛, anxiety：焦虑，更绑担忧, confidence 自信, security 安全感, self-assurance 自信
[记] insecurity about + 外貌/能力/关系。job/financial insecurity 指「工作/经济不稳定」，依语境区分。性格 insecure 为形容词；本篇收名词情感义，与 distrust / vulnerability 合读

**surprise**
英/美 /səˈpraɪz/
n. 惊讶，意外
v. 使惊讶；出其不意
[义] An unexpected event, or the feeling caused by something you did not anticipate. 因未料到而发生的事或由此生出的感觉——可褒可中性（a pleasant surprise 意外之喜）或略负面（take sb. by surprise 出其不意）。比 shock 更轻、更泛；比 amazement 更日常。名词可数/不可数均可；动词 surprise sb. with sth. 给某人惊喜/惊吓。
[例] ① To my surprise, the quiet student volunteered to lead the project. 令我惊讶的是，那个安静的学生主动要牵头项目。 ② They surprised her with a birthday cake when she got home. 她到家时，他们用生日蛋糕给了个惊喜。
[搭] to one's surprise 令某人惊讶的是, surprise sb. 使某人吃惊, surprise attack/party 突袭/惊喜派对, catch/take sb. by surprise 出其不意, express surprise 表示惊讶, surprised 惊讶的（形容词）
[联] astonishment：惊愕，更 sudden、更重, shock：震惊，可含负面打击, amazement：惊奇，更日常书面, expectation 预料, indifference 冷漠
[记] surprised at/by + 名词/动名词；surprised that + 从句。surprising 修饰事物（surprising news）。amazement / astonishment 条已列 surprise 为近义；本篇从惊讶延伸至困惑、倦怠与释压

**amazement**
英/美 /əˈmeɪzmənt/
n. 惊奇，惊愕
[义] A feeling of great surprise or wonder. 因意外而大吃一惊——to my amazement 令我惊讶的是, stare in amazement 惊愕地盯着。比 wonder 更绑突然、程度更强；比 astonishment 可略轻或更日常。不可数为主。
[例] ① To our amazement, the shy student gave the best speech. 令我们惊讶的是，那个害羞的学生演讲最好。 ② She watched in amazement as the magician made the bird disappear. 她惊愕地看着魔术师让鸟消失。
[搭] in amazement 惊愕地, to one's amazement 令某人惊讶的是, amazement at 对……感到惊奇, amazed 惊奇的（形容词）, amaze 使惊奇（动词）
[联] astonishment：惊愕，更正式、更重, wonder：惊奇，可更温和, surprise：惊讶，更泛, indifference 冷漠, boredom 无聊
[记] amazed at/by + 名词/动名词；amazed that + 从句。to one's amazement 类似 to my surprise。形容词 amazed 比名词更口语

**astonishment**
英 /əˈstɒnɪʃmənt/ 美 /əˈstɑːnɪʃmənt/
n. 惊愕，震惊
[义] Great surprise or wonder; the state of being very surprised. 极度吃惊、几乎愣住——to everyone's astonishment 令所有人震惊的是。比 amazement 更正式、更重；与 surprise 近，但 astonishment 多绑难以置信。不可数为主。
[例] ① To my astonishment, he remembered every name on the list. 令我震惊的是，他记住了名单上的每个名字。 ② She gasped in astonishment when she saw the gift. 看到礼物时，她惊愕得倒吸一口气。
[搭] in astonishment 惊愕地, to one's astonishment 令某人震惊的是, astonishment at 对……感到震惊, astonished 震惊的（形容词）, astonish 使震惊（动词）
[联] amazement：惊奇，更日常, shock：震惊，可含负面打击, surprise：惊讶，更泛, indifference 冷漠, composure 镇定, astonish, astonishment：动词, 名词 astonish, surprise（日常）：更强烈, 一般惊讶
[记] astonished at/by/that 结构与 amazed 相同。astonishing 修饰事物（astonishing news 令人震惊的消息）。本篇从 傲慢羞耻链经谦逊、勇气至敬畏惊叹，与 shame / pride 及性格 brave / resilient 对照完成情绪与品格延伸

**astonish**
英 /əˈstɒnɪʃ/ 美 /əˈstɑːnɪʃ/
v. 使吃惊, 使惊愕
[义] To surprise someone greatly; to fill with sudden wonder or amazement. 使人大吃一惊；以 sudden 惊奇或 amazement 充满，即使吃惊、使惊愕（astonish the audience 使观众惊愕, astonish with skill 以技艺令人震惊, be astonished at 对……感到震惊）。及物动词。与 astonishment 并读：动词 vs 名词；比 surprise 程度 更强、更绑「难以置信」。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① The comeback astonished even longtime fans. 逆转甚至 astonished 老粉丝。 ② Her memory astonishes everyone who meets her. 她的记忆力 astonishes 每个遇见她的人。
[搭] astonish the audience 使观众惊愕, astonish with skill 以技艺震惊, be astonished at 对……震惊, astonishment 惊愕
[记] astonish 常接人作宾语；be astonished at/by 为常见被动结构。与 astonishing 并读

**astonishing**
英 /əˈstɒnɪʃɪŋ/ 美 /əˈstɑːnɪʃɪŋ/
adj. 令人惊愕的, 惊人的
[义] Extremely surprising or impressive; causing great wonder or amazement. 极其令人惊讶或印象深刻；引起强烈惊奇，即令人惊愕的、惊人的（an astonishing feat 惊人的壮举, astonishing speed 惊人的速度, astonishing news 令人震惊的消息）。只作形容词。与 astonishment 并读：形容词 vs 名词；比 surprising 更强调程度。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① The team made an astonishing recovery in the second half. 球队下半场 astonishing 逆转。 ② It is astonishing how fast the city changed. 城市变化之快 astonishing。
[源] astonish + -ing → 引起强烈惊奇或难以置信的。
[族] astonish 使惊愕, astonishment 惊愕, astonishingly 惊人地, amazing 令人惊奇的
[搭] an astonishing feat 惊人壮举, astonishing speed 惊人速度, astonishing news 令人震惊的消息, amazing 令人惊奇的
[联] astonishing, astonishment：形容词, 名词 astonishing, surprising（日常）：更强烈, 一般, astonishingly, astonishing：副词, 形容词 astonishingly, surprisingly（日常）：更强调难以置信, 一般惊讶
[记] It is astonishing that… 为常见句型。与 astonishingly 并读

**astonishingly**
英 /əˈstɒnɪʃɪŋli/ 美 /əˈstɑːnɪʃɪŋli/
adv. 惊人地, 令人惊愕地
[义] In an astonishing manner; to a surprising or remarkable degree. 以令人惊愕的方式；达到惊人或显著程度，即惊人地、令人惊愕地（astonishingly fast 快得惊人, astonishingly accurate 准确得令人震惊, perform astonishingly well 表现好得惊人）。副词。由 astonishing + -ly 构成。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① The budget came in astonishingly under target. 预算 astonishingly 低于目标。 ② She astonishingly recalled every date on the list. 她 astonishingly 记住了名单上每个日期。
[搭] astonishingly fast 快得惊人, astonishingly accurate 准确得令人震惊, perform astonishingly well 表现好得惊人, surprisingly 令人惊讶地
[记] 修饰形容词或动词均可。与 astonishingness 并读 astonish- 链

**astonishedly**
英 /əˈstɒnɪʃtli/ 美 /əˈstɑːnɪʃtli/
adv. 惊愕地, 吃惊地
[义] In an astonished manner; with great surprise or amazement. 以惊愕或吃惊的方式；惊愕地、吃惊地（stare astonishedly惊愕地凝视, gasp astonishedly吃惊地倒吸气, react astonishedly to news 对消息惊愕反应）。副词。由 astonished + -ly 构成。与 astonishingly 并读：astonishedly 描主体反应；astonishingly 描事物程度。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① She looked astonishedly at the unexpected gift. 她对意外礼物 astonishedly 注视。 ② They listened astonishedly as the verdict was read. 宣读裁决时他们 astonishedly 倾听。
[搭] stare astonishedly 惊愕凝视, gasp astonishedly 吃惊倒吸气, react astonishedly to news 对消息惊愕反应, astonishingly 惊人地
[联] astonishedly, astonishingly：主体惊愕, 事物惊人 astonishedly, surprisedly（日常）：更强烈, 一般惊讶
[记] 修饰主语之反应。与 astonisher 并读 astonish- 链

**wonder**
英/美 /ˈwʌndər/
n. 惊奇，奇迹；好奇心
v. 想知道，感到惊奇
[义] ① A feeling of surprise mingled with admiration, caused by something beautiful or unexpected. 惊奇、赞叹——childlike wonder 孩子般的好奇, sense of wonder 惊奇感；② 奇迹（the wonders of nature 自然奇观）；③ 动词 想知道（I wonder why…）。比 awe 更轻、更常绑好奇与欣赏；比 amazement 可更持久、更温和。不可数/可数均可。
[例] ① The children watched the fireworks with wonder. 孩子们惊奇地看着烟花。 ② It's no wonder you're tired—you slept only three hours. 难怪你累——你只睡了三小时。
[搭] with wonder 惊奇地, wonder at 对……感到惊奇, sense of wonder 惊奇感, no wonder 难怪, wonderful 精彩的（形容词）, I wonder if/whether 我想知道是否
[联] amazement：惊愕，更 sudden, awe：敬畏，更重, curiosity：好奇心，更绑想了解, boredom 无聊, indifference 冷漠
[记] wonder at + 名词；wonder if/whether/why + 从句。wonderful 与 wonder 名词常并用。no wonder (that) 难怪。与 wander（漫游）不同形，勿混拼

**awe**
英/美 /ɔː/
n. 敬畏，惊叹
v. 使敬畏，使惊叹
[义] A feeling of reverential respect mixed with fear or wonder; to inspire such a feeling in someone. 核心义：① 敬畏——面对神、自然伟力、权威时的「又敬又怕」（stand in awe of）；② 惊叹、震撼（in awe of her talent 对她的才华叹为观止）。动词 awe sb. 使某人敬畏。不可数为主；an awe 较少用。
[例] ① Tourists stood in awe before the ancient cathedral. 游客们在古老大教堂前肃然起敬。 ② The scale of the canyon filled them with awe. 峡谷的壮阔让他们充满敬畏与惊叹。
[源] 源自古英语 *ege*（恐惧、敬畏）→ 中古英语 awe → 今义兼惧与敬：面对宏大、神圣或非凡而生屏息、肃然之感。
[族] awesome 令人敬畏/极好的（口语义已弱化）, awful 糟糕的（古义「令人敬畏」，今义分化）
[搭] in awe of 对……敬畏/惊叹, awe and wonder 敬畏与惊奇, stand in awe 肃然起敬, awe-inspiring 令人敬畏的, awesome（口语常表「很棒」）
[联] wonder：惊奇，更轻、更好奇, reverence：崇敬，更绑尊敬, amazement：惊愕，更绑突然吃惊, indifference 冷漠, contempt 蔑视, awe, awful：awful 今义「糟糕」；awesome 口语「超棒」已与古义「令人敬畏」疏远 awe, wonder：wonder 可纯好奇、少惧；awe 常含渺小感或肃然, awesome, awful（日常）：口语「超棒」, 糟糕（古义曾近敬畏） awesome, awedness：形容词赞叹/敬畏, 敬畏状态名词
[记] in awe of + 名词/代词。awesome 口语「太棒了」与 awe 名词需依语境区分。本篇 wonder / amazement / astonishment 为惊叹链延伸

**awesome**
英/美 /ˈɔːsəm/
adj. 令人敬畏的, 极好的（口语）
[义] Inspiring awe or profound respect; in informal modern use, extremely good or impressive. 令人敬畏或肃然起敬；口语中常指极好、超棒（an awesome view 壮丽景色〔可含敬畏义〕, awesome job 干得太棒了〔口语〕, truly awesome power 真正令人敬畏之力）。只作形容词。与 awedness、awe 并读：awesome 偏引发敬畏或赞叹的性质；口语义与古义敬畏已分化。表述宜依语境区分正式敬畏与口语称赞。
[例] ① The canyon was awesome in the original sense—silent and vast. 峡谷按本义 awesome——寂静而辽阔。 ② Teens said the concert was awesome, meaning they loved it. 青少年说演唱会 awesome，意指超爱。
[源] 古英语 ege（敬畏）+ -some（引起……的）→ 原义令人敬畏、望而生畏；当代口语常弱化作极好、超棒。
[族] awe 敬畏, awed 敬畏的, awesomeness 极好/敬畏感, awful 糟糕的（古义曾近「令人敬畏」）
[搭] an awesome view 壮丽景色, awesome job 干得太棒了（口语）, truly awesome power 真正令人敬畏之力, awesomeness 极好/敬畏感
[记] 正式写作中「极好」宜用 excellent, impressive；敬畏义用 awe-inspiring。与 awesomely 并读

**awesomely**
英/美 /ˈɔːsəmli/
adv. 令人敬畏地, 极好地（口语）
[义] In an awesome manner; to a remarkable or awe-inspiring degree (often informal praise). 以令人敬畏或极好之方式；极好地、令人叹服地（perform awesomely 表现极好, awesomely beautiful 美到令人敬畏）。副词。由 awesome + -ly 构成。表述宜依语境区分口语称赞与敬畏。
[例] ① The team played awesomely in the final quarter. 球队在末节打得 awesomely。 ② Critics rarely use awesomely when awe-inspiringly fits sacred art. 神圣艺术评论少用 awesomely，多用 awe-inspiringly。
[搭] perform awesomely 表现极好, awesomely beautiful 美到令人敬畏, awesome 极好的/令人敬畏的, impressively 令人印象深刻地
[联] awesomely, awesomeness：副词, 名词 awesomely, awfully（日常）：极好地, 糟糕地, awesomeness, awedness：极好/敬畏性质, 敬畏状态 awesomeness, greatness（日常）：口语精彩, 伟大
[记] 口语称赞常见。与 awesomeness 并读

**awesomeness**
英/美 /ˈɔːsəmnəs/
n. 极好, 精彩（口语）, 令人敬畏
[义] The quality of being awesome; in informal use, excellence or coolness; in formal use, the quality of inspiring awe. 令人敬畏或极好之性质；口语指精彩、超棒（pure awesomeness 纯粹精彩, the awesomeness of the vista 景致的壮丽敬畏感）。不可数。由 awesome + -ness 构成。与 awedness 并读：awesomeness 口语多指棒；awedness 指敬畏状态（更书面）。表述宜依语境区分。
[例] ① Fans tweeted about the awesomeness of the finale. 粉丝发推赞决赛 awesomeness。 ② The sermon spoke of God's awesomeness, not slang praise. 讲道谈上帝 awesomeness，非俚语称赞。
[搭] pure awesomeness 纯粹精彩, the awesomeness of the vista 景致壮丽敬畏感, awesome 极好的, awedness 敬畏
[记] 正式敬畏义可用 majesty, awe。与 autoinhibited 并读 awe- 收束后之 be- 链

**awedness**
英/美 /ˈɔːdnəs/
n. 敬畏, 畏敬, 肃然起敬
[义] The state or quality of being awed; a feeling of reverential wonder mixed with fear or respect. 处于敬畏的状态或性质；敬惧与 respect 交织之畏敬感，即敬畏/肃然起敬（deep awedness 深切的敬畏, awedness before the altar 祭坛前的肃敬, sense of awedness敬畏之感）。不可数。与 awe 并读：awe 为情感本身；awedness 为处于该情感之状态（更书面/罕）。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① Tourists spoke of awedness inside the cathedral. 游客谈及大教堂内的 awedness。 ② The memoir captures awedness at first sight of the peaks. 回忆录捕捉初见群峰之 awedness。
[源] awed（敬畏的）+ -ness → 因崇高、威严或神秘而产生的敬惧与肃穆感。
[族] awe 敬畏, awed 敬畏的, awesome 令人敬畏的, reverence 尊敬
[搭] deep awedness 深切敬畏, awedness before the altar 祭坛前肃敬, sense of awedness 敬畏之感, reverence 尊敬
[联] awedness, awe（日常）：状态名词, 情感/动词 awedness, awesomeness（日常）：敬畏, 很棒（口语）
[记] 产出亦可用 awe, reverence。与 athrill 并读 aw- 收束后之 athrill 链

**confusion**
英/美 /kənˈfjuːʒn/
n. 困惑，混乱；混淆
[义] ① A state of not being certain about what is happening or what to do. 搞不清状况、脑子一团乱——instructions caused confusion 说明造成混乱；② 混淆（confusion between A and B A 与 B 的混淆）。比 bewilderment 更日常、更泛；比 perplexity 少「苦思冥想」色彩。不可数为主；a confusion 较少用。
[例] ① There was some confusion about the meeting time—half the team showed up an hour early. 会议时间有些混乱——一半团队早到了一小时。 ② The new rules confused many customers at first. 新规定起初让许多顾客困惑。
[搭] cause confusion 造成混乱, in confusion 混乱中/困惑地, confusion about/over 对……的困惑, confused 困惑的（形容词）, confusing 令人困惑的
[联] bewilderment：茫然，更书面, perplexity：困惑，更绑「想不通」, disorder：无序，偏局面而非内心, clarity 清晰, order 秩序
[记] confused about/over + 名词/动名词；confusing for sb. 对某人来说难懂。confusion between A and B 表「把 A 和 B 搞混」。confuse with 表「误以为是」（Don't confuse me with my brother）

**perplexity**
英/美 /pəˈpleksəti/
n. 困惑，费解
[义] The state of being confused or puzzled, especially because something is difficult to understand. 因某事难懂或矛盾而苦思不得其解——perplexity over the results 对结果的困惑。比 confusion 更正式、更绑「想不通」；比 bewilderment 少「完全迷失」感。不可数为主。
[例] ① The scientists expressed perplexity over why the experiment failed twice. 科学家对实验为何两次失败表示费解。 ② She listened with perplexity as he explained a rule that seemed to contradict itself. 听他解释一条似乎自相矛盾的规则，她困惑地听着。
[搭] in perplexity 困惑地, perplexity about/over 对……的困惑, look of perplexity 困惑的神情, perplexed 困惑的（形容词）, perplexing 令人费解的
[联] confusion：困惑，更日常, bewilderment：茫然，更绑迷失感, puzzlement：困惑，更口语, understanding 理解, certainty 确定
[记] perplexed by/at + 名词/从句。perplexing question 令人费解的问题。与 complex（复杂）同根 *plex-*「交织」→ 思绪打结

**curiosity**
英 /ˌkjʊəriˈɒsəti/ 美 /ˌkjʊriˈɑːsəti/
n. 好奇心；奇物
[义] ① A strong desire to know or learn something. 想知道、想搞明白——intellectual curiosity 求知欲, out of curiosity 出于好奇；② 奇物、古董（a curiosity shop 古玩店，较旧用法）。比 fascination 更绑「想了解」；比 interest 更主动、更强。不可数/可数均可。
[例] ① Curiosity led her to open the letter even though it wasn't addressed to her. 好奇心驱使她打开了那封并非写给自己的信。 ② The child asked questions with endless curiosity. 孩子带着无穷的好奇心提问。
[搭] out of curiosity 出于好奇, satisfy curiosity 满足好奇心, curiosity about 对……的好奇, curious 好奇的（形容词）, curiosity killed the cat 好奇害死猫（谚语）
[联] interest：兴趣，语气更轻, inquisitiveness：好问，略正式, fascination：着迷，更绑「被吸引」, indifference 冷漠, apathy 漠然
[记] curious about + 名词/动名词；curious to do sth. 很想做某事。形容词 curious 比名词更口语。本篇 curiosity → fascination 为「好奇—着迷」链

**fascination**
英/美 /ˌfæsɪˈneɪʃn/
n. 着迷，魅力；魔力
[义] The state of being very interested in something, or the quality that makes something very interesting. 被深深吸引、移不开眼——fascination with space 对太空的着迷；hold a fascination for sb. 对某人有魅力。比 curiosity 更绑「被迷住、难以自拔」；可含一点神秘或危险色彩。不可数为主。
[例] ① He developed a fascination with old maps and spent hours studying them. 他对古地图着迷，一研究就是几小时。 ② The documentary held a fascination for viewers of all ages. 这部纪录片对各年龄段观众都有吸引力。
[搭] fascination with/for 对……的着迷, hold a fascination 具有魅力, in fascination 着迷地, fascinated 着迷的（形容词）, fascinating 迷人的
[联] curiosity：好奇心，更绑「想了解」, captivation：迷住，更绑「俘获注意力」, obsession：执念，更绑「过度沉迷」, boredom 无聊, indifference 冷漠, captivated, enthralled：enthralled 更强、更「摄住」 captivated, mesmerized：mesmerized 更催眠、凝视感
[记] fascinated by/with + 名词/动名词。fascinating 修饰事物（a fascinating story）。fascinate 动词；fascination 与 fascism 不同形，勿混拼

**captivated**
英/美 /ˈkæptɪveɪtɪd/
adj. 被迷住的, 被吸引的
[义] Strongly attracted and held by charm or interest; fascinated. 被魅力或趣味强烈吸引而专注（captivated audience 入迷的观众, captivated by her story 被她的故事吸引）。动词 captivate。比 obsessed 更正面、常短暂；比 enthralled 略宽（可指景色、演讲）。只作形容词（表语/定语）。
[例] ① The children sat captivated during the magic show. 魔术表演时孩子们看得入迷。 ② We were captivated by the old city’s architecture. 我们为古城建筑深深吸引。
[搭] captivated audience 入迷的观众, captivated by 被…迷住, instantly captivated 立刻被吸引, hold sb. captivated 使人着迷, captivated expression 着迷的神情, remain captivated 仍着迷
[记] captivate 及物动词。captivated 与 enthralled, spellbound 并提正面着迷链

**captivate**
英/美 /ˈkæptɪveɪt/
v. 迷住, 使倾倒
[义] To attract and hold the interest and admiration of; to charm deeply. 以魅力吸引并牢牢抓住兴趣与赞赏，即迷住/使倾倒（captivate an audience 迷住观众, captivate with wit 以机智倾倒众人）。动词。与 love 并读：爱慕可 captivate 心神；与 captive 并读：俘获义延伸为着迷。
[例] ① The dancer captivated the hall from the first bar. 舞者第一小节起迷住全场。 ② Her stories captivate children without loud effects. 她的故事不用喧闹特效也能迷住孩子。
[源] 拉丁语 captivus（俘虏）+ -ate → 以魅力使人如同被俘获般着迷。
[族] captivating 迷人的, captivation 魅力, captive 俘虏, capture 俘获
[搭] captivate an audience 迷住观众, captivate with wit 以机智倾倒众人, captivating 迷人的, captive 俘虏的
[联] captivate, capture（日常）：魅力吸引, 俘获抓获 captivate, fascinate：倾倒着迷, 强烈吸引, captivating, captivatingly：形容词, 副词 captivating, charming（日常）：强烈倾倒, 和蔼迷人
[记] 与 captivating 并读

**captivating**
英/美 /ˈkæptɪveɪtɪŋ/
adj. 迷人的, 令人倾倒的
[义] Attracting and holding interest strongly; charming. 强烈吸引并留住兴趣，即迷人的/令人倾倒的（captivating smile 迷人微笑, captivating performance 令人倾倒的演出）。只作形容词。
[例] ① The documentary offers a captivating portrait of reef life. 纪录片呈现迷人的珊瑚礁生命肖像。 ② Critics called the debut captivating yet uneven. 评论家称首秀迷人但不均衡。
[搭] captivating smile 迷人微笑, captivating performance 令人倾倒的演出, captivate 迷住, captivatingly 迷人地
[记] 与 captivatingly 并读

**captivatingly**
英/美 /ˈkæptɪveɪtɪŋli/
adv. 迷人地, 令人倾倒地
[义] In a captivating manner. 以迷人或令人倾倒之方式，即迷人地/令人倾倒地（dress captivatingly迷人着装, speak captivatingly令人倾倒地演讲）。
[例] ① She performed captivatingly in a role with few lines. 她在台词不多的角色中表演迷人。 ② The gallery lit the bronzes captivatingly at dusk. 画廊黄昏迷人打光青铜作品。
[搭] dress captivatingly 迷人着装, speak captivatingly 令人倾倒地演讲, captivating 迷人的, captivation 魅力
[联] captivatingly, captivately：较常用, 罕 captivatingly, charmingly：倾倒, 和蔼, captivation, fascination（日常）：魅力过程, 强烈兴趣 captivation, captivate：名词, 动词
[记] 与 captivation 并读

**captivation**
英/美 /ˌkæptɪˈveɪʃən/
n. 魅力, 着迷
[义] The action or effect of captivating; fascination and charm. 迷住他人之作用或效果，即魅力/着迷（hold captivation over fans 对粉丝具魅力, sheer captivation 纯粹着迷之力）。不可数。
[例] ① The singer's captivation lay in quiet phrasing, not volume. 歌手魅力 在于轻声句读，而非音量。 ② Scholars study rhetorical captivation in political speech. 学者研究政治演说中的修辞着迷效应。
[搭] hold captivation over fans 对粉丝具魅力, sheer captivation 纯粹着迷之力, captivate 迷住, fascination 着迷
[记] 与 captivative 并读

**captivator**
英/美 /ˈkæptɪveɪtə/
n. 使人着迷者
[义] One who or that which captivates (rare agent noun). 使人着迷之人或物，即使人着迷者（a natural captivator 天生迷人者〔罕〕）。可数。
[例] ① The memoir calls her mother a gentle captivator at parties. 回忆录称母亲在聚会上是温和的迷人者（罕）。 ② Media more often says charmer or star. 媒体更常说魅力人物或明星。
[搭] a natural captivator 天生迷人者（罕）, captivate 迷住, captivatrix 女迷人者, charmer 迷人者
[联] captivator, captivatrix：男性/通用, 女性罕 captivator, captive：施动, 被俘, captive, captivate：被俘, 迷住 captive, captivity：人/状态, 囚禁状况
[记] 与 captivatrix 并读

**captive**
英/美 /ˈkæptɪv/
adj. 被俘虏的, 受控制的
n. 俘虏, 囚徒
[义] Imprisoned or confined; unable to escape; a person held prisoner. 被囚禁或无法逃脱，即被俘虏的/受控制的；名词俘虏/囚徒（captive audience 被迫听讲的受控听众, take captives 俘获俘虏）。形容词/名词。与 captivate 并读：由俘获义引申为着迷。
[例] ① The talk felt aimed at a captive audience in the hall. 演讲仿佛针对厅内无法离开的听众。 ② Negotiators worked to free the captives safely. 谈判人员力争安全释放俘虏。
[搭] captive audience 受控听众, take captives 俘获俘虏, captivity 囚禁, captivate 迷住
[记] 商业用语 captive market 指受控市场。与 captivity 并读

**captivity**
英/美 /kæpˈtɪvəti/
n. 囚禁, 被俘状态
[义] The state of being imprisoned or confined; bondage. 被囚禁或拘禁之状态，即囚禁/被俘状态（years in captivity 多年囚禁, animals in captivity 圈养动物）。不可数。
[例] ① The journalist documented life in captivity during the war. 记者记录战时囚禁生活。 ② Zoos face debate over breeding species in captivity. 动物园面临圈养繁殖物种之争。
[搭] years in captivity 多年囚禁, animals in captivity 圈养动物, captive 俘虏的, release 释放
[联] captivity, captivation：囚禁, 着迷 captivity, captive：状态, 人/形容词, enthralled, spellbound：spellbound 更强调「惊呆般定住」 enthralled, captivated：enthralled 更书面、沉浸感更强
[记] 与 carping 并读

**enthralled**
英/美 /ɪnˈθrɔːld/
adj. 着迷的, 被迷住的
[义] Captivated and held spellbound; giving complete attention and delight. 被完全吸引、沉浸其中（enthralled by the novel 沉醉于小说, enthralled audience 如痴如醉的观众）。动词 enthrall（英式拼写 enthral）。与 captivated 近义，enthralled 更书面、强调全神贯注。只作形容词。
[例] ① Readers remained enthralled until the last page. 读者着迷至最后一页。 ② The documentary left us enthralled by Arctic wildlife. 纪录片让我们对北极野生动物沉醉不已。
[搭] enthralled by 沉醉于, enthralled audience 如痴如醉的观众, sit enthralled 坐着入迷, leave sb. enthralled 使人着迷, enthralled listeners 入神的听众, utterly enthralled 完全着迷
[记] 美式拼写 enthrall；英式 enthral。enthralled 与 spellbound, mesmerized 并提沉浸强度

**spellbound**
英/美 /ˈspelbaʊnd/
adj. 入迷的, 被施了魔似的（凝神屏息）
[义] So fascinated that one gives complete attention, as if under a spell. 像被施魔法般全神贯注（spellbound children 凝神的孩子, hold sb. spellbound 使人屏息凝神）。来自 spell + bound。与 mesmerized 近义：spellbound 偏故事、演讲的凝神，mesmerized 偏凝视、催眠感。只作形容词。
[例] ① The storyteller held the class spellbound. 讲故事的人让全班屏息凝神。 ② We watched spellbound as the eclipse began. 日食开始时我们目不转睛地看着。
[搭] hold sb. spellbound 使人屏息凝神, spellbound audience 入神的观众, sit spellbound 屏息观看, spellbound silence 鸦雀无声, leave the crowd spellbound 让观众如痴如醉, spellbound by 被…镇住
[联] spellbound, mesmerized：mesmerized 更绑凝视与催眠意象 spellbound, enthralled：spellbound 更「定住」；enthralled 更沉醉, mesmerized, spellbound：mesmerized 偏凝视催眠感 mesmerized, captivated：mesmerized 更强、更「定住」
[记] 无比较级常用搭配。spellbound 与 mesmerized, galvanized 并提强烈专注—行动链

**mesmerized**
英/美 /ˈmezməraɪzd/
adj. 被催眠般吸引的, 目不转睛的
[义] Hypnotized or fascinated so that attention cannot be turned away. 像被催眠般无法移开目光或注意力（mesmerized by the flames 盯着火焰出神, mesmerized crowd 呆视的观众）。动词 mesmerize；来自 Mesmer（麦斯麦，催眠术先驱）。与 spellbound 并提：mesmerized 更视觉—凝视，spellbound 更叙事—凝神。只作形容词。
[例] ① Tourists stood mesmerized before the giant waterfall. 游客站在巨瀑前看得发呆。 ② She was mesmerized by the dancer’s footwork. 她被舞者的步法看得入迷。
[搭] mesmerized by 被…吸引得目不转睛, mesmerized crowd 呆视的观众, sit mesmerized 呆坐着看, mesmerizing performance 令人着迷的表演, completely mesmerized 完全看呆, mesmerized gaze 呆视的目光
[记] mesmerizing 令人着迷的（定语）。mesmerized 与 galvanized, inspired 并提由吸引到行动

**galvanized**
英/美 /ˈɡælvənaɪzd/
adj. 被激励的, 被鞭策而行动的（常作表语）
[义] Shocked or stimulated into sudden activity or strong feeling; motivated to act urgently. 受到强烈刺激而立刻行动或振奋（galvanized into action 被激励行动起来, galvanized public 被鼓动的公众）。动词 galvanize。与 inspired 并提：galvanized 偏紧迫、集体行动（crisis galvanized the nation），inspired 偏信心与创意。只作形容词（表语）。物理义 galvanized steel 镀锌钢需据语境区分。
[例] ① The disaster galvanized volunteers across the region. 灾难激励了全区域的志愿者行动起来。 ② She felt galvanized to speak out after hearing the testimony. 听完证词她感到被鞭策要发声。
[源] 来自科学家 Galvani（电流实验）→ galvanize 原义「通电」→ 喻使人像触电般振奋、行动起来 → galvanized。
[族] galvanize 激励, galvanizing 振奋人心的, galvanism 电流作用（罕用）
[搭] galvanized into action 被激励行动起来, galvanized by the speech 被演讲振奋, galvanized public 被鼓动的公众, galvanizing moment 振奋时刻, feel galvanized 感到被鞭策, galvanized support 被激起的支持
[联] galvanized, inspired：galvanized 偏紧迫行动；inspired 偏信心与灵感 galvanized（情感）vs galvanized, steel：同形异义, inspired, inspiring：inspired 感受者「被鼓舞」；inspiring 令人鼓舞的 inspired, galvanized：inspired 偏灵感与信心；galvanized 偏紧迫行动
[记] galvanize 及物动词。galvanized 与 inspired, humbled 并提正负激励链

**inspired**
英/美 /ɪnˈspaɪəd/
adj. 受鼓舞的, 有灵感的；出色的
[义] ① Affected with a strong feeling of enthusiasm, creativity, or confidence because of someone or something. ② Excellent, as if filled with inspiration (an inspired choice 绝妙选择). ① 因某人或某事而振奋、充满创意或信心（feel inspired 感到受鼓舞, inspired by her mentor 受导师鼓舞）。② 定语：出色的、神来之笔的（inspired performance 惊艳表演）。动词 inspire；与 05-行为与动作 inspire 并读：偏动词用法与激励他人，本篇偏说话人自身情感状态。与 galvanized 区分：inspired 更持久、正面、可含艺术灵感；galvanized 更突发、紧迫、常绑集体行动。只作形容词。
[例] ① After the workshop, the team felt inspired to try new ideas. 工作坊后团队感到受鼓舞要尝试新点子。 ② It was an inspired decision to hire her as creative director. 聘她为创意总监是绝妙决定。
[搭] feel inspired 感到受鼓舞, inspired by 受…鼓舞, inspired performance 惊艳表演, inspired choice 绝妙选择, inspired leader 有远见的领导, leave sb. inspired 使人振奋
[记] inspiring 定语/表语。inspire 。inspired 与 humbled, degraded 并提自尊升降链

**breathtaking**
英/美 /ˈbreθteɪkɪŋ/
adj. 惊人的, 壮丽的, 令人屏息的
[义] Astonishing or awe-inspiring, often because of beauty, scale, or speed; literally taking one's breath away. 因壮美、规模或技艺而令人屏息、惊叹（breathtaking view 壮丽景色, breathtaking speed 惊人速度, breathtaking beauty 绝美）。与 chilling 对照：breathtaking 多为正面震撼，chilling 为阴森恐惧。只作形容词。
[例] ① The cliff offered a breathtaking view of the coast. 悬崖上壮丽的海岸景色令人屏息。 ② Her breathtaking skill won a standing ovation. 她惊艳的技艺赢得起立鼓掌。
[搭] breathtaking view 壮丽景色, breathtaking speed 惊人速度, breathtaking beauty 绝美, breathtaking performance 惊艳表演, breathtaking ignorance 令人咋舌的无知（可贬）, take one's breath away 令人屏息
[记] breathtaking 可褒可贬（breathtaking stupidity 愚蠢得惊人）。breathtaking 与 stunned, overjoyed 并提强烈情绪反应链

**love**
英/美 /lʌv/
n. 爱；爱情
v. 爱；热爱
[义] 情感尺度极宽：可对配偶说 I love you，也可对披萨说 I love pizza（中文对物较少用「爱」）。名词表「爱、爱情」（fall in love 坠入爱河）；动词表「热爱」活动（love reading）。
[例] ① They love each other deeply after twenty years of marriage. 结婚二十年后，他们仍然深深相爱。 ② I love reading novels on rainy afternoons. 我热爱 在雨天下午读小说。
[搭] fall in love, in love with 爱上, love doing sth. 热爱做某事, would love to 很愿意（礼貌）, love letter 情书, send love 代问候（英式）
[联] adore：深爱、崇拜，强度更高, like：喜欢，强度低得多 be fond, of：喜爱，较温和, hate 憎恨, dislike 不喜欢
[记] 可接人、物、动名词。would love to = 很愿意（礼貌请求）。对老板不宜说 love you——语境敏感

**beloved**
英/美 /bɪˈlʌvɪd/
adj. 深爱的, 挚爱的
n. 心爱的人, 挚爱
[义] Dearly loved; a person who is dearly loved. 被深爱的；心爱的人/挚爱（my beloved 我的挚爱, beloved teacher 敬爱的老师, the beloved of the nation 国民挚爱）。形容词/名词。表述宜用中性、温情或庄重语气。
[例] ① She read a poem to her beloved at the ceremony. 她在典礼上向 beloved 朗诵诗篇。 ② The beloved leader was mourned nationwide. 全国哀悼这位 beloved 领袖。
[源] be- + loved（被爱）→ 被深爱或珍视之人或物。
[族] love 爱, lover 爱人, belove 深爱（罕）, darling 亲爱的
[搭] my beloved 我的挚爱, beloved teacher 敬爱的老师, the beloved of the nation 国民挚爱, dearly loved 深爱的
[联] beloved, lover（日常）：挚爱/敬爱, 情人 beloved, belove：形容词/名词, 罕动词
[记] 名词可数；形容词常作定语或表语。与 benevolence 并读仁慈链

**hate**
英/美 /heɪt/
n. 憎恨
v. 憎恨；讨厌
[义] 可表真正仇恨，也可口语夸张（I hate Mondays! 我讨厌周一！——≠ 真仇恨）。hate speech、hate crime 为严肃社会/法律用语，须依语境区分。
[例] ① I hate getting up early in winter. 我讨厌冬天早起。 ② Don't you just hate it when your phone dies? 手机没电的时候，你是不是特别烦？
[搭] hate doing/to do 讨厌做, hate it when… 讨厌……的时候, hate crime 仇恨犯罪, hate speech 仇恨言论, pet hate 特别讨厌的事（英式）
[联] dislike：不喜欢，强度低 can't stand 受不了（口语）, love 爱, like 喜欢
[记] 可接名词、动名词、不定式。口语夸张 hate ≠ 真仇恨；名词 hatred 更正式

**affection**
英/美 /əˈfekʃn/
n. 喜爱，钟爱，柔情
[义] 对某人或某物的温柔喜爱与亲近感——show affection 表达关爱, affection for a pet 对宠物的喜爱。比 love 强度低、范围宽（可对父母、朋友、宠物）；比 fondness 更常指可见的温情举动（hug, kind words）。不可数为主。
[例] ① The old couple still show affection for each other after fifty years of marriage. 结婚五十年，老两口仍彼此流露温情。 ② She has a special affection for the neighborhood where she grew up. 她对成长的街区怀有特殊感情。
[搭] deep/genuine affection 深厚/真挚的关爱, affection for 对……的喜爱, show/display affection 表露关爱, public affection 公开亲昵, affectionate 充满爱意的（形容词）
[联] fondness：喜爱，更偏个人偏好, love：爱，强度更高, warmth：温情，更抽象, dislike 不喜欢, hostility 敌意, indifference 冷漠
[记] affection for + 人/物/地点。形容词 affectionate（an affectionate child 亲昵的孩子）。勿与 affect（影响；装出）混淆拼写

**fondness**
英 /ˈfɒndnəs/ 美 /ˈfɑːndnəs/
n. 喜爱，爱好，钟爱
[义] 对某人、某物或某活动的稳定喜爱与好感——fondness for chocolate 爱吃巧克力, fondness for old friends 对老友的眷恋。比 affection 更偏个人口味、习惯与怀旧；比 love 轻。不可数为主。
[例] ① He has a fondness for collecting vinyl records from the 1980s. 他酷爱收集八十年代黑胶唱片。 ② Her fondness for the seaside town drew her back every summer. 她对那座海滨小镇的眷恋让她每年夏天都回去。
[搭] great/deep fondness 十分喜爱, fondness for 对……的喜爱/爱好, have a fondness for 爱好…… childhood fondness 童年眷恋, fond 喜爱的（形容词）
[联] liking：喜欢，更日常, affection：柔情，更绑人际温情, preference：偏好，更理性, dislike 不喜欢, aversion 厌恶, loathing 憎恶
[记] fondness for + 名词/动名词。形容词 fond（be fond of 喜欢）。fond memories 美好回忆。与 found（find 过去式）不同词

**adoration**
英/美 /ˌædəˈreɪʃn/
n. 崇拜，深爱，极度仰慕
[义] 对某人近乎偶像化的极度喜爱或崇敬——look at sb. with adoration 以崇拜目光注视, public adoration 万众仰慕。比 love 更绑「仰望、着迷」；比 devotion 更偏情感上的狂热仰慕而非长期忠诚。不可数为主。
[例] ① Fans showed their adoration when the singer appeared on stage. 歌手登台时，歌迷狂热仰慕。 ② The child's adoration of her older sister was obvious in every hug. 妹妹对姐姐崇拜式的喜爱体现在每次拥抱里。
[搭] in adoration 崇拜地, adoration of/for 对……的崇拜/深爱, gaze of adoration 仰慕的目光, worshipful adoration 近乎敬拜的仰慕, adore 深爱/崇拜（动词）
[联] worship：崇拜，宗教或比喻义, devotion：奉献、忠诚，更绑持久付出, admiration：钦佩，强度可略低, contempt 蔑视, scorn 鄙视, hatred 憎恨, adore, admire：热爱崇拜, 钦佩欣赏 adore, adore 宗教义, 日常义：依语境区分崇拜与「超喜欢」
[记] adoration of/for + 人/偶像。动词 adore（I adore you 可表深爱或口语「太喜欢了」）。宗教 adore 指敬拜上帝。形容词 adoring

**adore**
英/美 /əˈdɔː(r)/
v. 热爱, 崇拜, 非常喜欢
[义] To love someone very much; to like something very much; in religion, to worship. 深爱某人；非常喜欢某物；宗教义为崇拜（adore your children 疼爱孩子, absolutely adore 极其喜欢, adore a celebrity 崇拜明星）。及物动词。比 admire 情感 更浓、更亲。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① She adores her grandchildren and visits every week. 她疼爱孙辈，每周都去看。 ② I absolutely adore this café—the coffee is perfect. 我超爱这家咖啡馆，咖啡完美。
[源] 拉丁语 *adorare*（向…祈祷）→ adore 原义崇拜，今多指深爱、极度喜欢。
[族] adoration 崇拜, adorable 可爱的, adorer 崇拜者
[搭] adore your children 疼爱孩子, absolutely adore 极其喜欢, adore a celebrity 崇拜明星, adore the song 酷爱这首歌, adore spending time together 酷爱共处时光
[记] adorable 形容词（可爱）。adoration 较正式。与 admirer、adorer 并读仰慕—崇拜链

**adorer**
英/美 /əˈdɔːrə(r)/
n. 崇拜者, 酷爱者
[义] A person who adores someone or something. 热爱或崇拜某人/某物的人（adorers of the band 乐迷, secret adorer 秘密爱慕者, religious adorer 宗教崇拜者）。可数。由 adore + -er 构成。与 admirer 并提：adorer 情感 更强。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① Adorers camped outside the stadium overnight. 崇拜者 在体育场外露营过夜。 ② He was shy about meeting his childhood adorer. 见到童年时的爱慕者他有些害羞。
[搭] adorers of the band 乐迷, secret adorer 秘密爱慕者, religious adorer 宗教崇拜者, crowd of adorers 一群崇拜者
[联] adorer, admirer：崇拜酷爱, 仰慕欣赏 adorer, adore：人, 动词
[记] 日常更常说 fan, lover。与 admire—adore 链收束

**devotion**
英 /dɪˈvəʊʃn/ 美 /dɪˈvoʊʃn/
n. 奉献，忠诚，挚爱
[义] 对某人、信念或事业的坚定忠诚与全心投入——marital devotion 夫妻忠贞, devotion to duty 恪尽职守。比 adoration 更绑行动、时间与忠诚；比 infatuation 持久、理性。不可数为主。
[例] ① Her devotion to her patients earned the respect of the whole hospital. 她对病人的奉献赢得全院尊重。 ② His devotion to the team never wavered during losing seasons. 球队连败时，他对队伍的忠诚从未动摇。
[搭] selfless devotion 无私奉献, devotion to 对……的忠诚/奉献, with devotion 忠心地, religious devotion 宗教虔诚, devoted 忠诚的（形容词）
[联] dedication：奉献，更绑投入与坚持, loyalty：忠诚，更绑立场不变, commitment：承诺、投入，更中性, indifference 冷漠, betrayal 背叛, neglect 忽视
[记] devotion to + 人/事业/信仰。形容词 devoted（a devoted husband 忠诚的丈夫）。devotional 宗教礼拜的。与 device（设备）不同词

**infatuation**
英/美 /ɪnˌfætʃuˈeɪʃn/
n. 迷恋，痴迷（常短暂）
[义] 对某人强烈却常短暂、欠理智的迷恋——teenage infatuation 少年迷恋, infatuation with a celebrity 对明星的一时痴迷。比 love 浅、短；比 crush 更书面，强调晕头转向、看不清对方缺点。可数/不可数均可。
[例] ① What felt like true love was only a summer infatuation. 以为是真爱的，不过是夏日迷恋。 ② His infatuation with the idea of fame blinded him to the risks. 对成名之念的痴迷让他看不见风险。
[搭] brief infatuation 短暂的迷恋, infatuation with 对……着迷, mutual infatuation 彼此痴迷, romantic infatuation 浪漫迷恋, infatuated 着迷的（形容词）
[联] crush：迷恋（口语，尤指暗恋）, obsession：执念，可更病态, passion：激情，可更持久, indifference 冷漠, disenchantment 幻灭, hatred 憎恨
[记] infatuation with + 人/观念。形容词 infatuated（be infatuated with）。常与 love 对比：infatuation fades, love deepens。名词可数：an infatuation

**crush**
英/美 /krʌʃ/
n. 迷恋，暗恋（口语）；压碎；拥挤
[义] （口语）对某人的短暂强烈好感或暗恋，尤指青少年——have a crush on sb. 暗恋某人, school crush 校园暗恋。比 infatuation 更口语、更轻；crush 也可指迷恋的对象（He's my crush）。作动词/名词另有「压碎、拥挤」义。
[例] ① She had a crush on her lab partner but never told him. 她暗恋实验搭档，却从未说出口。 ② Teen magazines often talk about how to tell if someone has a crush on you. 青少年杂志常写如何判断某人是否喜欢你。
[搭] have a crush on sb. 暗恋某人, childhood crush 童年暗恋对象, massive crush 挤垮（另一义）, crush on a classmate 暗恋同学
[联] infatuation：迷恋，更书面, fancy：喜欢（英式口语）, liking：好感，更轻, indifference 无感, repulsion 反感
[记] have a crush on + 人（不用 crush with）。crush 作「心上人」可数：a huge crush on her。勿与 crash（碰撞）混淆。动词 crush 表压碎时与情感义同形异义

**attraction**
英/美 /əˈtrækʃn/
n. 吸引，吸引力；爱慕；景点
[义] ① 被吸引的感觉或力量——feel an attraction to sb. 被某人吸引, sexual attraction 性吸引；② 引申为旅游景点（tourist attraction）。比 crush 更中性、可指单向或双向；与 repulsion 相对。可数/不可数均可。
[例] ① They felt an immediate attraction the first time they met. 初次见面彼此便相互吸引。 ② The museum is the city's main tourist attraction. 这座博物馆是该市主要旅游景点。
[搭] feel attraction 感到吸引, attraction to/for 对……的吸引, mutual attraction 相互吸引, physical attraction 外表吸引, tourist attraction 旅游景点, attractive 有吸引力的（形容词）
[联] appeal：魅力，更抽象, charm：魅力，偏人格, draw：吸引力（名词，口语）, repulsion 排斥, aversion 厌恶, indifference 无感
[记] attraction to/for + 人/物。形容词 attractive；动词 attract。物理 magnetic attraction 与情感义同词。景点义可数：many attractions

**repulsion**
英/美 /rɪˈpʌlʃn/
n. 排斥，厌恶，反感
[义] 对某人或某物的强烈反感与想要远离——feel repulsion at the sight 一见就恶心反感。与 attraction 相对；比 disgust 可更绑本能退缩、物理「推开」（亦指磁斥力）。不可数为主。
[例] ① She felt a sudden repulsion when she heard the cruel remark. 听到那番残忍的话，她顿时反感。 ② Like poles of a magnet show repulsion when brought together. 磁铁同极靠近会产生斥力。
[搭] feel repulsion 感到排斥, repulsion toward/from 对……的反感, mutual repulsion 相互排斥, moral repulsion 道德上的厌恶, repulsive 令人厌恶的（形容词）
[联] disgust：厌恶，更绑恶心感, aversion：厌恶，更常用, revulsion：憎恶，强度可更高, attraction 吸引, appeal 魅力, liking 喜爱
[记] repulsion toward/from/at + 名词/动名词。形容词 repulsive（repulsive smell 难闻的气味）。动词 repel。与 repulsive behavior 搭配常见

**enamored**
英/美 /ɪˈnæmərd/
adj. 倾慕的，迷恋的，相爱的
[义] In love with someone; filled with love, admiration, or strong liking for a person or, less often, an idea or activity. 对某人（或较少地对某观念、活动）充满爱意、倾慕或强烈好感——enamored of/with sb. 迷恋某人, newly enamored couple 新相恋的一对, enamored of the idea 对想法很着迷。比 love 更书面、更绑「倾慕、沉醉于」；比 infatuation更可持久、少绑「一时糊涂」。只作形容词；常用 be enamored of/with。
[例] ① He was enamored of his colleague long before either of them admitted it. 在彼此承认之前，他早已倾慕同事。 ② The press became enamored with the young politician's reform plans. 媒体对年轻政治家的改革计划颇为着迷。
[搭] be enamored of/with sb. 迷恋某人, newly enamored 新相恋的, enamored of the idea 对想法着迷, deeply enamored 深倾慕, enamor 使迷恋（动词，少用）
[联] infatuated：迷恋的，更绑一时痴迷, smitten：坠入爱河的，更口语, captivated：被迷住的，更泛, indifferent 冷漠的, repulsed 反感的, disenchanted 不再着迷的
[记] enamored of/with + 人/观念；美式 enamored，英式 enamoured。enamored 可形容恋爱，也可略书面地形容对理念的沉醉。本篇 enchanted → enamored → smitten 为「爱恋升温」链

**smitten**
英/美 /ˈsmɪtn/
adj. 坠入爱河的，被迷住的，被打动的
[义] Strongly attracted to or in love with someone; deeply charmed or impressed by a person or thing. 核心义：突然被某人迷住、深陷爱河或强烈被打动——completely smitten 完全坠入爱河, smitten with sb. 迷恋某人, smitten by her smile 被她的笑击中。比 enamored 更口语、更绑「一下子被击中」；比 infatuated更可褒、少绑「糊涂」。只作形容词；常说 be smitten with/by。
[例] ① Within a week of meeting her, he was completely smitten. 认识她不到一周，他就完全坠入爱河了。 ② I'm smitten with this little café; we come here every Sunday. 我迷上了这家小咖啡馆，每周日都来。
[源] 动词 smite（重击、猛击）的过去分词 → 古义「被（神）击中、眷顾」→ 今指被某人或某物「击中内心」、突然深陷爱河或强烈着迷。
[族] smite 重击/打动（文）, smiting 猛击的, besotted 昏头昏脑的（近义口语）
[搭] completely smitten 完全坠入爱河, smitten with/by sb. 迷恋某人, smitten by her smile 被她的笑迷住, smitten look 痴迷的眼神, smite 重击（动词，文）
[联] enamored：倾慕的，更书面, infatuated：迷恋的，更绑一时痴迷, besotted：昏头昏脑的，更绑失去理智, indifferent 冷漠的, unmoved 无动于表的, repulsed 反感的, smitten, bitten：smitten 为 smite 的分词；bitten 为 bite（咬）的分词，无「迷恋」义 smitten, stricken：stricken 多指「遭受打击、悲痛」；smitten 偏「被爱/魅力击中」
[记] smitten with/by + 人/事物。smitten 可幽默也可浪漫（She's smitten with the new puppy. 她迷上了新小狗）。无名词形式。本篇 smitten → lovesick 为「爱恋—相思」链

**lovesick**
英/美 /ˈlʌvsɪk/
adj. 害相思病的，为情所困的，相思的
[义] Weak, sad, or unable to think clearly because you are in love, especially when the feeling is strong, unrequited, or distracting. 因恋爱（尤指强烈、单恋或分心）而憔悴、难过或神不守舍——lovesick teenager 害相思的少年, feel lovesick 感到为情所困, lovesick lyrics 相思歌词。比 smitten 更绑「痛苦、憔悴、单恋」；比 heartbroken 更轻、偏「相思病」而非心碎破裂。只作形容词。
[例] ① The lovesick student couldn't focus on exams after the summer romance ended. 夏日恋情结束后，害相思的学生无法专心备考。 ② She wrote lovesick poems in a notebook she never showed anyone. 她在从不示人的笔记本里写相思诗。
[搭] lovesick teenager/poet 害相思的少年/诗人, feel lovesick 为情所困, lovesick lyrics/sigh 相思歌词/叹息, lovesick look 相思神情, love-sick 亦作连字符（少用）
[联] pining：苦苦思念的，更绑「盼」, languishing：憔悴的，更书面, yearning：渴望的，更泛, indifferent 冷漠的, over sb. 已放下某人的, heart-whole 未伤心的
[记] lovesick 常略带夸张或文学色彩；口语可说 I'm so lovesick I can't eat。lovesick 偏「相思苦」；heartbroken 偏「关系破裂后的碎裂感」。本篇 lovesick → heartbroken / spurned 为「爱恋—拒斥」链

**heartbroken**
英/美 /ˈhɑːtbrəʊkən/
adj. 心碎的，极度伤心的
[义] Extremely sad and upset because of loss, rejection, or the end of a relationship; feeling as if your heart has broken. 因失去、被拒或关系终结而极度悲伤，仿佛心都碎了——heartbroken over the breakup 因分手心碎, absolutely heartbroken 伤心欲绝, leave sb. heartbroken 使某人心碎。比 sad 更强、更绑「心上的破裂」；比 lovesick 更绑「打击已发生、关系已断」。只作形容词。
[例] ① She was heartbroken when her dog died after fifteen years together. 相伴十五年的狗去世后，她心碎了。 ② The letter left him heartbroken but determined to move on. 那封信让他心碎，却也决心往前走。
[搭] heartbroken over/about 因……心碎, absolutely heartbroken 伤心欲绝, leave sb. heartbroken 使某人心碎, heartbroken expression 心碎的表情, break sb.'s heart 伤某人的心（动词短语）
[联] devastated：崩溃的，更绑打击之深, grief-stricken：悲痛欲绝的，更绑丧亲, crushed：被击垮的，更口语, overjoyed 欣喜若狂的, elated 兴高采烈的, healed 已疗愈的
[记] heartbroken over/about + 名词/-ing。名词 heartbreak（a tale of heartbreak 心碎故事）。heartbroken 可用于恋爱、宠物、梦想落空等。本篇 heartbroken → spurned 为「拒斥—伤害」链，转向 vindictive / vengeful

**spurned**
英/美 /spɜːnd/
adj. 被拒斥的，遭摒弃的，被轻蔑拒绝的
[义] Rejected with contempt or scorn, especially in love or friendship; treated as not good enough. 被轻蔑地拒绝或摒弃，尤指恋爱或友情中的拒斥——a spurned lover 被拒的情人, feel spurned 感到遭摒弃, spurned advances 被回绝的示好。比 rejected 更书面、更绑「轻蔑、不屑」；比 heartbroken 更绑「被拒这一侧」的屈辱感。只作形容词；动词 spurn 轻蔑地拒绝。
