[例] ① The novel follows a spurned lover who seeks revenge in all the wrong ways. 小说写一个被拒的情人用种种错误方式报复。 ② She felt spurned when her suggestions were dismissed without a hearing. 建议未获倾听就被打发，她感到遭轻蔑拒绝。
[搭] a spurned lover/suitor 被拒的情人/求婚者, feel spurned 感到遭摒弃, spurned advances 被回绝的示好, spurn an offer 轻蔑地拒绝提议（动词）
[联] rejected：被拒绝的，更中性, jilted：被抛弃的（恋爱），更绑「甩」, scorned：被鄙视的，更绑蔑视, welcomed 受欢迎的, embraced 被接纳的, accepted 被接受的
[记] spurned 作定语（a spurned lover）或表语（feel spurned）。动词 spurn 比 reject 更带「不屑、唾弃」（spurn their help 不屑其帮助）。本篇 spurned → vindictive / vengeful 为「拒斥—怨怼」链

**tender**
英/美 /ˈtendə(r)/
adj. 温柔的，体贴的，慈爱的
[义] Gentle, kind, and showing care and affection; soft in feeling or touch, especially toward someone vulnerable. 温柔体贴、充满关爱，尤指对弱者或亲近之人——tender moment 温馨时刻, tender care 悉心照料, tender words 温情话语。比 gentle 更绑「情感上的软与暖」；比 affection更绑「轻柔、呵护」。只作形容词；名词 tenderness 温柔。
[例] ① He spoke in a tender voice to the frightened child. 他用温柔的声线对受惊的孩子说话。 ② There was a tender silence between them after the apology. 道歉之后，两人之间有一段温情的沉默。
[搭] tender moment/care 温馨时刻/悉心照料, tender words/look 温情话语/眼神, tender age 年幼（固定）, tenderness 温柔（名词）
[联] gentle：温和的，更泛, loving：充满爱意的，更绑 love, affectionate：亲昵的，更绑外露举动, harsh 苛刻的, ruthless 无情的, callous 冷酷的
[记] tender 作「投标」义（n./v.）与情感义同形，依语境区分。tender age 年幼。tender 可形容情感、话语、触摸（tender meat 嫩肉，义不同）。本篇 tender → moved / stirred 为「柔—动」链

**moved**
英/美 /muːvd/
adj. 感动的，动情的
[义] Feeling strong emotion, especially sympathy, gratitude, or sadness, because of something you have seen or heard. 因所见所闻而强烈动情，尤指感激、同情或伤感——deeply moved 深受感动, moved by the speech 被演讲打动, moved to tears 感动落泪。比 touched 更强、更绑「内心被推动」；比 stirred 更常绑「感动、泪目」。只作形容词；动词 move 打动。
[例] ① The audience was visibly moved by the veteran's account of the war. 听众显然被老兵的战争讲述打动了。 ② We were moved by how neighbors rallied after the fire. 邻居们在火灾后纷纷援手，让我们感动。
[搭] deeply moved 深受感动, moved by the speech/story 被演讲/故事打动, moved to tears 感动落泪, moving 感人的（形容词，修饰事物）
[联] touched：被触动的，更轻, stirred：被激起的，更绑「心潮起伏」, emotional：情绪激动的，更泛, unmoved 无动于表的, indifferent 冷漠的, detached 超然的
[记] moved by + 事物；moved to tears / moved to action 感动落泪/感动而行动。moving 修饰事物（a moving film 感人电影），moved 修饰人。本篇 moved ↔ stirred 为「感动—激荡」链，与 incensed 等「怒」词对照

**stirred**
英/美 /stɜːd/
adj. 心潮澎湃的，被激起的，动情的
[义] Emotionally aroused or excited; feeling strong feelings beginning to move inside you, often a mix of passion, memory, or resolve. 情绪被激起、心潮起伏，常含激情、回忆或决心的混合——stirred by the music 被音乐激起情怀, deeply stirred 心潮澎湃, stirred memories 被唤起的记忆。比 moved 更绑「内心被搅起、尚未落定」；比 excited 更书面、可绑复杂情感。只作形容词；动词 stir 搅动/激起。
[例] ① Something in the poem stirred her, though she couldn't say exactly what. 诗中的某种东西触动了她，尽管她说不清是什么。 ② The crowd grew stirred as the speaker called for reform. 演讲者呼吁改革，人群情绪渐起。
[搭] stirred by the music/sermon 被音乐/布道激起, deeply stirred 心潮澎湃, stirred memories/emotions 被唤起的记忆/情感, stir sb.'s conscience 触动某人良知（动词）
[联] moved：感动的，更常绑「泪目、感激」, roused：被唤醒的，更绑「从静到动」, stimulated：被激发的，更中性, unmoved 无动于表的, apathetic 冷漠的, impassive 无动于衷的
[记] stirred 作表语或过去分词（I was stirred by…）。stir up trouble/emotions 挑起事端/情绪。stirred 偏「心被搅起」；moved 偏「已被打动」。本篇 stirred → incensed / enraged / livid 为「情动—怒升」链

**longing**
英/美 /ˈlɒŋɪŋ/
n. 渴望, 思念
adj. 渴望的
[义] A prolonged, often tender or sad desire for someone or something absent or unattainable. 对不在或不可得之人或物的绵长渴望、思念——longing for the past 怀念过去, a look of longing 渴望的眼神。比 yearning 更日常、更通用；比 pining 更少「憔悴」色彩。不可数/可数均可。动词 long for。
[例] ① He spoke with longing about his hometown. 他带着思念谈起家乡。 ② There is a longing in her music that moves listeners. 她的音乐里有一种打动人的眷恋。
[搭] longing for the past 怀念过去, a look of longing 渴望的眼神, long for 渴望, romantic longing 浪漫思念, deep longing 深切渴望
[联] yearning 向往 nostalgia 怀旧
[记] 不可数/可数均可。long for + 名词/-ing。longing 与 lust, platonic 并提爱恋光谱

**yearning**
英/美 /ˈjɜːnɪŋ/
n. 渴望, 向往
adj. 渴望的
[义] A feeling of intense longing or desire for something or someone, often emotional or romantic. 对某人或某物的强烈向往、渴求——yearning for home 思乡, romantic yearning 浪漫渴望。比 craving 更诗意、偏情感；比 pining 更可名词化、略抽象。不可数/可数均可。动词 yearn。
[例] ① There was a yearning in her voice when she spoke of childhood. 谈到童年时她声音里带着向往。 ② He felt a yearning for a simpler life in the countryside. 他对乡村简朴生活心生渴望。
[搭] yearning for home 思乡, romantic yearning 浪漫渴望, yearn for 渴望, deep yearning 深切向往, yearning look 渴望的眼神
[联] longing 渴望 pining 苦思（
[记] 不可数/可数均可。yearning 与 homesickness 思乡并读；与 longing, craving 并提渴慕链

**pining**
英/美 /ˈpaɪnɪŋ/
n. 苦思, 憔悴思念
[义] Suffering mental or physical decline from longing for someone or something absent. 因思念不在的人或物而憔悴、苦闷——pining for a lost love 苦恋, pining away 思念成疾。动词 pine。比 yearning 更绑痛苦、消瘦；比 longing 更戏剧化。不可数。
[例] ① She spent weeks pining for her friend abroad. 她数周苦思海外的朋友。 ② The old song is about pining for a summer that never returns. 老歌咏的是对回不去夏天的眷恋。
[搭] pining for a lost love 苦恋, pine away 思念成疾, pining look 憔悴神情, pine for sb. 思念某人, die of pining 相思成疾（夸张/文学）
[联] yearning 渴望 longing 渴望
[记] 不可数。pine for + 名词/-ing。pining 与 longing, craving 并提思念强度

**craving**
英/美 /ˈkreɪvɪŋ/
n. 渴求, 瘾念
[义] An intense, often physical desire for something (food, nicotine, affection). 强烈、常带生理感的渴求——craving for chocolate 想吃巧克力, satisfy a craving 满足渴求。比 longing 更急、更生理；比 lust 更可指食物/物质。可数（a craving, cravings）。动词 crave。
[例] ① Late-night cravings made him raid the fridge. 深夜嘴馋让他翻冰箱。 ② She had a craving for salt after the long run. 长跑后她特别想吃咸的。
[搭] craving for chocolate 想吃巧克力, satisfy a craving 满足渴求, crave attention 渴望关注, food cravings 食欲渴求, intense craving 强烈渴求
[联] longing 渴望（更情感） desire 欲望（更宽）
[记] 可数。craving 与 lust 区分：craving 可中性（食物），lust 常指情欲。craving 与 longing 并提渴慕—渴求

**crave**
英/美 /kreɪv/
v. 渴望，渴求
[义] To feel a powerful desire for. 渴望，渴求（crave attention 渴望关注）。
[例] ① She craved silence after the tour. 巡演后她渴望安静。 ② Athletes crave carbs after long runs. 长跑后运动员渴求碳水。
[搭] crave attention 渴望关注, craving 渴求, desire 渴望

[联] long for 渴望, desire 渴望
**lust**
英/美 /lʌst/
n. 情欲, 强烈欲望
v. 渴求, 贪求
[义] ① Strong sexual desire. ② Intense desire for power, money, etc. ① 强烈性欲、情欲（sexual lust 性欲）。② 对权力、金钱等的贪婪渴求（lust for power 权力欲）。动词 lust after / lust for。比 craving 更绑情欲或道德色彩；比 erotic 更名词化、更强烈。可数/不可数均可。
[例] ① The novel explores the conflict between love and lust. 小说探讨爱 与情欲的冲突。 ② Politicians warned against a lust for unchecked power. 政界人士警惕对不受制约权力的贪欲。
[源] 古英语 *lust*（欲望, 快乐）→ 现代义常指强烈性欲（sexual lust），亦可指对权力、金钱等的贪婪（lust for power 权力欲）。与 love 区分：lust 偏肉体或强烈占有欲，未必含深情。
[族] lustful 好色的, lustrous 有光泽的（不同源, 勿混）, luster 光泽
[搭] sexual lust 性欲, lust for power 权力欲, lust after 贪求, lustful glance 好色一瞥, lust vs love 情欲与爱
[联] desire 欲望（更宽） passion 激情（可褒可贬）, platonic love 柏拉图式之爱, lust, craving：lust 常指情欲或贪婪；craving 可指食物、尼古丁等渴求
[记] lust after/for + 名词。lustful 常贬。lust 与 erotic, platonic 并提爱恋类型

**erotic**
英/美 /ɪˈrɒtɪk/
adj. 色情的, 性爱的
[义] Relating to sexual love or desire; arousing sexual feeling. 与性爱或情欲相关的；引起性兴奋的——erotic art 色情艺术, erotic dream 春梦。只作形容词。与 platonic 相对：erotic 涉性爱吸引力，platonic 强调无性爱成分。名词 erotica（色情作品, 不可数）。表述宜尊重语境，避免骚扰含义。
[例] ① The film was criticized for overly erotic scenes. 该片因过于色情的镜头受批评。 ② Scholars debated whether the poem had an erotic subtext. 学者争论这首诗是否有情欲潜台词。
[搭] erotic art 色情艺术, erotic dream 春梦, erotic literature 情色文学, erotic charge 性张力, erotica 色情作品
[联] platonic 柏拉图式的
[记] 形容词不可数修饰。erotic 与 platonic, lust 并提爱恋—吸引光谱

**platonic**
英/美 /pləˈtɒnɪk/
adj. 柏拉图式的, 纯精神恋爱的
[义] (of love or friendship) intimate and affectionate but not sexual. （爱或友谊）亲密而真挚，但不含性爱成分——platonic friendship 纯友谊, platonic relationship 柏拉图式关系。与 erotic, lust 对照。只作形容词。勿与 Platonic solids（柏拉图立体, 几何）混淆语境。
[例] ① They insisted their bond was strictly platonic. 他们坚称彼此的关系纯粹是友谊。 ② A platonic hug can still express deep care. 柏拉图式的拥抱仍可表达深切关怀。
[源] 来自哲学家 Plato（柏拉图）；Platonic love 指古希腊传统中升华至精神、非肉体占有的爱，现代常指无性爱成分的亲密（platonic friendship 纯友谊）。
[族] Plato 柏拉图, Platonism 柏拉图主义, platonic ideal 柏拉图式理想
[搭] platonic friendship 纯友谊, platonic relationship 柏拉图式关系, purely platonic 纯粹精神层面, platonic love 柏拉图之爱, remain platonic 保持无性交往
[联] erotic 色情的, 性爱的 romantic（含性吸引义时）, platonic, erotic：platonic 强调无性爱；erotic 强调性爱或性吸引
[记] platonic 作定语。与 爱恋词并读可辨情欲—精神之爱。platonic 后接 sublime 为「世俗爱恋—崇高」过渡

**intimacy**
英/美 /ˈɪntɪməsi/
n. 亲密，亲近；私密
[义] A close, familiar, and usually affectionate personal relationship; the quality of being private and personal. 彼此熟悉、信任、可分享私密心事与身体的亲近感——emotional intimacy 情感亲密, intimacy with sb. 与某人的亲密。比 closeness 更绑深度与私密；比 affection更重、可含浪漫或性。不可数为主。
[例] ① Trust and intimacy grew slowly after they started sharing their fears. 开始分享恐惧后，信任与亲密慢慢加深。 ② The novel explores the intimacy and tension between two old friends. 小说探讨两位老友之间的亲密 与张力。
[搭] emotional intimacy 情感亲密, intimacy with sb. 与某人的亲密, a lack of intimacy 缺乏亲密, intimate 亲密的（形容词）, intimate conversation 私密谈话
[联] closeness：亲近，更日常, familiarity：熟悉，可含「过分随便」, affection：柔情，更温和, distance 疏离, estrangement 疏远, loneliness 孤独
[记] intimacy with/between + 人。形容词 intimate（an intimate relationship 亲密关系）。与 vulnerability 合读：intimacy 常需一定 vulnerability。勿与 intimate（形容词）仅记「性」义，情感亲密同样常用

**enchanted**
英 /ɪnˈtʃɑːntɪd/ 美 /ɪnˈtʃæntɪd/
adj. 着迷的，陶醉的，被迷住的
[义] Filled with delight and fascination; feeling as if under a magical spell, especially because of beauty, charm, or a wonderful experience. 因魅力、美感或美好经历而满心欢喜、仿佛被施了魔法般陶醉——enchanted by the view 为景色着迷, an enchanted evening 令人陶醉的夜晚, enchanted audience 入迷的观众。比 fascinated 更绑「魔法、童话、梦幻」色彩；比 enamored 更可绑非恋爱对象（风景、音乐）。只作形容词；动词 enchant 使着迷。
[例] ① We were enchanted by the small village lit with lanterns at dusk. 暮色中灯笼点亮的村落让我们陶醉。 ② The children sat enchanted as the storyteller began the fairy tale. 讲故事的人一开讲，孩子们入迷地坐着。
[搭] enchanted by/with 为……着迷, an enchanted evening/forest 令人陶醉的夜晚/魔法森林, look enchanted 显得入迷, enchanted smile 陶醉的微笑, enchant 使着迷（动词）
[联] captivated：被吸引的，更绑注意力, charmed：被迷住的，更口语, fascinated：着迷的，更中性, disenchanted 不再着迷的, disillusioned 幻灭的, bored 厌烦的
[记] enchanted by/with + 名词。童话语境 enchanted forest 魔法森林。disillusioned 以 enchanted 为反义收束，本篇 enchanted 开启「爱恋升温」链，与 enamored / smitten 并读

**disillusioned**
英/美 /ˌdɪsɪˈluːʒənd/
adj. 幻灭的，理想破灭的，大失所望的
[义] Disappointed because you have discovered that something or someone is not as good as you believed. 发现人、事、制度不如原先所想那么好，因而理想破灭、失望清醒——become disillusioned with 对……幻灭, deeply disillusioned 十分幻灭, disillusioned voter 失望选民。比 disappointed 更绑「信念/illusion 被戳破」；比 cynical 更绑「曾经相信过」。只作形容词；动词 disillusion 使幻灭。
[例] ① Many young teachers become disillusioned within their first five years. 许多年轻教师头五年就幻灭了。 ② He grew disillusioned with politics after the scandal. 丑闻之后，他对政治大失所望。
[搭] become disillusioned with 对……幻灭, deeply disillusioned 十分幻灭, disillusioned voter/worker 失望选民/员工, disillusioned by experience 因经历而清醒, disillusionment 幻灭（名词）
[联] disappointed：失望的，更泛、未必涉及信念, cynical：愤世嫉俗的，更绑 thereafter 不信人性, jaded：腻烦的，更绑见多识广后的倦, idealistic 理想主义的, optimistic 乐观的, enchanted 着迷的
[记] disillusioned with/by + 名词。disillusion 动词（The film disillusioned fans. 电影让粉丝幻想破灭）。disillusioned 暗含「曾抱 illusion」；disappointed 可仅指一事落空。本篇以 disillusioned 收束：自 schadenfreude 的「他人之失」到 disillusioned 的「己之信念落空」，与 equanimity / nonchalance 链合读可辨凝神、心温与幻灭

**trust**
英/美 /trʌst/
n. 信任，信赖
v. 信任，托付
[义] A firm belief in the honesty, reliability, and good intentions of someone; the willingness to be vulnerable with another person because you feel safe with them. 相信对方诚实可靠、愿把心事与依赖交给对方——build trust 建立信任, breach of trust 背信, trust issues 信任问题。比 belief 更绑人际与情感；比 confidence更绑「把安危交给对方」。不可数为主。
[例] ① It took months of honesty before she could trust him again. 数月坦诚相待后，她才再次信任他。 ② Their friendship was built on mutual trust and respect. 他们的友谊建立在相互信任 与尊重之上。
[搭] build/earn trust 建立/赢得信任, trust in sb. 信赖某人, breach of trust 背信, trust issues 信任障碍, trustworthy 值得信赖的（形容词）
[联] faith：信念，更绑坚定信赖, confidence：信心，可指对能力, reliance：依赖，更偏依靠, distrust 不信任, suspicion 猜疑, betrayal 背叛
[记] trust sb. with sth. 把……托付给某人；trust sb. to do 相信某人会做。形容词 trustworthy / trusting。认知 已收 trust 与 distrust 条；本篇从 betrayal 延伸至 distrust / suspicion / insecurity，补情感关系链

**distrust**
英/美 /dɪsˈtrʌst/
n. 不信任，怀疑
v. 不信任
[义] The feeling that you cannot trust someone or something; lack of trust in a person's honesty or motives. 对某人或某机构诚实与动机的不信——deep distrust 深深不信任, distrust of authority 对权威的不信任。比 suspicion 更稳定、更制度化；比 doubt 更绑人际关系。不可数为主。
[例] ① Years of broken promises created distrust between the two communities. 多年背信在两个社群间造成不信任。 ② She distrusts anyone who flatters her too quickly. 她不信任过快奉承她的人。
[搭] deep/widespread distrust 深深/普遍的不信任, distrust of 对……的不信任, distrust the media 不信任媒体, mutual distrust 相互猜疑, distrustful 不信任的（形容词）
[联] mistrust：不信任，常可互换, suspicion：猜疑，更绑「觉得有问题」, skepticism：怀疑，更偏理性质疑, trust 信任, faith 信赖, confidence 信心, trustworthy, sincere：值得信赖, 发自内心真诚 trustworthy, trusting：trustworthy 值得被信；trusting 愿意信人
[记] distrust of + 对象。认知 distrust 条与 trust 成对；本篇从情感角度与 betrayal / suspicion 串联。形容词 distrustful of

**trustworthy**
英/美 /ˈtrʌstwɜːði/
adj. 值得信赖的, 可靠的
[义] Able to be relied on to be honest, truthful, and do what is right. 可指望其诚实、守信、行事正当（trustworthy friend 可信赖的朋友, trustworthy source 可靠来源, prove trustworthy 证明可靠）。只作形容词。与 sincere并读：sincere 偏表达真心；trustworthy 偏行为可靠、可托付。品格深解亦表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① She is trustworthy and always keeps her promises. 她靠得住，一向信守承诺。 ② Journalists need trustworthy sources before publishing claims. 记者发表说法前需要可靠信源。
[源] trust（信任）+ -worthy（值得……的）→ trustworthy 指值得托付信任。
[族] trust 信任, trustworthiness 可信度, distrust 不信任, untrustworthy 不可信的
[搭] trustworthy friend 可信赖的朋友, trustworthy source 可靠来源, prove trustworthy 证明可靠, highly trustworthy 非常可靠, trustworthy partner 可信伙伴, trustworthy advice 可信建议
[记] trustworthiness 名词。与 trust、unreliable并读信任光谱。认知  亦有 trust 词条可延伸阅读

**unreliable**
英/美 /ˌʌnrɪˈlaɪəbl/
adj. 不可靠的, 靠不住的
[义] Not able to be trusted or depended on to do what is needed or promised. 无法指望其兑现所需或承诺之事（unreliable witness 不可靠证人, unreliable data 不可靠数据, unreliable friend 靠不住的朋友）。只作形容词。与 trustworthy 对照。03-性格 有 unreliable 与 punctual 对照。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① The old car became unreliable in winter. 那辆旧车冬天变得靠不住。 ② Colleagues stopped sharing tasks with an unreliable teammate. 同事不再与不可靠的队友分担任务。
[搭] unreliable witness 不可靠证人, unreliable data 不可靠数据, unreliable friend 靠不住的朋友, unreliable service 靠不住的服务, notoriously unreliable 出了名靠不住, prove unreliable 显得不可靠
[联] unreliable, trustworthy：靠不住, 值得信赖 unreliable, unpredictable：unreliable 偏失信；unpredictable 偏难预料行为
[记] unreliability 名词。与 predictable 并读：不可靠者往往也难预料。trust 反义链可合读

**suspicion**
英/美 /səˈspɪʃn/
n. 怀疑，猜疑，嫌疑
[义] A feeling that someone may be guilty of something or cannot be trusted; the act of suspecting. 觉得某人有问题、不可信或可能做了坏事——above suspicion 无可置疑, under suspicion 受到怀疑, suspicion of foul play 对舞弊的怀疑。比 distrust 更绑具体疑点、尚未证实；动词 suspect 见认知 。可数/不可数均可。
[例] ① His sudden wealth aroused suspicion among the neighbors. 他突然暴富，引起邻居猜疑。 ② She looked at the stranger with suspicion. 她怀疑地打量陌生人。
[搭] above suspicion 无可置疑, under suspicion 受到怀疑, suspicion of 对……的怀疑/嫌疑, arouse suspicion 引起怀疑, suspicious 可疑的/猜疑的（形容词）
[联] distrust：不信任，更稳定, doubt：怀疑，更泛、可非人际, mistrust：不信任，常可互换, trust 信任, confidence 信心, certainty 确定
[记] suspicion of + 名词/-ing（suspicion of cheating 作弊嫌疑）。形容词 suspicious of/about + 对象。认知 suspect 条已辨析 suspect vs. doubt；本篇 suspicion 偏情感上的「心里犯嘀咕」

**lonely**
英/美 /ˈləʊnli/
adj. 孤独的，寂寞的
[义] 因独处或缺乏陪伴而感到难过、渴望联结（feel lonely 感到孤独, lonely night 寂寞的夜晚）。比 alone 更绑负面情绪；alone 可仅指「独自一人」。
[例] ① She felt lonely in the new city even though she was never alone at work. 在新城市她感到孤独，尽管工作时身边总有人。 ② It's normal to feel lonely after a breakup. 分手后感到寂寞很正常。
[搭] feel lonely 感到孤独, lonely life 孤独的生活, lonely road 人迹罕至的路, terribly lonely 非常孤独
[联] lonesome 孤寂的（尤美式）, isolated 孤立的, surrounded 被簇拥的, accompanied 有人陪伴的
[记] 比较级 lonelier，最高级 loneliest。名词 loneliness。勿与 alone（独自）混淆。

**loneliness**
英 /ˈləʊnlinəs/ 美 /ˈloʊnlinəs/
n. 孤独，寂寞
[义] The unhappy feeling of being alone and wishing for company or connection; the state of being without friends or companionship. 独处时感到难过、渴望陪伴与联结——feel loneliness 感到孤独, epidemic of loneliness 孤独流行。比 solitude 更负面、更绑「缺人陪」；比 isolation 更绑内心感受。不可数为主。
[例] ① Social media sometimes increases loneliness instead of easing it. 社交媒体有时加剧孤独，而非缓解。 ② He wrote about the loneliness of living in a foreign city without family. 他写身在异乡、无亲人相伴的寂寞。
[搭] feel loneliness 感到孤独, deep loneliness 深深的孤独, loneliness of old age 老年的孤独, combat loneliness 对抗孤独, lonely 孤独的（形容词）
[联] isolation：隔离，更偏客观处境, solitude：独处，可中性或正面, alienation：疏离，更绑被排斥, companionship 陪伴, belonging 归属感, connection 联结
[记] lonely 形容词更口语（feel lonely）。loneliness epidemic 为公共卫生话语。本篇 loneliness vs. solitude 为关键对照：loneliness 必含「不好受」；solitude 可自愿、可享受

**solitude**
英 /ˈsɒlɪtjuːd/ 美 /ˈsɑːlətuːd/
n. 独处，独居；清静
[义] The state of being alone, especially when this is pleasant and peaceful; time spent alone by choice. 独自一人的状态，常指自愿、宁静、可反思——enjoy solitude 享受独处, seek solitude 寻求清静。比 loneliness 更中性或正面；比 isolation 少「被隔绝」的被迫感。不可数为主。
[例] ① She walks in the hills to find solitude and clear her mind. 她到山里散步，寻找独处 与清思。 ② The writer cherished the solitude of his cabin in the woods. 作家珍视林中木屋的清静。
[搭] enjoy solitude 享受独处, in solitude 独自地, seek solitude 寻求清静, peaceful solitude 宁静的独处, solitary 独自的（形容词）
[联] seclusion：隐居，更绑远离人群, isolation：隔离，可中性或负面, privacy：隐私，更绑「不被打扰」, company 陪伴, crowds 人群, sociability 社交性
[记] solitude 常自愿（choose solitude）；loneliness 常非自愿。形容词 solitary（a solitary walk 独自散步）。固定搭配 leave sb. in solitude 让某人独处

**belonging**
英/美 /bɪˈlɒŋɪŋ/
n. 归属（感）；所有物
[义] ① A feeling of being accepted and included in a group; ② the things that belong to someone. 被群体接纳、有「我是其中一员」之感——a sense of belonging 归属感, need to belong 归属需求。比 acceptance 更绑群体与身份；belongings 常指「财物」。不可数（归属感）/ 可数复数 belongings（所有物）。
[例] ① The club gave new students a strong sense of belonging. 社团给新生强烈的归属感。 ② Finding belonging after moving abroad took longer than she expected. 出国后找到归属比她预想的更久。
[搭] a sense of belonging 归属感, belonging to a community 属于某社群, need for belonging 归属需求, personal belongings 私人物品, belong to 属于（动词）
[联] acceptance：接纳，更偏「被接受」, inclusion：包容，更绑制度与政策, connection：联结，更泛, rejection 拒绝, exclusion 排斥, alienation 疏离
[记] sense of belonging 为固定搭配。belong to 动词表「属于」；belongings 复数表财物。本篇 belonging ↔ rejection / acceptance 为「归属—拒纳」链

**vulnerability**
英/美 /ˌvʌlnərəˈbɪləti/
n. 脆弱，易受伤害；可敞心扉的状态
[义] The quality of being easily hurt physically or emotionally; the willingness to show one's true feelings and weaknesses to others. 核心义：① 易被伤害、易受打击——emotional vulnerability 情感脆弱；② （正面）愿袒露弱点、不装强——show vulnerability 展现脆弱一面。比 fragility 更绑可被攻击的缺口；比 weakness 少贬、可含「真诚」。不可数为主。
[例] ① Admitting mistakes requires a kind of vulnerability that many leaders avoid. 承认错误需要许多领导者回避的那种敞开。 ② The scandal exposed the company's financial vulnerability. 丑闻暴露了公司财务上的脆弱。
[源] 源自拉丁语 *vulnus*（伤口）+ -able + -ity → 原义「可被伤害」→ 今指身心易受打击，亦指愿意暴露真实自我以建立亲密。
[族] vulnerable 脆弱的, invulnerable 无懈可击的, vulnerably adv.
[搭] emotional vulnerability 情感脆弱, show vulnerability 展现脆弱/敞开, sense of vulnerability 脆弱感, exploit sb.'s vulnerability 利用某人的弱点, vulnerable 脆弱的（形容词）
[联] weakness：弱点，更常贬义, fragility：脆弱，更绑易碎, sensitivity：敏感，更绑感受力, strength 力量, resilience 韧性, invulnerability 无懈可击, vulnerability, weakness：weakness 常指缺陷；vulnerability 可褒义指「愿示弱以建信任」 vulnerability, insecurity：insecurity 偏「我不够好」；vulnerability 偏「我可能受伤」或「我敢让你看见真实的我」, belong, belonger：动词, 成员 belong, own（日常）：属于, 拥有
[记] 现代心理与领导力话语中 show vulnerability 为正面（与 intimacy 相关）。resilience 反义列 vulnerability。本篇 vulnerability → intimacy 为「敞开—亲密」链

**belong**
英 /bɪˈlɒŋ/ 美 /bɪˈlɔːŋ/
v. 属于, 归属, 应在（某处）
[义] To be the property of; to be a member of; to be in the right place or fit in socially. 为……所有或成员；应在某处或融入群体，即属于/归属/应在（belong to a club 属于俱乐部, belong here 属于这里, feel you belong 感到归属）。动词。与 belonger并读：动词 vs 成员名词。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① These files belong in the shared drive. 这些文件应 belong 于共享盘。 ② Teens need spaces where they feel they belong. 青少年需要感到 belong 的空间。
[搭] belong to a club 属于俱乐部, belong here 属于这里, feel you belong 感到归属, belonger 成员
[记] belong to + 组织/人；belong in/on + 地点。与 belonger 并读

**belonger**
英 /bɪˈlɒŋə(r)/ 美 /bɪˈlɔːŋər/
n. 成员, 归属者（罕）
[义] One who belongs to a group, place, or tradition; a member or insider (somewhat rare). 属于某群体、地方或传统之人；成员/圈内人（party belonger 党派成员〔罕〕, coastal belongers 沿海老居民〔地方用语〕）。可数。belong + -er。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① The study contrasted newcomers with long-time belongers. 研究将新来者与长期 belonger 对照。 ② In some islands, belongers hold special voting rights. 部分岛屿 belonger 享有特殊投票权。
[搭] party belonger 党派成员（罕）, coastal belongers 沿海老居民（地方用语）, belong 属于, member 成员
[联] belonger, belong：成员, 动词 belonger, member（日常）：罕/地方, 常用
[记] 地方政治语境有专义。与 belove 并读慈爱链

**rejection**
英/美 /rɪˈdʒekʃn/
n. 拒绝，拒斥；否决
[义] The act of refusing to accept, believe, or allow someone; the feeling of being unwanted or not accepted. 被拒绝、不被接纳——fear of rejection 怕被拒, face rejection 遭遇拒绝, rejection letter 拒信。比 refusal 更绑情感伤害；比 exclusion 更绑「具体一次说不」。可数/不可数均可。
[例] ① After several rejections, she finally received a job offer. 数次被拒后，她终于拿到录用。 ② Fear of rejection kept him from asking anyone to dance. 怕被拒绝让他不敢邀人跳舞。
[搭] fear of rejection 怕被拒, face rejection 遭遇拒绝, rejection letter 拒信, social rejection 社交拒斥, reject 拒绝（动词）
[联] refusal：拒绝，更中性, exclusion：排斥，更绑被排除在外, dismissal：驳回，更正式, acceptance 接纳, approval 认可, welcome 欢迎, accept, acceptance：动词接受/承认, 名词（含情感接纳态） accept, receive：accept 强调认可；receive 强调收到
[记] rejection of + 提案/申请；rejection by + 人/机构。reject 动词（reject an offer 拒绝提议）。本篇 rejection ↔ acceptance 为直接反义对

**accept**
英/美 /əkˈsept/
v. 接受, 承认, 接纳
[义] To take something offered; to agree to something; to recognize a difficult truth; to regard someone as belonging. 收下或同意；承认难堪的事实；接纳某人（accept an apology 接受道歉, accept defeat 承认失败, accept yourself 接纳自己）。及物动词。情感链上与 acceptance并读：accept 为动词行动；acceptance 为心理状态。与 reject 对照。表述宜用温暖、中性语气。
[例] ① It took years to accept that the relationship was over. 她花数年才接受关系已结束。 ② Therapy helped him accept his anxiety instead of fighting it. 治疗帮他接纳焦虑而非对抗。
[源] 拉丁语 *acceptare*（取受）→ accept 指主观上认可、收下、承认。
[族] acceptance 接受, acceptable 可接受的, acceptance 情感接纳
[搭] accept an apology 接受道歉, accept defeat 承认失败, accept yourself 接纳自己, accept the situation 接受现实, accept responsibility 承担责任, widely accepted 广泛接受
[记] accept that 从句。acceptance 情感义。与 admire—adore 并读：先接纳自我，再健康仰慕他人

**acceptance**
英/美 /əkˈseptəns/
n. 接纳，接受；认可
[义] The act of agreeing to receive or take something offered; the feeling of being welcomed and included; approval of a situation you cannot change. 被群体或个人接纳；对无法改变之事的认命与和解——social acceptance 社会接纳, acceptance of defeat 接受失败, letter of acceptance 录取通知书。比 approval 更绑「纳入圈内」；比 tolerance 更积极。不可数为主。
[例] ① The support group offered her acceptance without judgment. 互助小组接纳她，不加评判。 ② His acceptance of the diagnosis allowed him to focus on treatment. 接受诊断后，他得以专心治疗。
[搭] social acceptance 社会接纳, acceptance of 对……的接受, letter of acceptance 录取通知书, gain acceptance 获得认可, accept 接受（动词）, acceptable 可接受的
[联] approval：认可，更偏赞成, welcome：欢迎，更绑热情, tolerance：包容，语气可更 passive, rejection 拒绝, refusal 拒绝, denial 否认, acceptability, acceptable：名词程度, 形容词
[记] acceptance of + 事实/提议/群体。self-acceptance 自我接纳为心理常用词。acceptance speech 获奖感言。本篇以 acceptance 收束「成败—信任—归属」链：从 fulfillment 经 betrayal / trust 至 belonging / rejection / acceptance

**acceptability**
英/美 /əkˌseptəˈbɪləti/
n. 可接受性, 可容许度
[义] The quality of being acceptable or allowable; the degree to which something can be tolerated or approved. 可被接受或认可的程度（social acceptability 社会可接受度, acceptability of grief 悲伤的可容许度, within acceptability limits 在可接受范围内）。不可数。与 acceptable并读：-ity 为名词抽象度。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① She questioned the acceptability of staying silent. 她质疑保持沉默的可接受性。 ② Cultural acceptability shapes how anger is expressed. 文化可接受度塑造愤怒表达方式。
[搭] social acceptability 社会可接受度, acceptability of emotions 情绪的可容许度, within acceptability limits 在可接受范围内, raise acceptability 提高可接受度
[记] 与 认知篇 acceptability 并读时，本篇偏情感—社会规范。后接 acceptable 形容词链

**acceptable**
英/美 /əkˈseptəbl/
adj. 可接受的, 尚可的, 合意的
[义] Able to be agreed to or tolerated; satisfactory though not ideal, especially in emotional or social judgment. 在情感或社会判断上可以被认可、忍受或认为还过得去的（socially acceptable grief 社会容许的悲伤表达, acceptable compromise 可接受的妥协, acceptable level of contact 可接受的联系程度）。只作形容词。与 accept 并读：accept 为行动；acceptable 为「这样还行吗」的评价。05-行为 偏标准与谈判；本篇偏关系—情绪边界。表述宜用温暖、中性语气。
[例] ① Is it acceptable to need space after an argument? 争吵后需要空间算可以接受吗？ ② Her tears felt acceptable for the first time in years. 多年来她的眼泪第一次显得可以被容许。
[源] 拉丁语 acceptare（取受）→ accept + -able → 能够被认可或承受的。
[族] accept 接受, acceptance 接纳, acceptability 可接受性
[搭] socially acceptable 社会可接受的, acceptable compromise 可接受的妥协, acceptable apology 可接受的道歉, barely acceptable 勉强能接受
[联] tolerable 可忍受的, satisfactory 令人满意的, permissible 容许的, unacceptable 不可接受的, intolerable 无法容忍的, acceptable, accepted：可以还行, 已被公认/收下 acceptable, accept：形容词评价, 动词接纳, acceptably, acceptable：副词方式, 形容词性质
[记] acceptable to + 人。与 acceptability、accepted 本组并读接纳光谱

**acceptably**
英/美 /əkˈseptəbli/
adv. 可接受地, 尚可地
[义] In a manner that is acceptable or satisfactory enough. 以可接受或足够令人满意的方式（behave acceptably 表现得体, acceptably calm 尚可的平静, acceptably honest 诚实程度尚可）。副词。由 acceptable + -ly 构成。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① He apologized acceptably, though not warmly. 他道了歉，还算过得去，但并不热忱。 ② She functioned acceptably at work while grieving. 哀伤中她工作上仍勉强应付得过去。
[搭] behave acceptably 表现得体, acceptably calm 尚可的平静, perform acceptably 表现尚可, acceptably honest 诚实程度尚可
[记] 与 acceptable、accepted 并读。否定用 unacceptably

**accepted**
英/美 /əkˈseptɪd/
adj. 被接受的, 公认的, 已被认可的
[义] Generally approved or recognized; taken as valid or normal; emotionally, felt as belonging or allowed. 被普遍认可或视为正常有效的；情感上亦可指被群体或自己「准许存在」的（widely accepted 广泛接受的, feel accepted 感到被接纳, accepted part of the family 被家庭认可的一员）。只作形容词。与 acceptable 并读：acceptable = 可以；accepted = 已经。表述宜用温暖语气。
[例] ① For years she never felt accepted by her peers. 多年来她在同伴中从未感到被接纳。 ② It became accepted to talk about burnout at work. 在职场上谈论倦怠已变得被普遍接受。
[源] accept 的过去分词作形容词 → 已被收下或广泛认可的状态。
[族] accept 接受, acceptance 接纳, acceptable 可接受的
[搭] feel accepted 感到被接纳, widely accepted 广泛接受的, accepted grief 被承认的悲伤, socially accepted 社会认可的
[联] recognized 被认可的, welcomed 受欢迎的, embraced 被拥抱的（比喻）, rejected 被拒绝的, excluded 被排斥的, ostracized 被疏远的, accepted, acceptable：已成状态, 可否达标 accepted, acceptant：被认可/接纳, 主动接纳的, acceptedly, accepted：副词, 形容词 acceptedly, acceptably：公认地, 尚可地
[记] feel accepted 为情感高频搭配。与 accept、acceptance 并读

**acceptedly**
英/美 /əkˈseptɪdli/
adv. 公认地, 已被接受地
[义] In a manner that is accepted or generally recognized; as something already agreed or established. 以已被公认或接纳的方式（acceptedly true 公认属实, acceptedly normal 已被视为正常）。副词。由 accepted+ -ly 构成。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① It is acceptedly normal to grieve after loss. 丧失后哀伤已被公认为正常。 ② The rule was acceptedly binding in that community. 该规则在该社群中已被公认有约束力。
[搭] acceptedly true 公认属实, acceptedly normal 已被视为正常, widely accepted 广泛接受的（形容词）, by accepted standards 按公认标准
[记] 产出更常说 widely accepted, generally recognized。与 accepted 并读

**accepter**
英/美 /əkˈseptə(r)/
n. 接受者, 接纳者
[义] One who accepts; a person or entity that receives or takes what is offered. 接受或接纳的人（accepter of terms 条款接受者, accepter of an apology 道歉的接受者）。可数。与 acceptor并读：accepter 偏一般接受者；acceptor 在化学/金融为受体、承兑人。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① As the accepter of the gift, she felt obliged to respond. 作为礼物的接受者，她觉得有义务回应。 ② Both parties named an accepter in the agreement. 双方在协议中指定接受方。
[搭] accepter of terms 条款接受者, accepter of an apology 道歉的接受者, acceptor 接受者/受体, accept 接受
[联] accepter, acceptor：一般接受者, 专名受体/承兑人 accepter, acceptress：接受者, 女性接受者, acceptant, accepting（日常）：文、罕, 口语「愿意接受的」 acceptant, acceptive：近义，acceptive 更罕
[记] 日常情感语境少用；比喻可读「愿意收下对方的人」。与 acceptress 并读

**acceptant**
英/美 /əkˈseptənt/
adj. 接纳的, 愿接受的
[义] Ready or willing to accept; receptive. 愿意接受或持接纳态度的（acceptant attitude 接纳态度, acceptant of change 愿接受改变, remain acceptant 保持接纳）。只作形容词。比 accepting（更口语）文、罕；情感写作可换用 open, receptive。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① Therapy encouraged an acceptant stance toward anger. 治疗鼓励对愤怒采取接纳立场。 ② She tried to stay acceptant without approving the harm. 她努力接纳现实却不认可伤害行为。
[搭] acceptant attitude 接纳态度, acceptant of change 愿接受改变, remain acceptant 保持接纳, acceptant listener 持接纳态度的倾听者
[记] 与 acceptive、accept 并读。产出优先用 accepting, open.

**acceptive**
英/美 /əkˈseptɪv/
adj. 接纳性的, 愿接受的
[义] Inclined to accept; characterized by acceptance. 倾向于接受或具有接纳性质的（acceptive mood 接纳心境, acceptive therapy 接纳取向治疗）。只作形容词。与 acceptant近义，acceptive 更罕。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① An acceptive stance reduced his inner struggle. 接纳性立场减轻了他的内心挣扎。 ② The counselor modeled acceptive responses. 咨询师示范接纳性回应。
[搭] acceptive mood 接纳心境, acceptive therapy 接纳取向治疗, acceptive stance 接纳立场, acceptive listening 接纳式倾听
[联] acceptive, acceptant：近义罕用形容词, acceptor, acceptor（coacceptor 词表 #1114）：受体, 共同承兑人（专名）
[记] 产出用 accepting, receptive。与 accept 并读

**acceptor**
英/美 /əkˈseptə(r)/
n. 接受者, 接纳者
[义] One who accepts; in chemistry/finance, an entity that accepts electrons, bills, etc. 接受或接纳的人或物；化学/金融中指受体、承兑人（electron acceptor 电子受体, social acceptor 社会接纳者〔比喻〕）。可数。情感比喻可读作「愿意收下对方的人」。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① In the metaphor, she became the acceptor of his apology. 在比喻中，她成为他道歉的接受者。 ② The molecule acts as an electron acceptor. 该分子充当电子受体。
[搭] electron acceptor 电子受体, bill acceptor 票据承兑人, social acceptor 社会接纳者（比喻）, role as acceptor 作为接受者的角色
[记] 日常情感语境少用；化学/金融识读。与 accept 动词链并读

**abandoned**
英/美 /əˈbændənd/
adj. 被抛弃的, 被遗弃的；荒废的；纵情不羁的（书面）
[义] ① Left completely and permanently by someone who should stay or take care. ② Left unused (buildings, places). ③ Literary: unrestrained (abandoned laughter). 被本应留下或照料者彻底、永久地丢下；荒废的（建筑、场所）；书面亦指纵情不羁（feel abandoned 感到被抛弃, abandoned building 荒废建筑, abandoned children 被遗弃儿童）。只作形容词。与 abandonment、abandon 并读。05-行为 、情感本篇并收情感义。表述宜用中性、共情语气。
[例] ① The child felt abandoned when neither parent came to the game. 父母都没来看比赛，孩子感到被抛弃。 ② Explorers entered the abandoned mine with caution. 探险者谨慎进入荒废矿井。
[源] 古法语 abandon（放任, 放弃）→ 动词 abandon + -ed → abandoned 被弃置或无人照管。
[族] abandon 放弃, abandonment 遗弃, abandoner 抛弃者（罕）
[搭] feel abandoned 感到被抛弃, abandoned building 荒废建筑, abandoned children 被遗弃儿童, abandoned project 夭折项目, abandoned property 遗弃财产, abandoned laughter 放声大笑（书面）
[联] abandoned, abandonment：形容词状态, 名词行为/状态 abandoned, neglectful：abandoned 强调已被弃；neglectful 强调疏于照料, abandon, abandonment：动词抛弃, 名词遗弃 abandon, abandonable：放弃行为, 可放弃性（品格）
[记] abandoned 只作形容词。与 abandon、abandonment 本组三连。03-性格 abandonable为「可放弃」义，勿混

**abandon**
英/美 /əˈbændən/
v. 抛弃, 放弃, 遗弃
n. 放任, 纵情（书面）
[义] To leave someone or something permanently, especially when you should stay; to give up completely. 永久离开本应留下的人或物；完全放弃（abandon a plan 放弃计划, abandon ship 弃船, abandon a child 遗弃儿童）。动词及物；名词 abandon 书面指纵情、放任（with abandon）。与 abandoned、abandonment 本组三连。05-行为 、亦有行为义；本篇偏情感—关系伤害。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① He would never abandon his friends in trouble. 他绝不会在朋友有难时抛弃他们。 ② They had to abandon the car in the flood. 他们不得不在洪水中弃车。
[搭] abandon a plan 放弃计划, abandon ship 弃船, abandon hope 放弃希望, abandon a child 遗弃儿童, abandon the project 放弃项目, with wild abandon 纵情肆意（书面）
[记] abandon 不可用于「放弃做某事」的 abandon to do；用 abandon + 名词/动名词。与 abandoned、abandonment 并读

**abandonment**
英/美 /əˈbændənmənt/
n. 抛弃, 遗弃, 放弃
[义] The act of leaving someone or something permanently; the state of being left; giving up a right or claim. 永久离开某人或某物的行为或状态；放弃权利或主张（child abandonment 遗弃儿童, abandonment of a project 项目夭折, sense of abandonment 被抛弃感）。不可数为主，可数指具体实例。与 abandoned、abandon 并读。05-行为  亦有遗弃义。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① Abandonment in childhood can affect adult relationships. 童年被遗弃可影响成年后的关系。 ② The abandonment of the treaty shocked allies. 条约的废止令盟友震惊。
[搭] child abandonment 遗弃儿童, abandonment of a project 项目放弃, sense of abandonment 被抛弃感, abandonment issues 被弃创伤（心理用语）, emotional abandonment 情感遗弃, abandonment of property 遗弃财产
[联] abandonment, abandoned：名词遗弃, 形容词被弃 abandonment, neglect：abandonment 强调彻底离开；neglect 强调疏于照料, estranged, widowed：estranged 偏关系破裂；widowed 偏丧偶 estranged, orphaned：estranged 多指主动疏远；orphaned 指失去父母
[记] abandonment issues 为心理咨询常见搭配。与 abuse 并读关系创伤链。行为篇可延伸阅读

**estranged**
英/美 /ɪˈstreɪndʒd/
adj. 疏远的, 失和的
[义] No longer close or affectionate with someone; having become separated in relationship though not necessarily in law. 关系不再亲密；失和、疏远（estranged parents 关系疏远的父母, estranged couple 失和夫妻, estranged from family 与家庭疏远）。只作形容词。常与 from 连用：estranged from sb. 与某人疏远。表述宜用中性、尊重语气。
[例] ① He had been estranged from his brother for years. 他与兄弟疏远多年。 ② The estranged couple announced they would not divorce. 关系失和的夫妻宣布不离婚。
[搭] estranged parents 疏远的父母, estranged couple 失和夫妻, estranged from family 与家庭疏远, estranged wife/husband 分居的妻子/丈夫（常与法律分居区分）, become estranged 关系疏远, long estranged 长期失和
[记] estrange 动词。estrangement 名词。法律上 separated 与 estranged 用法依法域而异。与 家庭篇 widow 链并读人生关系变故

**nurturing**
英/美 /ˈnɜːtʃərɪŋ/
adj. 养育的, 培育的, 关怀的
[义] Giving care and encouragement that helps someone or something develop. 给予有助成长发展的关怀与鼓励（nurturing parent 有爱心的家长, nurturing environment 培育性环境, nurturing relationship 支持性关系）。只作形容词。与 neglectful、abusive对照：nurturing 偏积极照料；neglectful 偏疏于照顾；abusive 偏伤害。表述宜用温暖、中性语气。
[例] ① A nurturing teacher helped shy students find their voice. 一位善于培育的老师帮腼腆学生开口表达。 ② Children thrive in a nurturing home. 孩子在充满关怀的家庭中茁壮成长。
[源] nurture（养育）+ -ing → nurturing 指提供成长所需关怀与支持的态度或环境。
[族] nurture 养育, nurturer 养育者, nurturing environment 培育性环境
[搭] nurturing parent 有爱心的家长, nurturing environment 培育性环境, nurturing relationship 支持性关系, nurturing teacher 善于培育的教师, nurturing care 滋养性照护
[联] nurturing, neglectful：积极养育, 疏于照料 nurturing, wholesome：nurturing 偏行为态度；wholesome 偏健康有益, neglectful, abusive：疏忽, 虐待伤害 neglectful, nurturing：疏于照料, 积极培育
[记] nurture 动词/名词。与 foster 并读照护路径：foster 偏寄养制度；nurturing 偏日常情感支持

**neglectful**
英/美 /nɪˈɡlektfəl/
adj. 疏于照料的, 疏忽的
[义] Failing to give proper care or attention to someone or something you are responsible for. 对应负责的人或事未能给予适当照料或关注（neglectful parent 疏于照料的家长, neglectful of duties 玩忽职守, neglectful care 疏忽性照护）。贬义。与 nurturing 对照；与 abusive 区分：neglectful 偏 omission（未做）；abusive 偏 commission（伤害）。表述宜用严肃、中性语气。
[例] ① Social workers intervened when the home was neglectful. 家庭疏于照料时社工介入。 ② He was neglectful of his own health during the crisis. 危机中他忽视了自己的健康。
[搭] neglectful parent 疏于照料的家长, neglectful of duties 玩忽职守, neglectful care 疏忽性照护, neglectful supervision 监管疏忽, emotionally neglectful 情感上疏于关怀
[记] neglect 名词/动词。与 nurturing、abusive、wholesome 并读养育—伤害光谱

**betrayal**
英/美 /bɪˈtreɪəl/
n. 背叛，辜负，出卖
[义] The act of betraying someone or something; the breaking of trust that causes deep hurt and anger. 核心义：辜负了本可信赖的关系或承诺——a sense of betrayal 被背叛感, betrayal of trust 背信。比 treachery 更日常；比 disappointment 更重、更绑信任被践踏。不可数为主；a betrayal 可数指「一次背叛行为」。
[例] ① Learning that her friend had spread the rumor felt like a personal betrayal. 得知朋友散布谣言，她觉得这是个人背叛。 ② The betrayal of public trust led to mass protests. 对公众信任的辜负引发大规模抗议。
[源] betray（背叛、辜负）+ -al（名词后缀）→ 指违背信任、出卖或辜负的行为与随之的伤害感。
[族] betray 背叛, betrayer 背叛者, traitor 叛徒
[搭] a sense of betrayal 被背叛感, betrayal of trust 背信, feel betrayal 感到被辜负, personal/political betrayal 个人/政治背叛, betray sb. 背叛某人（动词）
[联] treachery：叛变，更绑阴险, disloyalty：不忠，更抽象, double-cross：出卖（口语）, loyalty 忠诚, devotion 奉献, faithfulness 忠实, betrayal, disappointment：disappointment 可因结果不如预期；betrayal 必涉信任被违背betrayal, betray：betrayal 为名词（the betrayal hurt）；betray 为动词（he betrayed me）
[记] betrayal of + 信任/国家/原则。devotion 反义词列 betrayal。本篇 betrayal 开启「信任—归属」链，与 trust / distrust / suspicion 合读

**approval**
英/美 /əˈpruːvl/
n. 认可，赞成，批准
[义] The feeling or expression that something is good, right, or acceptable; official permission or agreement. 对某人、某事表示赞同、认可或批准——seek approval 寻求认可, nod of approval 点头赞许, seal of approval 官方认可。比 acceptance更偏「赞成、说好」；比 praise 更轻、可仅表「不反对」。不可数为主；an approval 可数指「一次批准」。
[例] ① She waited nervously for her boss's approval of the proposal. 她紧张地等待老板对提案的批准。 ② His parents gave their approval to the marriage. 父母同意了这门婚事。
[搭] seek/win approval 寻求/赢得认可, approval of 对……的赞成, approval rating 支持率, sign of approval 赞许的迹象, parental approval 父母认可, approve 赞成（动词）
[联] acceptance：接纳，更绑「纳入圈内」, endorsement：背书，更正式, sanction：批准，更官方, disapproval 不赞成, rejection 拒绝, criticism 批评, approve, appraise：赞成/批准, 估价/评估 approve, apprise（日常）：approve 赞成；apprise 通知（音近义异）
[记] approval of/from + 人/机构。动词 approve（approve of 赞成）。acceptance 条已列 approval 为近义；本篇 approval → validation → encouragement 为「认可—肯定」链

**approve**
英/美 /əˈpruːv/
v. 赞成, 同意, 批准
[义] To agree that something is good, right, or acceptable (approve of); to give official permission or consent (approve a plan, approve the budget). 认为某事好、对或可接受（approve of）；正式批准或同意（approve a plan 批准计划）。及物/不及物。与 approval 并读：动词 vs 名词；与 05-approve 并读：本篇偏情感/态度上的「赞成、认可」；行为章亦谈机构批准。与 appraise 并读：approve 偏态度赞同；appraise 偏估价/评估。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① My parents didn't approve of me staying out late. 我父母不赞成我晚归。 ② The board approved the budget unanimously. 董事会一致批准了预算。
[源] 古法语 aprover（认为好）← 拉丁语 probare（检验、认可）→ 对行为或方案表示赞同，或正式予以批准。
[族] approval 认可, approver 批准者, disapprove 不赞成, acceptable 可接受的
[搭] approve of 赞成, approve a plan/budget 批准计划/预算, win approval 赢得认可, disapprove 不赞成
[记] approve of + 名词/动名词（赞成）；approve + 宾语（批准文件）。与 approvedly 并读

**validation**
英/美 /ˌvælɪˈdeɪʃn/
n. 认可，肯定，确认
[义] The act of recognizing or confirming that someone's feelings, experiences, or worth are real and legitimate; the feeling of being seen and affirmed. 确认某人的感受、经历或价值是真实、正当的，并由此获得被看见、被肯定之感——emotional validation 情感确认, seek validation 寻求外界肯定。比 approval 更绑「你的感受/存在是对的」；比 praise 更重心理层面。不可数为主。
[例] ① Sometimes a simple "I hear you" offers more validation than advice. 有时一句「我听到了」比建议更能带来确认感。 ② She realized she had been looking for validation on social media instead of from herself. 她意识到自己一直在社交媒体上寻求认可，而非来自内心。
[搭] emotional validation 情感确认, seek validation 寻求肯定, validation from others 来自他人的认可, need for validation 对肯定的需求, validate 确认/使生效（动词）
[联] affirmation：肯定，更绑积极陈述, recognition：承认，更绑被看见, confirmation：确认，更中性, invalidation 否定, dismissal 漠视, rejection 拒绝
[记] validate 动词亦指「使数据/文件有效」（validate a ticket 验票），依语境区分。心理话语中 validation 为高频正面词；过度 seek validation 可含依赖外界评价之意。与 rejection 成对照

**encouragement**
英/美 /ɪnˈkʌrɪdʒmənt/
n. 鼓励，鼓舞
[义] Words or actions that give someone confidence, hope, or motivation to continue or try. 让人更有信心、更愿意继或尝试的言语与行动——words of encouragement 鼓励的话, a source of encouragement 鼓舞之源。比 praise 更绑「推一把、给动力」；比 validation 更偏行动导向。不可数为主；an encouragement 可数指「一句鼓励」。
[例] ① Her teacher's encouragement helped her apply to medical school. 老师的鼓励帮她下定决心申请医学院。 ② A small nod of encouragement was all he needed to keep going. 一点鼓舞就足以让他继走下去。
[搭] words of encouragement 鼓励的话, give/offer encouragement 给予鼓励, encouragement to do 鼓励做…… a letter of encouragement 鼓励信, encourage 鼓励（动词）
[联] support：支持，更泛, motivation：动力，更绑内在驱动, inspiration：激励，更绑启发, discouragement 使气馁, criticism 批评, dismissal 漠视
[记] encourage sb. to do；encouraging 形容词（encouraging news 令人振奋的消息）。性格 条已述 encouraging 褒义用法。本篇沿 approval → validation → encouragement → praise 铺就正向评价链

**praise**
英/美 /preɪz/
n. 赞扬，表扬
v. 赞扬，表扬
[义] Words that show approval and admiration for someone or something they have done; to express such approval. 对某人或其所为公开表示赞赏与表扬——high praise 高度赞扬, praise for 对……的表扬, sing sb.'s praises 大力称赞。比 encouragement 更绑「做得好」的评价；比 flattery 更真诚、更绑实绩。不可数为主。
[例] ① The rescue team received widespread praise for their bravery. 救援队因勇敢获得广泛赞扬。 ② Critics praised the film for its honest portrayal of grief. 评论家称赞影片对悲痛的真实刻画。
[搭] high praise 高度赞扬, praise for 对……的表扬, earn/win praise 赢得赞誉, sing sb.'s praise 大力称赞, beyond praise 赞不绝口, praise sb. for 因……表扬某人
[联] compliment：恭维，更口语、可更轻, acclaim：赞誉，更正式、更绑公众喝彩, commendation：嘉奖，更正式、可含官方表彰, criticism 批评, blame 责备, condemnation 谴责
[记] praise sb. for sth.；praiseworthy 形容词（值得赞扬的）。praise 可接 God（宗教语境）。与 flattery 对照：praise 通常有根据，flattery 常空洞讨好

**admiration**
英/美 /ˌædməˈreɪʃn/
n. 钦佩，赞赏，仰慕
[义] A feeling of respect and warm approval combined with wonder or pleasure; the act of looking at something with pleasure. 对他人品质、成就或事物之美产生的敬重、赞赏与赞叹——deep admiration 深深钦佩, admiration for 对……的仰慕, mutual admiration 相互欣赏。比 respect 更绑欣赏、仰慕、带一点「哇」；比 praise 更绑内心感受。不可数为主。
[例] ① She spoke of her mentor with obvious admiration. 她谈到导师时，钦佩之情溢于言表。 ② The athlete's comeback earned admiration from fans around the world. 运动员的复出赢得全球球迷的赞赏。
[搭] deep admiration 深深钦佩, admiration for 对……的仰慕, mutual admiration 相互欣赏, win admiration 赢得赞赏, admire 钦佩（动词
[联] respect：尊重，更绑基本敬意, esteem：尊重，更正式, awe：敬畏，更绑震撼, contempt 蔑视, disdain 鄙视, scorn 轻蔑, admire, adore：钦佩欣赏, 热爱崇拜 admire, admirer：动词, 仰慕者
[记] admiration for + 人/品质。动词 admire admiration vs. respect：admiration 含欣赏与赞叹；respect 可仅表基本礼貌与平等。本篇 admiration → respect → dignity 为「敬—尊—严」链

**admire**
英/美 /ədˈmaɪə(r)/
v. 钦佩, 赞赏, 欣赏
[义] To respect and approve of someone or something; to look at with pleasure. 尊重并赞赏某人或某事；欣赏、观赏（admire someone's courage 钦佩某人的勇气, admire the view 欣赏风景, widely admired 广受钦佩）。及物动词。与 adore并提：admire 偏钦佩、欣赏；adore 偏热爱、崇拜、程度更深。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① I admire how she handled the crisis calmly. 我钦佩她冷静应对危机。 ② Visitors admired the paintings in the gallery. 参观者欣赏画廊中的画作。
[搭] admire someone's courage 钦佩勇气, admire the view 欣赏风景, widely admired 广受钦佩, admire from afar 远远欣赏, admire honesty 赞赏诚实
[记] admiration 名词。与 adore、admirer 并读仰慕链。admired 为形容词态

**admired**
英/美 /ədˈmaɪəd/
adj. 受人钦佩的, 被赞赏的
[义] Regarded with respect, approval, or warm approval because of qualities or achievements. 因品质或成就而受尊重、赞赏（widely admired leader 广受钦佩的领袖, admired for courage 因勇气受钦佩, admired work 令人赞赏的作品）。只作形容词（过去分词作定语）。动词 admire。与 despised对照。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① She is admired for her honesty under pressure. 她在压力下仍诚实，令人钦佩。 ② The admired coach retired after twenty seasons. 这位备受敬重的教练在二十个赛季后退休。
[搭] widely admired leader 广受钦佩的领袖, admired for courage 因勇气受钦佩, admired work 令人赞赏的作品, highly admired 备受赞赏, admired teacher 受敬重的老师, long admired tradition 长期受推崇的传统
[联] admired, despised：受钦佩, 被鄙视 admired, favored：admired 偏尊敬；favored 偏受宠、得偏爱, admirer, adorer：仰慕/追求者, 崇拜者 admirer, admire：人, 动词
[记] admiration 名词。admire 动词。与 trustworthy 并读：受钦佩者未必同样可托付（人格维度不同）

**admirer**
英/美 /ədˈmaɪərə(r)/
n. 仰慕者, 赞赏者, 追求者
[义] A person who admires someone or something; sometimes, a secret romantic admirer. 钦佩或赞赏某人/某物的人；亦指秘密爱慕者（secret admirer 秘密仰慕者, admirer of art 艺术爱好者, admirers gathered 仰慕者聚集）。可数。由 admire + -er 构成。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① She discovered a note from a secret admirer. 她发现一张秘密仰慕者的纸条。 ② The author signed books for dozens of admirers. 作者为数十位仰慕者签书。
[搭] secret admirer 秘密仰慕者, admirer of art 艺术爱好者, admirers gathered 仰慕者聚集, admirer sent flowers 仰慕者送花, lifelong admirer 终身仰慕者
[记] secret admirer 为固定表达。与 adorer 并读 admire—adore 名词链

**respect**
英/美 /rɪˈspekt/
n. 尊重，敬意
[义] A feeling of deep admiration for someone because of their qualities, achievements, or experience; the sense of being valued and treated with dignity by others. 因对方品质、成就或经历而生的敬重；以及「被重视、被正当对待」的感受——earn/gain respect 赢得尊重, command respect 令人肃然起敬, self-respect 自尊。本篇侧重情感上的敬意与被尊重感；行为义 respect sb. / respect the rules 。不可数为主。
[例] ① Years of honest work earned her the respect of her colleagues. 多年诚实工作为她赢得同事的尊重。 ② He lost respect for the company after the scandal. 丑闻之后，他对公司失去了敬意。
[搭] earn/gain respect 赢得尊重, respect for 对……的尊重, command respect 令人肃然起敬, self-respect 自尊, with respect 恭敬地, show respect 表示尊重
[联] admiration：钦佩，更绑欣赏, esteem：尊重，更正式, regard：尊重，更书面, contempt 蔑视, disrespect 不敬, scorn 轻蔑
[记] respect for + 人/原则。self-respect 与 dignity 常并用。行为 respect 条已述 respect vs. admire；本篇从情感角度收 respect 为「被看见、被正当对待」之需，与 dignity 合读

**dignity**
英/美 /ˈdɪɡnəti/
n. 尊严，体面
[义] The quality of being worthy of honor and respect; calm and serious behavior that shows self-respect and commands respect from others. 值得被尊重的品质；保持自尊、庄重、不失体面——human dignity 人的尊严, maintain one's dignity 保持尊严, die with dignity 有尊严地离去。比 respect 更绑内在价值与不可侵犯性；比 pride 更绑人格底线而非自负。不可数为主。
[例] ① The refugees pleaded for food and shelter without losing their dignity. 难民在请求食宿时并未丧失尊严。 ② She handled the unfair accusation with quiet dignity. 她以沉静的体面应对不公指控。
[搭] human dignity 人的尊严, maintain/preserve dignity 保持尊严, dignity in defeat 败而不辱, affront to dignity 对尊严的冒犯, dignified 有尊严的（形容词）
[联] self-respect：自尊，更绑自我感受, honor：荣誉，更绑名誉, decorum：体面，更绑举止得体, humiliation 羞辱, degradation 贬低, indignity 侮辱
[记] 固定搭配 affront to sb.'s dignity。动词 dignify（dignify an absurd remark 使荒谬言论显得郑重，常 ironic）。本篇 dignity 与 offense / insult 成「尊严—冒犯」对照；humiliation 为 dignity 的反义链

**offense**
英/美 /əˈfens/
n. 冒犯，得罪；违法行为
[义] ① The act of upsetting or insulting someone; the feeling of being upset or insulted. 言行使他人不快、感到被冒犯；或被冒犯的感受——take offense 觉得被冒犯, give offense 冒犯他人, no offense 无意冒犯。② 违法行为（traffic offense 交通违章），依语境区分。本篇侧重① 情感上的被冒犯。可数/不可数均可。
[例] ① She took offense at his joke about her accent. 她对他拿口音开玩笑感到被冒犯。 ② He didn't mean to cause offense, but his comment sounded dismissive. 他无意冒犯，但那句话听起来很轻视。
[搭] take offense at 对……感到被冒犯, give/cause offense 冒犯他人, no offense 无意冒犯（口语）, deeply offended 深感被冒犯（形容词）, offensive 冒犯的（形容词）
[联] insult：侮辱，更具体、更重, slight：怠慢，更轻, affront：冒犯，更正式, compliment 恭维, praise 赞扬, respect 尊重
[记] take offense at/to + 言行。no offense 后常接 but...（无意冒犯，但是……）。英式拼写 offence。offensive 形容词（offensive remark 冒犯性言论）。本篇 offense → insult → mockery 为「被冒犯—被羞辱」链

**insult**
英/美 /ˈɪnsʌlt/
n. 侮辱，辱骂
英/美 /ɪnˈsʌlt/
v. 侮辱，辱骂
[义] A remark or action that is deliberately rude or disrespectful and hurts someone's feelings; to say or do something that treats someone with disrespect. 故意无礼、伤人尊严的言行——add insult to injury 雪上加霜, hurl insults 破口辱骂, take sth. as an insult 视为侮辱。比 offense 更直接、更重、更绑人身攻击；比 criticism 更绑恶意与羞辱。可数/不可数均可。
[例] ① Calling her lazy was an insult after she had worked overtime all week. 她加了一整周班还被说懒，简直是侮辱。 ② He felt insulted when they ignored his suggestion in the meeting. 会议上没人理会他的建议，他觉得受了侮辱。
[搭] hurl/shout insults 辱骂, take sth. as an insult 视为侮辱, add insult to injury 雪上加霜, insult sb.'s intelligence 侮辱某人智商, insulting 侮辱性的（形容词）
[联] affront：冒犯，更正式, slur：污蔑性言辞，更绑歧视性用语, disrespect：不敬，更泛, compliment 恭维, praise 赞扬, honor 致敬
[记] 名词重音在第一音节 /ˈɪnsʌlt/；动词在第二音节 /ɪnˈsʌlt/。insult to + 人/尊严。add insult to injury 为固定成语。与 mockery / ridicule 对照：insult 更直接骂人，mockery 更绑嘲弄取笑

**mockery**
英 /ˈmɒkəri/ 美 /ˈmɑːkəri/
n. 嘲弄，取笑，笑柄
[义] Teasing or laughing at someone or something in a cruel or unkind way; a person, thing, or situation that is so bad it seems ridiculous. 以刻薄或轻蔑的方式取笑、愚弄；或「沦为笑柄、形同嘲弄」的局面——make a mockery of 把……变成笑柄, subject sb. to mockery 让某人遭嘲弄。比 ridicule 更绑整体嘲弄氛围或局面；比 insult 更绑嘲笑而非直接辱骂。不可数为主；a mockery 可数指「笑柄、闹剧」。
[例] ① The biased trial made a mockery of the legal system. 不公正的审判使法律制度沦为笑柄。 ② School bullies turned his stutter into daily mockery. 校园霸凌者拿他的口吃日日嘲弄。
[搭] make a mockery of 把……变成笑柄, mockery of justice 对正义的嘲弄, subject to mockery 遭受嘲弄, mock 嘲笑（动词）, mocking 嘲弄的（形容词）
[联] ridicule：奚落，更绑公开嘲笑, derision：嘲弄，更书面, scorn：轻蔑，更绑不屑, respect 尊重, praise 赞扬, admiration 钦佩
[记] make a mockery of + 制度/原则/承诺 为高频搭配。mock 动词（mock sb.'s accent 模仿口音取笑）。本篇 mockery ↔ ridicule 为近义对，mockery 更偏「局面、氛围」，ridicule 更偏「具体嘲笑行为」

**ridicule**
英/美 /ˈrɪdɪkjuːl/
n. 奚落，嘲笑
v. 奚落，嘲笑
[义] The act of making fun of someone or something in a way that shows they are stupid or not worth serious attention; to laugh at someone in an unkind way. 以嘲笑方式使人显得愚蠢或不足挂齿；公开奚落——become an object of ridicule 成为笑柄, heap ridicule on 大肆嘲笑, ridicule sb. for 因……嘲笑某人。比 mockery 更具体、更绑「笑、奚落」这一行为；比 criticism 更绑羞辱而非建设性。不可数为主。
[例] ① The proposal was met with ridicule in the press. 提案在媒体上遭到奚落。 ② He was ridiculed online for his outdated views. 他因过时观点在网上被嘲笑。
[搭] heap ridicule on 大肆嘲笑, object of ridicule 笑柄, ridicule sb. for 因……奚落某人, face ridicule 面对嘲笑, ridiculous 荒谬的（形容词）
[联] mockery：嘲弄，更偏局面, derision：嘲弄，更书面, scorn：轻蔑，更绑不屑, praise 赞扬, respect 尊重, admiration 钦佩
[记] ridicule sb. for sth.；形容词 ridiculous（a ridiculous idea 荒唐主意）。ridicule 比 mockery 更常作动词。humiliation 与 ridicule 常同现；本篇沿 offense → insult → mockery → ridicule 完成「冒犯—羞辱」链

**criticism**
英/美 /ˈkrɪtɪsɪzəm/
n. 批评，批判；评论
[义] The act of expressing disapproval or pointing out faults; also, the analysis and judgment of works of art or literature. 指出不足、表达不满；亦指对作品的分析性评论——constructive criticism 建设性批评, face criticism 面对批评, literary criticism 文学评论。比 insult 更绑有理有据的负面评价；比 mockery 更正式。不可数为主；a criticism 可数指「一条批评」。
[例] ① She welcomed criticism because it helped her improve the design. 她欢迎批评，因为有助于改进设计。 ② The policy drew sharp criticism from human-rights groups. 该政策遭到人权组织的尖锐批评。
[搭] constructive criticism 建设性批评, face/invite criticism 面对/欢迎批评, criticism of 对……的批评, harsh criticism 严厉批评, criticize 批评（动词）
[联] disapproval：不赞成，更轻, censure：谴责，更正式, rebuke：斥责，更绑当面指责, praise 赞扬, approval 认可, compliment 恭维
[记] criticism of + 对象；criticize（美式）/ criticise（英式）。constructive vs. destructive criticism 为常见对照。本篇沿 praise / criticism 延伸至 flattery 与冒犯链

**flattery**
英/美 /ˈflætəri/
n. 奉承，谄媚，恭维话
[义] Praise that is excessive or insincere, given especially to gain favor from someone. 核心义：言过其实、往往不真诚的恭维，常为讨好或操纵——empty flattery 空洞奉承, immune to flattery 不吃奉承这一套。比 praise 更贬、更绑动机不纯；比 compliment 更强调过头、虚假。不可数为主。
[例] ① He saw through the flattery and refused to change his decision. 他看穿奉承，拒绝改变决定。 ② She used flattery to win the client's trust, which made her colleagues uncomfortable. 她用谄媚赢得客户信任，令同事不安。
[源] flat（古义「平、顺」）+ -ery（名词后缀）→ 原指「用话把对方「抹平」、顺耳」→ 今指空洞讨好的恭维。
[族] flatter 奉承, flattering 谄媚的/讨人喜欢的, flatters 奉承者
[搭] empty flattery 空洞奉承, flattery will get you nowhere 拍马屁没用, immune to flattery 不吃奉承, flatter sb. 奉承某人（动词）, flattering 讨人喜欢的/谄媚的（形容词）
[联] adulation：谄媚，更书面、更夸张, blandishment：甜言蜜语，更文学 sweet, talk：甜言蜜语，更口语, criticism 批评, honesty 诚实, insult 侮辱, flattery, praise：praise 通常真诚、有根据；flattery 常空洞、为讨好 flattery, compliment：compliment 可真诚可轻；flattery 偏「过头、不真诚」, favored, estranged：得宠, 疏远 favored, admired：偏爱, 钦佩
[记] flatter 动词（flatter oneself 自以为是）；flattering 可褒可贬（a flattering dress 显身材的衣服 vs. flattering remarks 谄媚之言）。distrust 例句已出现 flatters；本篇 flattery 与 praise 成对照

**favored**
英/美 /ˈfeɪvəd/
adj. 受宠的, 得偏爱的；受欢迎的
[义] Preferred or treated better than others; regarded with special kindness or advantage. 较他人更受偏爱或优待（favored child 受宠的孩子, favored candidate 热门候选人, teacher's favored student 老师偏爱的学生）。只作形容词。美式拼写 favored，英式 favoured 亦见。与 estranged并读：favored 偏被偏爱；estranged 偏关系疏远。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① Rumors spread that one sibling was the favored heir. 传言某兄弟姐妹是受宠的继承人。 ② The favored proposal won support from most board members. 更受青睐的方案获大多数董事支持。
[搭] favored child 受宠的孩子, favored candidate 热门候选人, favored treatment 优待, favored nation 最惠国（贸易）, highly favored 备受眷顾, no longer favored 不再得宠
[记] favor 名词/动词。favorite 更日常指「最喜欢的」。most-favored-nation 为贸易固定术语

**widowed**
英/美 /ˈwɪdəʊd/
adj. 丧偶的
[义] Having lost one's spouse through death and not remarried. 因配偶去世而丧偶且未再婚（widowed mother 丧偶母亲, recently widowed 新近丧偶, widowed at a young age 年轻丧偶）。只作形容词。名词 widow（女性）, widower（男性）。06-人生  有 widow 条。表述宜用尊重、中性语气。
[例] ① The widowed neighbor found comfort in a support group. 丧偶邻居在互助小组中找到慰藉。 ② She was widowed during the war and raised three children alone. 她在战争中丧偶，独自抚养三个孩子。
[搭] widowed mother 丧偶母亲, recently widowed 新近丧偶, widowed parent 丧偶父母, widowed at forty 四十岁时丧偶, support for the widowed 对丧偶者的支持, widowed and alone 丧偶独居
[联] widowed, bereaved：widowed 专指丧偶；bereaved 泛指丧亲 widowed, estranged：丧偶, 关系疏远, orphaned, bereaved：orphaned 强调失去父母；bereaved 泛指丧亲者 orphaned, widowed：失怙, 丧偶
[记] widow, widower 名词。be widowed 为常用系表结构。与 orphaned, bereaved 并读丧亲人生阶段链

**orphaned**
英/美 /ˈɔːfənd/
adj. 孤儿般的, 失去父母的
[义] Made an orphan by the death of one's parents; left without parental care. 因父母去世而成为孤儿；失去双亲照料（orphaned children 孤儿, orphaned by the epidemic 因疫情成为孤儿, feel orphaned 感到像被抛弃）。只作形容词。名词 orphan。表述宜用尊重语气，避免轻率比喻。
[例] ① The charity cares for orphaned children in rural areas. 慈善机构照料农村孤儿。 ② Many were orphaned when the earthquake destroyed their town. 地震摧毁城镇后许多人沦为孤儿。
[搭] orphaned children 孤儿, orphaned by war 因战争失去父母, orphaned calf 失去母兽的幼崽, feel orphaned 感到孤苦无依, orphaned at birth 出生即失怙, support orphaned families 援助失亲家庭
[记] orphan 名词/动词。orphanage 孤儿院。比喻义 feel orphaned 指「被抛弃感」，与字面义区分。与 death, family 并读

**gaslit**
英/美 /ˈɡæslɪt/
adj. 被煤气灯效应操控的（心理操纵）
[义] Made to doubt one's own memory, perception, or sanity through persistent manipulation by another person; describing the victim of gaslighting. 因他人持操纵而怀疑自身记忆、感知或理智；描述煤气灯效应受害者（feel gaslit 感到被精神操控, gaslit partner 遭伴侣精神控制的一方, gaslit into silence 被操控至不敢发声）。只作形容词（过去分词作表语/定语）。动词 gaslight；名词 gaslighting。表述宜用审慎语气，涉及指控时避免轻率贴标签。
[例] ① She finally realized she had been gaslit for years about events he denied. 她终于意识到自己多年来被他否认的事件精神操控。 ② Therapists help clients name patterns when they feel gaslit at work. 治疗师帮助来访者识别职场中感到被操控的模式。
[搭] feel gaslit 感到被精神操控, gaslit partner 遭操控的一方, gaslit into silence 被操控至沉默, gaslighting behavior 煤气灯式操控, accuse sb of gaslighting 指责某人进行精神操控, chronic gaslighting 长期精神操控
[联] gaslit, deceived（日常）：deceived 偏被骗；gaslit 偏系统性否定你的现实感gaslit, untrustworthy：untrustworthy 偏不可靠；gaslit 偏心理操纵机制, manipulated, gaslit：被摆布选择, 被否定现实感 manipulated, deceived（日常）：manipulated 偏控制；deceived 偏被骗
[记] 源出戏剧/电影 Gas Light。gaslight 作动词已入日常心理词汇。与 trust、sincere对照：信任被破坏常伴 gaslighting。法律/临床场景需专业评估，不宜滥用。 * trust / distrust、sincere gaslitgregarious 深解与  活力优越链并读可辨人际情感与社交行为体系。*。

**manipulated**
英/美 /məˈnɪpjuleɪtɪd/
adj. 被操控的, 被摆布的
[义] Controlled or influenced unfairly or dishonestly by someone else. 被他人以不公或欺骗方式控制或影响（feel manipulated 感到被操控, manipulated into agreeing 被摆布而同意, emotionally manipulated 遭情感操控）。只作形容词（过去分词作表语/定语）。与 gaslit并读：gaslit 偏否定你的现实感；manipulated 偏被摆布行为与选择。表述宜用审慎语气，涉及指控时避免轻率贴标签。
[例] ① She realized she had been manipulated into signing the contract. 她意识到自己曾被操控签下合同。 ② Teenagers can be manipulated by misleading social media posts. 青少年可能被误导性帖文摆布。
[源] 拉丁语 *manipulus*（一把, 一束）→ manipulate（操纵）+ -ed → manipulated 指被他人不当控制或影响。
[族] manipulate 操纵, manipulation 操控, manipulative 爱操控人的
[搭] feel manipulated 感到被操控, manipulated into agreeing 被摆布而同意, emotionally manipulated 遭情感操控, easily manipulated 易被摆布, manipulated data 被篡改的数据（另一义）, manipulated by others 被他人操纵
[记] manipulate 动词；manipulation 名词。与 gaslit、本组 abandoned 并读关系伤害链

**abusive**
英/美 /əˈbjuːsɪv/
adj. 虐待性的, 辱骂性的
[义] Involving cruelty, violence, or insulting language toward another person. 对他人施以残忍、暴力或侮辱性言语（abusive relationship 虐待性关系, abusive language 辱骂性语言, emotionally abusive 情感虐待的）。贬义。名词 abuse、施害者 abuser 本篇偏形容词受害—环境描述。表述宜用严肃、中性语气。
[例] ① She left an abusive marriage with help from a shelter. 她在庇护所帮助下离开虐待性婚姻。 ② Abusive comments were removed from the forum. 论坛删除了辱骂性评论。
[源] abuse（虐待, 辱骂）+ -ive → abusive 指具有虐待或辱骂性质的。
[族] abuse 虐待/辱骂, abuser 施虐者, abusively 虐待性地
[搭] abusive relationship 虐待性关系, abusive language 辱骂性语言, emotionally abusive 情感虐待的, physically abusive 身体虐待的, abusive partner 施虐伴侣, abusive behavior 虐待行为
[联] abusive, abuse：形容词, 名词/动词 abusive, neglectful：伤害/辱骂, 疏于照料
[记] 与 abuse / abuser 三连。report abusive behavior 为求助高频表达

**abuse**
英/美 /əˈbjuːs/（n.）/əˈbjuːz/（v.）
n. 虐待, 滥用, 辱骂
v. 虐待, 滥用, 辱骂
[义] ① To treat someone cruelly or violently, especially repeatedly. ② To use something wrongly or harmfully. ③ Insulting language. 以残忍或暴力方式对待他人，尤指反复发生；滥用（药物、权力等）；辱骂性言语。名词 /əˈbjuːs/，动词 /əˈbjuːz/。与 abuser并读。05-行为 亦有词条；本篇偏情感—受害义。表述宜用严肃、中性语气，涉及指控时依事实陈述。
[例] ① Victims of abuse need safe support, not blame. 虐待受害者需要安全支持，而非指责。 ② He was arrested for abusing his authority. 他因滥用职权被捕。
[源] 拉丁语 *ab-*（偏离）+ *uti*（使用）→ abuse 原义「误用、背离正当使用」，引申为虐待、辱骂。
[族] abusive 虐待性的, abuser 施虐者, abusable 可滥用的（罕）
[搭] child abuse 虐待儿童, domestic abuse 家暴, substance abuse 物质滥用, abuse of power 滥用权力, verbal abuse 言语虐待, report abuse 举报虐待
[联] abuse, abuser：虐待行为/状态, 施虐者 abuse, manipulated：abuse 偏伤害与暴力；manipulated 偏操控摆布, abuser, abuse：施虐者, 虐待行为 abuser, abandoner（词表罕词）：abuser 偏虐待；abandoner 偏抛弃
[记] abusive 形容词。report abuse 为求助高频表达。与 abuser、abandoned 并读关系伤害链

**abuser**
英/美 /əˈbjuːzə(r)/
n. 施虐者, 滥用者
[义] A person who treats someone cruelly or violently, especially habitually; someone who misuses power, drugs, or privileges. 习惯性残忍或暴力对待他人者；滥用权力、药物或特权者（child abuser 虐待儿童者, domestic abuser 家暴施害者, drug abuser 药物滥用者）。可数名词。由 abuse + -er 构成。表述宜用严肃、中性语气，避免未证实即贴标签。
[例] ① The shelter helps survivors distance themselves from an abuser. 庇护所帮助幸存者远离施虐者。 ② He was identified as a repeat abuser in court. 法庭上他被认定为累犯施虐者。
[搭] child abuser 虐待儿童者, domestic abuser 家暴施害者, drug abuser 药物滥用者, convicted abuser 已定罪的施虐者, report an abuser 举报施虐者, repeat abuser 累犯施虐者
[记] abuser 法律与心理语境高频。与 abuse、abandonment 并读受害—施害关系。求助语境常用 survivor 指幸存者

**abusively**
英/美 /əˈbjuːsɪvli/
adv. 辱骂地
[义] In an insulting or harmful manner. 辱骂地（treat staff abusively辱骂对待员工）。
[例] ① Witnesses said he shouted abusively. 证人称他辱骂叫嚣。 ② Platforms ban abusively tagged posts. 平台封禁辱骂标签帖文。
[搭] treat staff abusively 辱骂对待员工, abusive 辱骂的, verbal abuse 言语虐待

[联] insultingly 侮辱地
**abusiveness**
英 /əˈbjuːsɪvnəs/ 美 /əˈbjuːsɪvnəs/
n. 辱骂性，虐待性
[义] The quality of being abusive; tendency to insult or mistreat. 辱骂或虐待的性质（verbal abusiveness 言语辱骂性）。不可数。
[例] ① The report documented patterns of abusiveness in the workplace. 报告记录了职场中的辱骂模式。 ② Therapy addressed the abusiveness behind his outbursts. 治疗处理他爆发背后的虐待性。
[搭] verbal abusiveness 言语辱骂性, abusive 辱骂的, abuse 虐待/辱骂

**wholesome**
英/美 /ˈhəʊlsəm/
adj. 有益身心的, 健康的, 正派健康的
[义] Good for your physical or moral health; suggesting virtue and simplicity without being harmful or offensive. 对身心或道德健康有益；正派、朴实、无害（wholesome food 健康食物, wholesome entertainment 有益身心的娱乐, wholesome values 正派价值观）。褒义。与 abusive、neglectful 对照：wholesome 偏安全、有益的成长环境。表述宜用中性、正面语气。
[例] ① Parents wanted wholesome shows for young children. 家长希望儿童观看有益身心的节目。 ② The camp offered wholesome meals and outdoor activities. 营地提供健康餐食与户外活动。
[搭] wholesome food 健康食物, wholesome entertainment 有益身心的娱乐, wholesome values 正派价值观, wholesome family image 健康家庭形象, wholesome fun 健康的乐趣
[联] wholesome, nurturing：wholesome 偏「健康正派」；nurturing 偏「悉心培育」 wholesome, whole（日常）：wholesome 为有益身心；whole 为「整个」
[记] wholesomeness 名词。与 nurturing 并读积极养育—环境链

**grateful**
英/美 /ˈɡreɪtfəl/
adj. 感激的，感谢的
[义] 因他人帮助或好事而心里温暖、想道谢——强调对方付出带来的感受（grateful to you for your help）。比 thankful 稍正式，书面感谢信常用。
[例] ① I'm grateful to my teacher for believing in me when no one else did. 我感谢老师，在没人看好我时仍相信我。 ② We were grateful that everyone arrived home safely. 大家都平安到家，我们庆幸不已。
[搭] feel/be grateful 感到/很感激, grateful to sb. for sth. 因某事感谢某人, grateful that 庆幸…… deeply grateful 深为感激, grateful smile 感激的微笑
[联] thankful：感谢的，更口语, appreciative：感激的，偏欣赏, ungrateful 忘恩负义的, unthankful 不领情的
[记] grateful to sb. for sth. / grateful for sth. / grateful that + 从句。只作表语或定语（a grateful student）。名词 gratitude 不可数

**thankful**
英/美 /ˈθæŋkfəl/
adj. 感谢的，感激的
[义] 因某件好事或他人帮助而心里感激、想道谢——强调具体事由带来的庆幸（thankful for your support, thankful that you came）。宗教语境 I'm thankful to God 也常见。比 grateful 稍口语，感恩节常说 thankful。
[例] ① I'm thankful for my family's love and support during a difficult year. 艰难的一年里，我感激家人的爱和支持。 ② We were thankful that the storm missed our town. 风暴没袭击我们镇，我们庆幸不已。
[搭] feel/be thankful 感到/很感激, thankful for 因……而感谢, thankful that 庆幸…… thankful heart 感恩的心, be thankful to sb. 感谢某人
[联] grateful：感激的，略正式, appreciative：感激的，偏欣赏, unthankful 不领情的, ungrateful 忘恩负义的
[记] thankful for + 名词/动名词；thankful that + 从句；thankful to sb. for sth. 与 grateful 结构相同。Thanksgiving（感恩节）与 thankful 同源。只作表语或定语（a thankful smile）

**jealous**
英/美 /ˈdʒeləs/
adj. 嫉妒的；吃醋的
[义] Feeling or showing envy of someone or their achievements and advantages; fiercely protective of one's rights or possessions. 核心义：① 吃醋/嫉妒——怕失去所爱或他人得到自己想要的（jealous of his success, jealous girlfriend）；② 严防死守——jealous of one's reputation 极力维护自己的名誉。比 envious 更绑关系与占有。
[例] ① She felt jealous when she saw her friend post photos from the party she wasn't invited to. 看到朋友发派对照片而没邀请自己，她感到嫉妒。 ② He's jealous of anyone who gets too close to his girlfriend. 谁跟他女友走太近，他都会吃醋。 ③ The company is jealous of its trade secrets. 公司对商业机密严防死守。
[源] 源自法语 *jalous*，拉丁语 *zelosus*（热情、争风吃醋），与 zeal（热情）同族 → 原义「 zealously guarding」→ 今指因怕失去或他人拥有而生出的占有式不快。
[族] jealousy 嫉妒（名词）, zeal 热情, zealous 热心的
[搭] feel/get jealous 感到/变得嫉妒, jealous of 嫉妒……, jealous about 对……吃醋, jealous rage 妒火中烧, jealous type 爱吃醋的人, jealous husband 好嫉妒的丈夫
[联] envious：羡慕/嫉妒他人所有，未必涉感情占有, possessive：占有欲强，偏行为, covetous：觊觎，较正式古旧, trustful 信任的, generous 大方的, jealous, envious：envious of her house 羡慕/嫉妒她的房子（想要她有的）；jealous 常含「怕失去」或感情竞争（jealous of his coworker 吃他和同事的醋） jealous, zealous：zealous 热心、 zeal 热情；拼写与词源相关，义已分化
[记] jealous of + 人/物；jealous about + 具体情境（jealous about the time he spends with her）。名词 jealousy 不可数为主。口语 I'm jealous 可半开玩笑（「真好啊我也想要」）

**envious**
英/美 /ˈenviəs/
adj. 嫉妒的，羡慕的
[义] Feeling or showing a desire to have what someone else has because you think they are luckier than you. 核心义：因他人拥有而自己未有而生出的「我也想要」——可中性（envious of her talent 羡慕她的才华）或带贬义（envious gossip 嫉妒性闲话）。比 jealous 少绑「怕失去所爱/占有关系」，多绑财物、地位、能力。只作表语或定语（envious glance 嫉妒的一瞥）。
[例] ① She felt envious when her colleague got the promotion she had wanted. 同事得到了她想要的晋升，她感到嫉妒。 ② I'm not envious of their wealth—I just wish I had more time to travel. 我并不羡慕他们的财富——我只是希望有更多时间旅行。 ③ He gave her an envious look when she showed off her new car. 她炫耀新车时，他眼馋地看了她一眼。
[源] 源自拉丁语 *invidere*（in- 向 + videre 看）→「盯着看、眼红」→ 法语 *envie* → 英语 envy / envious。与 view、vision 同根，原义「用目光打量他人所有」。
[族] envy 嫉妒/羡慕（n./v.）, enviously adv., enviable 令人羡慕的
[搭] feel/be envious 感到嫉妒/羡慕, envious of 嫉妒/羡慕……, envious look/glance 羡慕/嫉妒的眼神, envious of sb.'s success 羡慕某人的成功, secretly envious 暗自羡慕
[联] jealous：常含吃醋、怕失去或严防死守, covetous：觊觎，较正式古旧 green with, envy：嫉妒眼红（习语）, content 满足的, generous 大方的, envious, jealous：envious of her house 想要她那样的房子；jealous 常涉感情竞争或占有（jealous of his coworker 吃他和同事的醋） envious, enviable：enviable 修饰「令人想要的」（an enviable job）；envious 描述人的感受, covetous, greedy：垂涎他物, 一般贪心 covetous, possessive：觊觎外物, 占有伴侣/关系
[记] envious of + 名词/代词。名词 envy 可数/不可数均可（a feeling of envy）。口语 I'm so envious 可半开玩笑表「真好啊我也想要」。green with envy 为固定习语

**covetous**
英/美 /ˈkʌvətəs/
adj. 垂涎的, 觊觎的
[义] Having or showing a strong desire to possess something that belongs to someone else. 强烈想要占有属于他人的东西（covetous glance 垂涎的一瞥, covetous of success 觊觎他人的成功, covetous neighbor 眼馋的邻居）。贬义。比 greedy 更正式、常带道德评判（十诫语境 covet）。表述宜用中性或书面语气。
[例] ① She shot a covetous look at her colleague's award. 她垂涎地看了同事奖杯一眼。 ② Religious texts warn against covetous thoughts. 宗教文本告诫勿起觊觎之念。
[搭] covetous glance 垂涎的一瞥, covetous of success 觊觎成功, covetous neighbor 眼馋的邻居, covetous desires 贪欲, covetous eye 贪羡的目光
[记] covet 动词；covetousness 名词。与 greedy、possessive 并读贪—占链

**covet**
英/美 /ˈkʌvɪt/
v. 觊觎，垂涎
[义] To yearn to possess something belonging to another. 觊觎，垂涎（covet wealth 觊觎财富）。
[例] ① Do not covet your neighbor's success. 不要觊觎邻人的成功。 ② Ads teach children to covet gadgets. 广告教孩子垂涎电子产品。
[搭] covet wealth 觊觎财富, covetous 贪婪的, envy 嫉妒
[联] desire 渴望, envy 嫉妒
[记] 宗教语境常译「贪恋」

**covetously**
英/美 /ˈkʌvɪtəsli/
adv. 贪婪地，觊觎地
[义] In a covetous manner; with greedy desire for what belongs to others. 贪婪地，觊觎地。
[例] ① He looked covetously at his rival's trophy. 他垂涎地望着对手的奖杯。 ② She eyed the jewelry covetously but said nothing. 她贪羡地打量珠宝，却一言不发。
[搭] look covetously 贪婪地看着, covetous 贪婪的

**covetousness**
英/美 /ˈkʌvɪtəsnəs/
n. 贪婪，觊觎
[义] Inordinate desire for wealth or possessions. 贪婪，觊觎（covetousness breeds conflict 贪婪滋生冲突）。不可数。
[例] ① The sermon warned against covetousness. 布道警示贪恋之害。 ② Covetousness poisoned the partnership. 贪婪毒害了合伙关系。
[搭] covetousness breeds conflict 贪婪滋生冲突, greed 贪婪, avarice 贪财

[联] greed 贪婪, avarice 贪财, generosity 慷慨, begrudge, envy（日常）：含吝惜/不甘, 羡慕/嫉妒 begrudge, grudge（日常）：动词吝惜/嫉妒, 名词怨恨
**begrudge**
英/美 /bɪˈɡrʌdʒ/
v. 嫉妒, 吝惜, 不情愿给予
[义] To envy someone the possession or enjoyment of something; to give or allow reluctantly. 嫉妒他人拥有或享受某物；不情愿地给予或允许，即嫉妒/吝惜/不情愿给予（begrudge someone success 嫉妒某人成功, begrudge paying the fee 吝于付费, not begrudge the time 不吝惜时间）。动词。be- + grudge（怨恨）。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① She did not begrudge her rival the award. 她并不 begrudge 对手得奖。 ② Many begrudge paying taxes while demanding services. 许多人 begrudge 纳税却要求服务。
[搭] begrudge someone success 嫉妒某人成功, begrudge paying the fee 吝于付费, not begrudge the time 不吝惜时间, envy 嫉妒
[记] 常接宾语 + 名词或动名词。与 begloom 并读

**proud**
英/美 /praʊd/
adj. 骄傲的，自豪的
[义] 因自己或所爱的人的成就而心里满足、想被看见——可褒可贬：褒义 proud of you（真为你骄傲），贬义 too proud to apologize（太傲不肯道歉）。常作表语 proud of / proud to do。
[例] ① Her parents are proud of her for getting into medical school. 父母为她考上医学院而自豪。 ② I'm proud to be part of this team. 我很荣幸是这个团队的一员。
[搭] proud of 为……感到骄傲, proud to do sth. 很荣幸做某事, proud parent 自豪的家长, proud moment 光荣时刻, too proud 太自负
[联] pleased：高兴、满意，未必含「骄傲」, honored：荣幸，偏正式, ashamed 羞愧的, humble 谦逊的
[记] proud of + 名词/代词/动名词；proud to + 动词原形。名词 pride；take pride in 与 be proud of 近义。pride 亦可贬义（false pride 虚荣）

**pride**
英/美 /praɪd/
n. 骄傲，自豪；傲慢
[义] ① 正当的自豪（take pride in your work 以工作为荣）；② 傲慢、自尊过强（hurt someone's pride 伤害自尊, pride comes before a fall 骄者必败）。与 形容词 proud 同族；本篇侧重名词及贬义「傲慢」。不可数为主；a pride of lions 狮群为另一义。
[例] ① She takes great pride in her children's achievements. 她为孩子的成就感到自豪。 ② His pride would not let him ask for help. 他的傲气让他不肯求助。
[搭] take pride in 以……为荣, pride and joy 掌上明珠, swallow one's pride 放下架子, hurt one's pride 伤害自尊, false pride 虚荣的骄傲, pride parade 骄傲游行（LGBTQ+）
[联] vanity：虚荣，更贬, conceit：自负，更绑自视过高, dignity：尊严，可近义不同侧重, humility 谦逊, shame 羞耻
[记] take pride in + 名词/动名词。Swallow your pride 劝人低头。proud 为形容词；亦谈 pride 贬义（false pride）。本篇与 ashamed / shame 形成对照

**ashamed**
英/美 /əˈʃeɪmd/
adj. 羞愧的，惭愧的
[义] Feeling guilt, embarrassment, or disgrace because of one's actions, characteristics, or associations. 核心义：因做错了事、有缺陷或牵连而自觉丢脸——比 embarrassed 更绑「自我道德判断」（ashamed of lying）。只作表语；不说 × an ashamed person，用 a shameful act / He looks ashamed。
[例] ① He was ashamed of how he had treated his sister when they were young. 他为年轻时对待妹妹的方式感到羞愧。 ② She felt ashamed that she had not stood up for her friend. 她因没有为朋友出头而自责。 ③ I'm ashamed to admit that I never learned to swim. 我不好意思承认我从没学过游泳。
[源] a-（处于……状态）+ shame（羞耻）→ 因自身行为或关联而「脸上无光」。古英语 *āscemed*，今仍强调道德/自我评价上的羞耻。
[族] shame 羞耻, shameful 可耻的, shameless 不知羞耻的
[搭] feel/be ashamed 感到羞愧, ashamed of 对……感到羞耻, ashamed that 因……而惭愧, ashamed to do sth. 不好意思/耻于做某事, deeply ashamed 深感羞愧
[联] embarrassed：尴尬，多因社交情境、脸热, guilty：内疚，偏「做了错事」, humiliated：受辱，强调被他人贬低, proud 自豪的, unashamed 不害臊的, ashamed, embarrassed：embarrassed 多社交窘态（裤子拉链开了）；ashamed 多道德/人格羞耻（撒谎、抛弃家人） ashamed, shameful：shameful 修饰行为（a shameful decision）；ashamed 描述人的感受
[记] ashamed of + 名词/oneself/动名词；ashamed that + 从句；ashamed to + 动词原形。不可作定语。短语 shame on you 表「你真丢人」（指责对方）

**shame**
英/美 /ʃeɪm/
n. 羞耻，耻辱；遗憾（what a shame）
v. 使羞愧，使丢脸
[义] ① A painful feeling of humiliation or distress caused by awareness of wrong or foolish behavior. 羞耻感——hang one's head in shame 羞愧低头, feel shame 感到羞耻；② 耻辱、丢脸（bring shame on the family 给家族蒙羞）；③ 感叹遗憾（What a shame! 真可惜！）。比 embarrassment 更重、更绑道德与人格；比 humiliation 可更内在。不可数为主。
[例] ① He could not bear the shame of having been caught cheating. 他无法忍受作弊被抓的耻辱。 ② What a shame you can't come to the party! 你不能来参加派对，真可惜！
[源] 源自古英语 *sceamu*，与德语 *Scham* 同源，指因自身或关联而自觉丢脸的状态。
[族] ashamed 羞愧的, shameful 可耻的, shameless 不知羞耻的
[搭] feel shame 感到羞耻, shame on you 你真丢人, put sb. to shame 使某人自愧不如, in shame 羞愧地, sense of shame 羞耻心, without shame 无耻地
[联] embarrassment：尴尬，更轻、更社交, humiliation：羞辱，更绑被他人贬低, disgrace：耻辱，更绑名誉扫地, pride 自豪, honor 荣誉, dignity 尊严, shame, ashamed：shame 为名词/动词；ashamed 为形容词表语 shame, embarrassed：embarrassed 多社交窘态；shame 多道德羞耻
[记] shame on you 指责对方。put sb. to shame 可褒义「胜过某人」。What a shame 表遗憾，非道德羞耻。ashamed / guilty 条已列 shame 为同源；本篇 shame 为名词/动词核心

**guilty**
英/美 /ˈɡɪlti/
adj. 内疚的；有罪的
[义] 两种核心义：① 情感上觉得「是我错了」（feel guilty about eating the cake）；② 法律上「罪名成立」（guilty of murder）。可表「显出愧疚的样子」（a guilty look 心虚的表情）。
[例] ① I felt guilty about not calling my grandmother more often. 我不常给奶奶打电话，心里过意不去。 ② The jury found him guilty of theft. 陪审团裁定他盗窃罪名成立。
[搭] feel guilty 感到内疚, guilty about/of 对……内疚/犯有……罪, guilty conscience 亏心事, guilty pleasure 私享的小, guilty 乐趣（明知不该仍喜欢）, plead guilty 认罪
[联] ashamed：羞愧，更偏「丢脸」, remorseful：懊悔，偏正式, innocent 无罪的, guiltless 无愧疚的
[记] guilty about + 名词/动名词（情感）；guilty of + 罪名（法律）。名词 guilt；guilty pleasure 为固定搭配，不可直译成「有罪的 pleasure」

**embarrassment**
英/美 /ˌæmbəˈræsmənt/
n. 尴尬，难堪，窘迫
[义] A feeling of self-consciousness, shame, or awkwardness; a person or situation causing this. 社交或处境上的难堪——脸热、想躲开、不知如何自处。比 shame 更轻、更少道德审判；比 humiliation 少「被当众践踏」的强度。可数/不可数均可（an embarrassment 令人尴尬的人/事）。
[例] ① To my embarrassment, I forgot her name as soon as we were introduced. 令我尴尬的是，一介绍完我就忘了她的名字。 ② His old photos are an embarrassment he hopes never surface online. 那些老照片是他希望永不上网的黑历史。
[搭] feel embarrassment 感到尴尬, to one's embarrassment 令某人尴尬的是, embarrassment of riches 选择过多（习语）, die of embarrassment 尴尬得要命（夸张）, financial embarrassment 财务窘迫（正式）
[联] awkwardness：别扭，更偏气氛, shame：羞耻，更重, discomfort：不适，更泛, confidence 自信, ease 自在
[记] embarrassed（形容词）比名词更口语。embarrassment of riches 指「好到不知如何选」。ashamed 条：embarrassed 多脸热窘态；ashamed 多道德羞耻

**humiliation**
英/美 /hjuːˌmɪliˈeɪʃn/
n. 羞辱，屈辱，丢脸
[义] The act of making someone feel ashamed or foolish, or the state of feeling this way. 核心义：被他人或情境当众贬低、尊严受损的屈辱感——public humiliation 公开羞辱, suffer humiliation 遭受屈辱。比 embarrassment 更重、更绑权力不对等或故意施辱；比 shame 更强调外加的践踏。可数/不可数均可。
[例] ① Losing the match in front of thousands was a bitter humiliation for the champion. 在数千观众面前输掉比赛，对冠军是痛苦的屈辱。 ② He still remembered the humiliation of being laughed at in school. 他仍记得在学校被嘲笑的羞辱。
[源] 源自 humiliate（使丢脸）+ -ation；拉丁语 *humilis*（低下的）→ 使人「矮化、贬到尘土里」。
[族] humiliate 羞辱, humiliating 羞辱人的, humble 谦逊的（同根 *hum-* 低，义已分化）
[搭] public humiliation 公开羞辱, suffer humiliation 遭受屈辱, humiliation of 对……的羞辱, inflict humiliation on 羞辱, humiliating defeat 耻辱性失败
[联] embarrassment：尴尬，更轻, degradation：贬低，更正式, mortification：难堪，可近义, dignity 尊严, honor 荣誉, triumph 胜利, humiliation, embarrassment：embarrassment 可因小事（叫错名）；humiliation 多尊严被故意伤害humiliation, humility：humility 谦逊为美德；humiliation 屈辱，同根义已分化
[记] 形容词 humiliating / humiliated。ashamed 条已列 humiliated 为近义；humiliation 为名词「屈辱」本身。与 chagrin / mortification 对照：chagrin 偏懊恼失面子；humiliation 偏被辱。

**mortification**
英/美 /ˌmɔːtɪfɪˈkeɪʃn/
n. 难堪，羞辱感；苦行（宗教）
[义] ① 强烈的羞耻与难堪——to one's mortification 令某人极度难堪；② 宗教苦修、禁欲（mortification of the flesh 克己肉身）。日常多①义，与 embarrassment、humiliation 近，但偏书面、脸上挂不住。不可数为主。
[例] ① To her mortification, she tripped on stage during the award ceremony. 令她极度难堪的是，她在颁奖典礼上绊倒了。 ② He recalled the mortification of being corrected in front of the whole class. 他想起在全班面前被纠正的羞辱感。
[搭] to one's mortification 令某人难堪的是, feel mortification 感到羞辱, mortification at 对……感到难堪, mortify 使难堪（动词）, mortifying 令人难堪的（形容词）
[联] embarrassment：尴尬，更日常, humiliation：屈辱，更绑被贬低, chagrin：懊恼，更绑计划落空, pride 自豪, satisfaction 满足
[记] to one's mortification 类似 to one's embarrassment，语气更重。宗教 mortification of the flesh 与日常「难堪」需依语境区分。chagrin 条已列 mortification 为近义

**remorse**
英/美 /rɪˈmɔːs/
n. 懊悔，自责，痛悔
[义] Deep regret or guilt for a wrong or injury one has done. 核心义：因伤害他人或严重违背良心而生的沉重自责——feel remorse, show no remorse 毫无悔意。比 regret 更道德化、更持久；比 penitence 可更少宗教仪式。不可数为主。
[例] ① He was filled with remorse after shouting at his mother. 对母亲大吼之后，他充满懊悔。 ② The killer showed no remorse during the trial. 审判期间，凶手毫无悔意。
[源] 源自拉丁语 *remordere*（再咬、折磨）→ 内心被反复「咬住」→ 今指对恶行的深切懊悔。
[族] remorseful 懊悔的, remorseless 无悔意的, morbid 病态的（同根 *mord-* 咬，义已分化）
[搭] feel remorse 感到懊悔, remorse for/over 对……痛悔, without remorse 毫无悔意, pangs of remorse 悔恨的折磨, remorseful 懊悔的（形容词）
[联] regret：后悔，更轻、更日常, contrition：痛悔，更宗教, guilt：内疚, indifference 冷漠, satisfaction 自得, remorse, regret：regret 可仅「遗憾没去看演出」；remorse 多绑对不起别人、良心不安remorse, guilt：guilt更泛；remorse 更书面、更重
[记] 法律与新闻 show no remorse 常见。形容词 remorseful。guilty / ashamed 条：guilty 偏「我有罪/我做错了」；remorse 偏「我深受良心折磨」

**penitence**
英/美 /ˈpenɪtəns/
n. 忏悔，悔过
[义] The action of feeling or showing sorrow and regret for having done wrong; repentance. 悔过、忏悔——尤带向神或权威认错、愿受罚的色彩。比 remorse 更绑仪式与悔改行动；比 contrition 可略偏「外在表现」。不可数为主。
[例] ① He expressed penitence for the harm he had caused years earlier. 他为多年前造成的伤害表达了悔过。 ② The sermon called the congregation to penitence and renewal. 布道呼吁会众忏悔并更新生命。
[搭] show penitence 表示悔过, acts of penitence 悔罪行为, in penitence 忏悔中, penitent 悔过的（形容词/名词）, do penance 做补赎（相关短语）
[联] repentance：悔改，更常用, contrition：痛悔，更强调心碎, remorse：懊悔，更偏内心煎熬, impenitence 不悔, defiance 顽抗
[记] 形容词 penitent（a penitent sinner 悔罪的罪人）。penance（补赎、苦修）为相关名词。口语日常悔过说 sorry / I regret it 即可

**contrition**
英/美 /kənˈtrɪʃn/
n. 痛悔，悔罪（尤宗教）
[义] The state of feeling remorseful and penitent; heartfelt sorrow for sin. 因罪过而心碎般的痛悔——perfect contrition 完美痛悔（宗教术语）。比 penitence 更强调内心被压碎、彻底认错；比 regret 重得多。不可数为主。
[例] ① She knelt in contrition, asking forgiveness from those she had wronged. 她痛悔跪下，请求被她伤害的人原谅。 ② His apology sounded sincere, full of contrition. 他的道歉听起来真诚，充满悔罪之意。
[搭] deep contrition 深切痛悔, act of contrition 悔罪经/悔罪行为, in contrition 痛悔中, contrite 痛悔的（形容词）, express contrition 表达悔罪
[联] penitence：忏悔，可更世俗, repentance：悔改，更常用, impenitence 不悔, arrogance 傲慢
[记] 形容词 contrite（a contrite expression 痛悔的表情）比名词更常见。与 contribute（贡献）不同形，勿混拼。ashamed 偏羞耻感；contrition 偏宗教/道德上的彻底悔罪。

**vanity**
英/美 /ˈvænəti/
n. 虚荣，自负；空虚（vanities）
[义] ① 过度在意容貌、名声或自我形象——out of vanity 出于虚荣, wounded vanity 受伤的虚荣心；② 空虚、徒劳（the vanity of human wishes 人生愿望的虚妄）。比 conceit 可更绑外表与名利；比 pride 贬义更明确。不可数为主。
[例] ① She refused to admit she had acted out of vanity rather than principle. 她不肯承认自己出于虚荣而非原则行事。 ② The novel explores the vanity of pursuing wealth at any cost. 小说探讨不顾一切追求财富的虚妄。
[搭] out of vanity 出于虚荣, vanity project 面子工程, wounded vanity 受挫的虚荣心, vanity table/mirror 梳妆台/镜, vain 自负的（形容词）
[联] conceit：自负，更绑智力/才华, pride：骄傲，可褒可贬, narcissism：自恋，更心理, humility 谦逊, modesty 谦虚
[记] 形容词 vain（in vain 徒劳，另一义）。vanity fair 可指「名利场」。proud 为自豪；本篇 pride 多指傲慢之 pride

**conceit**
英/美 /kənˈsiːt/
n. 自负，自大；巧妙比喻（文学）
[义] ① 对自己能力或价值的过高评价——full of conceit 极其自负；② 文学中的巧妙比喻或奇思（a poetic conceit）。日常学习以①为主。比 vanity 更绑才华/能力上的自傲；比 pride 更贬。不可数为主。
[例] ① His conceit made it hard for colleagues to work with him. 他的自负让同事难以与他合作。 ② The poem's central conceit compares love to a voyage. 诗的核心奇喻将爱比作航行。
[搭] full of conceit 极其自负, conceit about 对……自负, inflated conceit 膨胀的自大, conceited 自负的
[联] vanity：虚荣，更绑外表与名利, arrogance：傲慢，更绑态度, pride：骄傲，可中性, humility 谦逊, modesty 谦虚
[记] conceited（形容词）比 conceit 名词更口语。文学 conceit 与「自负」同形异义，依学科区分。conceited 见本篇末条

**conceited**
英/美 /kənˈsiːtɪd/
adj. 自负的，自高自大的
[义] Excessively proud of oneself; vain about one's abilities or appearance. 觉得自己特别了不起——爱照镜子、爱显摆、听不进批评。比 vain 更口语指人；比 proud 全贬。只作定语或表语（a conceited actor；Don't be so conceited）。
[例] ① He became conceited after winning one local competition. 赢了一场地方赛，他就飘飘然了。 ② She worried that praising his looks would make him conceited. 她担心夸他长相会让他 更自负。
[搭] conceited about 对……自负, sound conceited 听起来自大, conceited remark 自夸的话, conceit 自负（名词
[联] vain：虚荣的，更绑外表, arrogant：傲慢的，更绑待人, cocky：自大的，口语, modest 谦虚的, humble 谦逊的
[记] conceited about + 名词/动名词。名词 conceit。proud 可褒（proud of you）；conceited 全贬。本篇从 bereavement 经悔恨至 shame/humiliation，再至 vanity/conceit/pride 与 haughty/snobbish/conceited，与 guilty / ashamed 对照完成情绪链

**conceitedly**
英/美 /kənˈsiːtɪdli/
adv. 自负地
[义] In a conceited manner; with excessive pride in oneself. 自负地，自大地。
[例] ① He conceitedly claimed he could have done it better. 他自负地声称自己本可以做得更好。 ② She smiled conceitedly at the compliment. 她对恭维得意地一笑。
[搭] speak conceitedly 自负地说, act conceitedly 表现得自大, conceited 自负的

**self-esteem**
英/美 /ˌself ɪˈstiːm/
n. 自尊，自我价值感
[义] A feeling of confidence in your own worth, abilities, and right to be treated with respect; how much you value and approve of yourself. 对自己价值、能力与应被尊重之感的总体评价——high/low self-esteem 高/低自尊, boost self-esteem 提升自尊, damage sb.'s self-esteem 伤害某人的自尊。比 confidence更绑「我值不值得被尊重」；比 pride 更中性、可含脆弱面。不可数为主。
[例] ① Praise that focuses on effort rather than talent can help children develop healthy self-esteem. 强调努力而非天赋的表扬，有助于孩子建立健康的自尊。 ② Years of criticism left her with very low self-esteem. 多年批评使她自尊极低。
[搭] high/low self-esteem 高/低自尊, boost/build self-esteem 提升自尊, self-esteem issues 自尊问题, damage/hurt self-esteem 伤害自尊, sense of self-esteem 自尊感
[联] self-worth：自我价值，更绑内在价值感, self-respect：自尊，更绑「应被尊重」, confidence：信心，更绑能力把握, self-doubt 自我怀疑, insecurity 不安全感, shame 羞耻
[记] self-esteem 偏名词；形容词 self-esteem 少用，常说 low self-esteem person。与 assurance / self-assurance 常并用；与 confident 不同：confident 可指对具体事的把握，self-esteem 指对自我整体的评价。本篇 self-esteem → self-doubt → self-pity 为「自我评价」链

**self-pity**
英/美 /ˌself ˈpɪti/
n. 自怜，自哀
[义] An excessive or self-indulgent feeling of sadness for yourself because of your own problems or misfortunes, often without taking action to improve things. 因自身遭遇而过度沉浸于「我好可怜」，常含不行动、不负责的意味——wallow in self-pity 沉溺自怜, a bout of self-pity 一阵自怜, no self-pity 别自怨自艾。比 sadness 更绑「可怜自己」的姿态；比 grief 更轻、更绑抱怨式自哀。不可数为主。
[例] ① After the breakup, she spent a week in self-pity before calling a friend. 分手后，她自怜了一周才给朋友打电话。 ② He refused to wallow in self-pity and instead looked for a new job. 他拒绝沉溺自怜，转而找新工作。
[搭] wallow in self-pity 沉溺自怜, self-pity party 自怜聚会（口语，略贬）, no self-pity 别自怨自艾, a bout of self-pity 一阵自怜, feel self-pity 感到自怜
[联] self-indulgence：自我放纵，更绑纵容自己 victim, mentality：受害者心态，更绑「都是别人的错」, misery：痛苦，更泛、未必自指, resilience 韧性, self-esteem 自尊, acceptance 接纳
[记] self-pity 常含批评或自我提醒意味（Stop the self-pity!）。wallow in self-pity 为固定搭配。与 regret 不同：regret 懊悔选择；self-pity 哀叹处境。本篇 self-pity 收束「自我评价」负向链，转向 equanimity 等静定词

**self-doubt**
英/美 /ˌself ˈdaʊt/
n. 自我怀疑，对自身的不确信
[义] A lack of confidence in yourself, your abilities, or your decisions; the feeling that you may not be good enough or may be wrong. 对自身能力、判断或价值的怀疑，觉得「我不够好」或「我可能错了」——crippling self-doubt 严重的自我怀疑, plagued by self-doubt 被自我怀疑困扰, moments of self-doubt 一时的自我怀疑。比 doubt更绑指向自己；比 insecurity更绑能力/判断而非归属。不可数为主；self-doubts 复数表各种具体疑虑。
[例] ① Even successful writers sometimes struggle with self-doubt before publishing. 即便成功的作家，发表前有时也会与自我怀疑搏斗。 ② Self-doubt made her hesitate to apply for the promotion. 自我怀疑让她不敢申请晋升。
[搭] crippling self-doubt 严重的自我怀疑, plagued by self-doubt 被自我怀疑困扰, self-doubt about 对……的自我怀疑, overcome self-doubt 克服自我怀疑, moment of self-doubt 一时的自我怀疑
[联] insecurity：不安全感，更绑归属与关系, uncertainty：不确定，更泛 lack of, confidence：缺乏信心，更口语, self-esteem 自尊, assurance 把握, ashamedly, ashamed：副词, 形容词 ashamedly, embarrassedly（日常）：道德羞耻, 社交窘态
[记] self-doubt about + 名词/-ing。doubt 条已述 self-doubt 与 insecurity 并用；本篇 self-doubt 专收「指向自身」的疑虑，与 self-esteem 成对照。动词 doubt oneself 近义

**ashamedly**
英/美 /əˈʃeɪmɪdli/
adv. 羞愧地, 惭愧地, 不好意思地
[义] In a manner showing shame, guilt, or embarrassment about one's actions or situation. 以表现羞耻、内疚或难为情的方式；羞愧地、惭愧地（admit ashamedly 羞愧地承认, look ashamedly at the floor 惭愧地看着地面, speak ashamedly of the mistake 惭愧谈及错误）。副词。由 ashamed+ -ly 构成。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① He ashamedly confessed he had lied. 他羞愧地承认撒了谎。 ② She ashamedly returned the unused gift. 她不好意思地退还未用礼物。
[搭] admit ashamedly 羞愧地承认, look ashamedly at the floor 惭愧看地面, speak ashamedly of the mistake 惭愧谈及错误, ashamed 羞愧的
[记] 与 ashamed、shame 并读羞耻链。与 ashamedness 并读

**ashamedness**
英/美 /əˈʃeɪmɪdnəs/
n. 羞愧, 羞耻感, 惭愧
[义] The state or feeling of being ashamed; the quality of experiencing shame or moral self-reproach. 处于或感到羞愧的状态；体验羞耻或道德自责的性质（deep ashamedness 深切的羞愧, ashamedness over the lie 对谎言的羞耻感, hide one's ashamedness 掩饰惭愧）。不可数。与 ashamed 并读：形容词 vs 名词；与 shame 并读：shame 为羞耻本身/动词；ashamedness 为羞愧状态（较书面）。表述宜用中性、非污名化语气。
[例] ① Therapy helped her name the ashamedness she had carried for years. 治疗帮她命名多年背负的羞愧感。 ② He wrote of ashamedness without self-pity. 他书写惭愧而不自怜。
[源] ashamed+ -ness → 处于或体验羞愧、羞耻的状态。
[族] ashamed 羞愧的, shame 羞耻, guilt 内疚, embarrassment 尴尬
[搭] deep ashamedness 深切羞愧, ashamedness over the lie 对谎言的羞耻感, hide one's ashamedness 掩饰惭愧, shame 羞耻
[联] ashamedness, shame：羞愧状态, 羞耻/使羞 ashamedness, guilt：羞耻丢脸, 有罪感
[记] 产出亦可用 shame, feeling ashamed。本篇收束 approv- 至 ashamed- 段； 留待后

**arrogance**
英/美 /ˈærəɡəns/
n. 傲慢，自大
[义] The offensive display of superiority and self-importance; behavior that shows you think you are much better than others. 傲慢、目中无人——觉得自己高人一等、不屑他人（his arrogance offended everyone）。比 pride 更贬、更绑待人态度；比 haughtiness 更常用。不可数为主。
[例] ① His arrogance made it hard for the team to trust his leadership. 他的傲慢让团队难以信任他的领导。 ② She apologized for the arrogance of her earlier remarks. 她为早先言论的傲慢道了歉。
[搭] with arrogance 傲慢地, arrogance of power 权力的傲慢, display/show arrogance 表现出傲慢, arrogant 傲慢的（形容词）, arrogant attitude 傲慢态度
[联] haughtiness：傲慢，更书面, conceit：自负，更绑自视过高, hubris：狂妄，更文学/正式, humility 谦逊, modesty 谦虚, arrogant, confident：傲慢, 自信（不含贬义） arrogant, haughty：更常用, 更书面冷淡
[记] arrogant（形容词）比名词更口语。haughty / pride / conceit 条已列 arrogance 为近义；本篇从傲慢延伸至谦逊与勇气

**arrogant**
英/美 /ˈærəɡənt/
adj. 傲慢的, 自大的, 盛气凌人的
[义] Behaving as if you are more important or better than others; showing an offensive sense of superiority. 表现得仿佛比他人更重要或更优秀；表现出冒犯性的优越感，即傲慢的、自大的（arrogant tone 傲慢语气, arrogant attitude 傲慢态度, be arrogant about one's success 对自己的成功自命不凡）。只作形容词。与 03-arrogant、arrogance 并读：本篇为情感章 approv- 链后之高傲—羞耻对照；贬义。表述宜用中性批评语气。
[例] ① His arrogant tone made everyone uncomfortable. 他傲慢的语气让众人不适。 ② She found him arrogant because he never listened. 她觉得他很自大，因为从不倾听。
[源] 拉丁语 arrogāre（擅自声称, 僭取）→ 以居高临下、瞧不起人的方式自视过高。
[族] arrogance 傲慢, arrogantly 傲慢地, haughty 傲慢的, conceited 自大的
[搭] arrogant tone 傲慢语气, arrogant attitude 傲慢态度, be arrogant about 对……自命不凡, arrogance 傲慢
[记] 几乎只含贬义；表自信用 confident。与 arrogantly 并读

**arrogantly**
英/美 /ˈærəɡəntli/
adv. 傲慢地, 自大地, 盛气凌人地
[义] In an arrogant manner; with offensive superiority or disdain. 以傲慢的方式；带着冒犯性的优越感或轻蔑（speak arrogantly 傲慢地说, behave arrogantly 行为盛气凌人, smile arrogantly 自大地笑）。副词。由 arrogant + -ly 构成。表述宜用中性语气。
[例] ① He arrogantly dismissed their concerns. 他傲慢地漠视他们的顾虑。 ② The CEO arrogantly assumed the deal was won. 首席执行官自大地以为交易已到手。
[搭] speak arrogantly 傲慢地说, behave arrogantly 盛气凌人, smile arrogantly 自大地笑, disdainfully 轻蔑地
[联] arrogantly, arrogant：副词, 形容词 arrogantly, confidently：贬义傲慢, 褒义自信
[记] 批评语境常见。与 arrogantness 并读
